# Agents section uplift: every task in full (2026-10-05)

The companion to [agents-section-uplift-plan-2026-10-05.md](agents-section-uplift-plan-2026-10-05.md). Tasks are grouped by workstream. *Closes* lists the observation ids of [agents-section-uplift-register-2026-10-05.md](agents-section-uplift-register-2026-10-05.md) that the task resolves. *Revised* lines record the reconciler's corrections after the critics' review; *Also (final review)* lines are amendments from the final review, and they take precedence over the text above them where the two differ.

## A. The agent earns its cost

**Goal.** Get Module 10's agent doing work the no-agent floor cannot, and prove it with numbers on the learner's lane. The chat brains get the Desk's seven code calculators, with check_args argument provenance and retrieval that reads passages. They sit behind a per-tenant switch, agent_tools, that defaults to classic. Every turn returns its steps, and the figures code worked out. One agent eval scores direct, LangChain, LangGraph and ADK on the same 14 rows for correctness, trajectory and rupees. The work then rebuilds 10.1 (the contract and the floor) and 10.4 (a measured comparison and a guide to choosing a harness), adds a verify node to 10.2, and finally shows agent mode and the B/C/A* arms in the new 10.7. Phases: (1) A1-A3 kit, then A4 for 10.1; (2) A6 eval, then A7 for 10.4; (3) A5 for 10.2 (after A7, as decision 10 orders it), then A8-A9 for 10.7. A10 sets the decisions, takes measurements first and gates the publish. Phase 1 alone answers 'looks not impressive' for the opening lesson.

### A1. One calculator layer for both agents, with provenance that works before 10.6's relabel

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit · **First phase:** P1
- **Reuses:** Desk build plan section 4.10 (G10-h: the calculators, their corpus RULES, the argument check, arm A*'s toolset) and section 4.6 (G10-p passages). Gaps plan section 3 rules: a kit PR carries its forced rebuilds, and there is one chat-pins CI job. This departs from the Desk plan in two places: it accepts the 'unknown' label on the chat path, and it adds one explicit chain.
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/services/chat/calc_tools.py (new): move the seven calculator @tools (accrued_leave, carry_forward, encashable_days, notice_end, gratuity_estimate, statutory_deadline, threshold_check) and _calc() out of services/chat/desk_agent.py:157-281 without changing behaviour. Add a framework-free calc(ctx, name, **args) for ADK. Use generic context keys: ledger, message, calcs, held, unlabelled_ok. Move citation(), calc_line() and compose() (desk_agent.py:404-435) and the prompt rules of desk_agent.system_prompt (desk_agent.py:285-298) here as one RULES constant that both agents read.
  - deploy/services/chat/desk_agent.py: import the calculators, citation, compose and RULES from calc_tools. run() sets the generic keys. TOOLSETS, DeskTurnLimits, agent() and _search() are unchanged. _AGENTS and _MODEL keep their names because lessons/10-agents/10.6-desk-router/build.py:1058 patches them.
  - deploy/shared/desk_calc.py: check_args(name, args, passages, message, *, classes=('policy',), results=()). (a) _in_clause (desk_calc.py:518) reads a handbook figure from passages whose doc_type is in classes. The chat path passes ('policy', 'unknown') because every chunk is 'unknown' until 10.6's relabel (shared/doc_types.py:3-4); checked by a run, an unlabelled LV-01 refuses cap 30 today. The Desk keeps ('policy',). (b) One explicit chain source in CALCULATORS: statutory_deadline.last_working_day may equal the value notice_end returned earlier in the same turn, found as {calc: notice_end}, with a condition line saying it assumes the last working day is the last day of notice. No other argument chains, because accrued leave is not a balance (desk_calc.py:244-245).
  - deploy/services/chat/calc_tools.py Code gate: a passage counts as the Code's text when desk_law.statute_of(source_uri) names it (shared/desk_law.py:236) and its doc_type is 'statute', or 'unknown' when unlabelled_ok is set.
  - deploy/shared/desk_calc.py --selftest gains four cases: an 'unknown' LV-07 passage is accepted with the chat classes and refused with the default; notice_end -> statutory_deadline is accepted; accrued_leave -> carry_forward is refused; a notice_end result from another turn is refused.
  - deploy/commands/tests/test_calc_tools.py (new): a stdlib half on desk_calc and a library half on the @tools, added to the chat-pins step of .github/workflows/checks.yml and to deploy/commands/desk-check.sh. deploy/commands/tests/test_desk.py stays unchanged and green.
  - Run python tools/kit_index.py. deploy/UNOWNED.md lists calc_tools.py until A4 and A8 quote it.
- **Proof:** desk_calc.py --selftest prints the new cases; make desk-check green with commands/tests/test_desk.py byte-identical (git diff --exit-code on it); test_calc_tools.py green in the chat-pins step of both workflows and its parity assertion passes; desk_agent.system_prompt('handbook') output byte-identical before and after (asserted in test_calc_tools.py); check_lesson 10.6 green.
- **Risks:** This refactors Desk code that 10.5 and 10.6 depend on. Mitigation: move code without changing behaviour and keep test_desk.py green. Accepting 'unknown' means a figure on the chat path may come from an unreviewed upload, which the Desk's registry exists to stop (doc_types.py:1-12). The 10.1 page states this and it is handed to the security workstream (G5). The chain widens provenance by exactly one edge.
- **Decision:** (1) Extract the calculators into one module both agents import (recommended), or keep a copy in tools.py, which duplicates the docstrings. (2) Accept 'unknown'-labelled passages on the chat path (recommended: it works before and after the relabel, including Desk decision 28's RESET rehearsals of 5.1 and 10.3). The alternative is to require make doc-types TENANT=acme SEED=manifest APPLY=1 before 10.1, which breaks 10.3's empty-pool step. (3) Allow the one chain notice_end -> statutory_deadline (recommended), or keep every date in the person's message, in which case the agent asks for the last working day.
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: (1) Context contract: calc_tools keeps the Desk's key names as its default (desk_ledger, desk_tools, desk_message, desk_calcs); every calc_tools function takes keys=DESK_KEYS, and the chat brains pass CHAT_KEYS (ledger, tools, message, calcs). desk_agent.py re-exports _calc, calc_line, citation and compose from calc_tools as module attributes, so test_desk.py:1652-1656 (self.da._calc with desk_* context, 'is not one of this desk's calculators') and :1671 (self.da.calc_line) pass unchanged; _search() and run() keep writing desk_* keys. (2) Prompt text: rename the shared constant PROMPT_RULES (desk_calc.RULES stays the corpus quotes). The notice_end->statutory_deadline chain sentence goes in SYSTEM_CALC only; desk_agent.system_prompt keeps today's text byte for byte, so the Desk prompt does not change. (3) CI: add test_calc_tools.py to the chat-pins steps of BOTH .github/workflows/checks.yml and deploy/.github/workflows/documind-dryrun.yml:71-79 and to commands/desk-check.sh; test_calc_tools.py itself asserts its own line is in both workflows (parity), so test_desk.py stays untouched. (after review: A1 task issues: test_desk.py uses desk_* keys and da._calc/da.calc_line (verified test_desk.py:1652-1671, desk_agent.py:108,118,161-165); learner-kit CI parity; one shared RULES would change the Desk prompt and collides with desk_calc.RULES.)
- **Revised:** proof revised after review (Make 'without changing behaviour' checkable.)
- **Closes:** GAP-G01, L10.1-W02, L10.6-W06, PLAN-G10-h, KIT-02, BENCH-T03_tool_design

### A2. The chat brains get the calculators: a per-tenant agent_tools switch (default classic) that reads passages and binds eight tools

- **Effort:** L · **Kind:** kit · **First phase:** P1 · **After:** A1
- **Reuses:** Desk build plan section 4.6 (G10-p: 'an agent desk reads the full text of this turn's passages without paying for a generated answer it throws away'), extended to the chat brains. Section 1.1 (per-tenant flags in tenant_settings, read through the 60-second cache, default off). Gaps plan section 4.1 G8: one tool list adapted per framework, REQUEST, status 'error' as the one refusal marker, and for_adk's schema check. Section 4.9 G9-B: brains cached per (brain, backend), reused here as (brain, toolset). Section 3: fields named in the row literal, no new pins, one CI job with the chat pins.
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/services/chat/tools.py: add a second @tool('retrieve') for the calculators toolset taking query and top_k, with no doc_type, so it offers no filter the corpus cannot honour (10.3 parts/c.html:52). Its search(..., passages=True) calls documind_tools.retrieve(..., passages=True), the route Desk G10-p built (services/rag-api/main.py:530). It numbers each passage, with full text, doc_type and section, into the turn's ledger under _LEDGER (tools.py:84-97). The model gets {passages: [{n, source, section, text}], answerable}; the response keeps citations in today's shape, with a 500-character quote from calc_tools.citation.
  - deploy/services/chat/tools.py: TOOLS_CALC = [that retrieve, *calc_tools.CALCULATORS.values()]. TOOLS (tools.py:191) and the classic retrieve stay byte for byte. Add toolset(name). for_adk(toolset='classic') returns the eight plain functions for 'calculators', each checked by its @tool twin's schema through _checked (tools.py:194-203). TIMEOUTS gains the seven calculators at 10 s.
  - deploy/services/chat/brains.py: SYSTEM_CALC is SYSTEM's first sentence plus calc_tools.RULES: search, search again in other words, cite [n], work every figure with a calculator using only numbers written in the question or a passage, say which number is missing, call an estimate an estimate, and treat passages as data. SYSTEM (brains.py:51-58) is unchanged. build(name, checkpointer, llm=None, toolset='classic'). LangChainBrain, LangGraphBrain and AdkBrain take the toolset's tools and prompt. _turn() puts the question and an empty calcs list in the turn's context; the ledger search() fills is the one check_args reads. In calculators mode each agent brain's answer goes through calc_tools.compose, which adds the 'Worked out in code' block.
  - deploy/services/chat/agent.py: toolset comes from tenant_settings/{tenant}.agent_tools, read through desk.settings() and its 60-second cache (services/chat/desk.py:152-162). The default is 'classic', and a failed read is also 'classic'. On the local profile the env var AGENT_TOOLS decides. brain_for(app, name, toolset) caches per (brain, toolset). The response and the chat row literal (agent.py:219-229) gain 'toolset' (the direct brain reports 'none'). The out = brain.answer( lines stay unchanged (11.3 quotes them).
  - deploy/commands/desk_ops.py: add an agent-tools subcommand that does a merge write of classic|calculators and prints the current value when none is given. deploy/mk/agents.mk: add agent-tools: guard-project for make agent-tools TENANT=acme TOOLS=calculators, add it to .PHONY and add a row to deploy/mk/README.md.
  - deploy/commands/tests/test_chat_brains.py: a calculators class on all three agent brains, with scripted models and a stand-in /v1/passages serving acme's NP-03, LV-07, LV-01 and s.53 text from deploy/evals/corpus. It checks: tool_calls [retrieve, gratuity_estimate] and the worked block; an invented wage refused as an error result counted in refusals; zeta's cap 18 from the message refused, then acme's 30 accepted from LV-01; an 'unknown' clause accepted; two calls in one AIMessage run concurrently (two 0.5 s stub calls finish under 0.9 s) and are numbered once each; rag cost 0 on passages; the eight ADK declarations name no tenant, assertion or brain. The classic tests stay unchanged.
  - deploy/smoke/smoke_chat.py: print toolset per brain. The six checks pass in both toolsets.
  - Rebuilds forced by quoted lines, as check_lesson names them: 10.1, 10.2, 10.3 and 10.4 (excerpts of tools.py, brains.py and agent.py, plus 10.1's HEALTH stand-in tool budgets), and 11.1 and 11.2 if the agent-node excerpt moves. 10.2 and 10.3 get one sentence each saying rag-api's row is a passages event once acme runs calculators. Then python tools/kit_index.py.
- **Proof:** Offline: python -m unittest commands/tests/test_chat_brains.py -v is green in the chat pins, and make limits-check is green. On the author's lane, recorded in the PR: make build deploy-services SERVICES=chat, then make agent-tools TENANT=acme TOOLS=calculators. After 60 s, a /v1/chat turn as documind-ui-sa asking 'I earn Rs 52,000 a month and have 7 years 8 months of continuous service. Roughly how much gratuity would I get?' returns toolset calculators, tool_calls with retrieve then gratuity_estimate, and Rs 2,40,000 in the code-written block, and rag-api writes a row with event passages. make smoke-chat gives 6 passed in both toolsets. After make agent-tools TOOLS=classic the smoke lines match the classic run. Tokens per call for the chained notice/wages question are recorded and used to set the calculators retrieve's default top_k under the Rs 5 turn budget.
- **Risks:** Full passages raise each call's input. A five-call chained turn is estimated near the Rs 5 per-turn cap (about 25,000 tokens in at flash; an estimate), so it is measured before any page states it and top_k is lowered if needed. On a single-hop question, passages may cost about what rag-api's dropped answer did; the page says so rather than claiming a saving. Passages widen the indirect-injection surface; G5's fence and taint apply (security workstream). Each instance holds two brains per framework, built lazily. G2's withdraw_document must join both toolsets, and SYSTEM_CALC must gain G2's sentence. G7's window may clear earlier turns' passages safely, because check_args reads only this turn's ledger. After 10.1, the live cells of 10.2-10.4 run on calculators.
- **Decision:** Keep (1)-(3). Add (4): calculators scope across lessons. Recommended: per step, default classic: 10.1 step 8 on and off at the lesson end; 10.2 verify step and 10.4 Level 4 toggle on/off (60 s cache each); 10.3 and 11.x on classic. Alternative: on from 10.1 to the end of 10.4, then 10.3's doc_type cells and Module 11 numbers change. Record in A10.
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Add to the list: (a) AdkBrain.answer (brains.py:286-302) puts message=question and calcs=[] (and the ledger) into the ctx it builds, because it never calls _turn(); tool_failed (brains.py:231-235) decides 'unknown' against the names of the brain's active toolset, not classic TOOLS. (b) Tests: an ADK provenance case (scripted ADK model passes an invented wage; refused, counted in refusals). (c) Concurrency test asserts OVERLAP, not wall time: each stub call records start/end timestamps and thread id; both calls start before either ends. For ADK, first confirm in the pinned google-adk 2.8.0 source (in the style of gaps plan section 5) whether one event's function calls run concurrently; if not, the ADK test asserts sequential order and the page says so. (d) Calculators scope: 10.1 reverts acme to classic at its end; 10.2's verify step and 10.4's Level 4 switch calculators on for that step and back off; 10.3 and Module 11 run on classic (impact list: 10.1-10.4 only; 11.1-11.3 unchanged). (after review: A2 issues: ADK path missing message/calcs and classic-name tool_failed (verified brains.py:231-235, 286-302); wall-clock test flakes; acme left on calculators would change Module 11 and break K12's doc_type demo (calculators retrieve has no doc_type).)
- **Revised:** decision_needed revised after review (Settle the lesson-state question before phase 2.)
- **Closes:** GAP-G01, PRES-01, PRES-08, L10.1-W02, L10.1-W03, L10.1-W04, L10.1-W10, L10.1-S03, L10.3-S04, L10.4-W01, L10.4-W02, BENCH-T01_agent_loop_function_calling, BENCH-T03_tool_design, BENCH-T29_agentic_rag

### A3. Show the agent's work: steps on every turn, figures written by code, and a deterministic figure check

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit · **First phase:** P1 · **After:** A2
- **Reuses:** Register GAP-G07's interim suggestion: print a full turn with tool-call arguments, each result and the final message. Gaps plan section 4.5 G3's rule ('no prompts, answers, tool arguments or credentials in spans'), applied to log rows as well. Section 4.1 G8's escape of agent text before render_with_citations. This departs from G3 by providing a response-level trajectory now, without spans. G3 adds spans later and needs no row key from this task.
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/services/chat/brains.py: _summary() (brains.py:61-82) returns a fifth key, steps. For each tool call of the turn, in order, it records: round (which model message asked, so parallel calls share a round), tool, args as the model wrote them, status, and a result summary. For retrieve the summary is passage numbers and sources with no text; for a calculator it is the value and formula; for an error result it is the refusal text. DirectBrain's steps are one retrieve marked 'by code' (brains.py:325-354). AdkBrain._run (brains.py:304-321) builds the same from the events' function calls and responses.
  - deploy/services/chat/calc_tools.py: verify(answer, calcs, ledger, message). Every number and date written in the answer, with citation markers removed (desk_calc.numbers_in and dates_in, desk_calc.py:462-497), counts as unsupported unless any of its readings appears in the person's message, in a passage this turn read, or in a calculation's value or formula. In calculators mode the brain appends one code-written line per unsupported figure beside the worked one, and the response carries verify: {unsupported: [...]}.
  - deploy/services/chat/agent.py: the response carries steps and verify. The chat row gains unsupported_figures (a count). No tool argument enters Cloud Logging, so the person's wage stays out of the row.
  - deploy/services/frontend/chat.py: the agent branch (chat.py:140-177) gains an expander, 'How this answer was worked out', listing each step (tool, arguments, result or refusal), escaped with html.escape the way the answer is (chat.py:162-165). The caption adds the toolset. _headers, stream_answer and the cost lines stay unchanged because 6.3, 6.4 and 8.1 quote them.
  - deploy/commands/tests/test_chat_brains.py: steps from all four brains; one round for two parallel calls; verify flags 'Rs 2,50,000' against a calculation of Rs 2,40,000, passes the right answer, and ignores [n] markers and a year written in a passage; the UI escapes a '<' in an argument; the chat row carries no args. deploy/smoke/smoke_chat.py: the step names equal tool_calls.
  - Rebuilds: 10.1 (the summary excerpt), 10.2 (its _turn excerpt if the block moves), 10.4, and every page that says 'the same four keys'. check_lesson confirms 6.3, 6.4 and 8.1 are unchanged.
- **Proof:** As before, plus the exact redeploy that reaches the UI: make build deploy-services PROJECT=documind-ai-YOUR-ID REGION=REGION SERVICES="chat ui" SCRIPTS="commands/deploy-chat.sh commands/lesson-12.4.sh" ADMIN_EMAILS="$ME" (ADMIN_EMAILS required, gaps plan section 4.1, or lesson-12.4.sh resets the UI admins). The same line goes into A4's and A7's setup steps. Offline tests include 'Code on Social Security, 2020' and 's.53' not flagged.
- **Risks:** Arguments can hold personal data (a wage, a date). They return only to the person who asked and are never logged. The verifier can flag a legitimate derived number. It appends a line and never blocks, and any reading counts (2.4 lakh = 2,40,000). A fifth key changes pages that say 'four keys'.
- **Decision:** Whether to return tool arguments to the caller in steps and in the UI. Recommended yes, since the caller already wrote or can read every value; never in logs or spans.
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: (1) Scope of the UI change is the agent branch only (frontend/chat.py:141-177); the UI's 'direct' radio streams from rag-api and never calls /v1/chat (chat.py:103 help text, :141), so direct shows no expander/toolset; direct's steps are shown via the /v1/chat API (chat10 direct) only. (2) verify() support set also counts each calculation's cites and basis strings and each passage's source name and section; Act-year and section patterns ('2020', 's.53', 'Code on Social Security, 2020') are exempt; tests cover these false-flag cases. (after review: A3 issues: direct never reaches /v1/chat (verified frontend/chat.py:103,141); verifier false positives on Act years/sections.)
- **Revised:** proof revised after review (A3 issue: lane proof needs the UI image rebuilt with ADMIN_EMAILS.)
- **Also:** (final review) Extends G4's steps_view (adds the toolset and verify lines) instead of adding a second expander; steps and arguments are returned only while CHAT_STEPS is on (decision 3).
- **Closes:** GAP-G01, PRES-09, PRES-10, L10.1-W06, L10.4-W04, BENCH-T01_agent_loop_function_calling, BENCH-T29_agentic_rag

### A4. Lesson 10.1 rebuilt around the contract the code enforces, with the floor measured

- **Effort:** L · **Kind:** lesson · **First phase:** P1 · **After:** A1, A2, A3
- **Reuses:** Course plan row 10.1 (one retrieve, the adapter, the floor) is kept. Register GAP-G01 and GAP-G02 suggestions. Gaps plan decision 15: proof text is rewritten in the lesson's own PR. The Desk plan section 4.10 lane proofs (a gratuity estimate; a number not in the passages refused) appear first here, on the chat brains.
- **Kit:**
  - No kit change beyond A1-A3. lessons/10-agents/10.1-agent-loop/build.py takes excerpts through pagekit block() and computes the contract, the check_args verdicts and every figure from the kit at build time (desk_calc on the corpus clauses, as the selftest's _clause_passage does). It drops Q_COST and the PLAN stand-ins for the live cells.
  - Regenerate deploy/workshop_demos/module_10/lesson_10_1/ with tools/build_workshop_demos.py and update its digest in tools/workshop_demo_reviewed.json.
  - Rewrite the 10.1 proof in course-manifest.json, row 173 of plan/course-plan-v5-story-2026-09-22.md and the roadmap row, in this lesson's PR (gaps plan decision 15).
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.1**, Level 0 (step 1) theory and interactive: Reads that a tool contract is a schema plus the runtime context plus provenance, i.e. where each argument may come from. Reads workflow against agent as a question of who owns the next step: code in the direct brain and the Desk, the model in the loop. Reads why a calculator tool beats model arithmetic for a regulated figure. Plays the panel 'One turn, three ways': direct, agent on classic tools, agent on calculators, each against four questions (a lookup, a gratuity figure, zeta's cap, no wage given). *The learner sees:* Each path's steps, with check_args's real refusal texts and the calculators' figures computed by the build from desk_calc on acme's clauses, and the rag-api rows and model calls each path makes.
  - **10.1**, Level 1 (step 2) definitions: Reads the new rows: toolset, passages, ledger, argument provenance (check_args), 'Worked out in code', steps. *The learner sees:* Each word with its value on the lane, e.g. toolset classic, then calculators after step 8.
  - **10.1**, Level 2 (step 3) the chat service: Runs the same make deploy-services. The history of the region bug is cut to one sentence and the invoker list moves to a collapsed note. *The learner sees:* The same deploy and /health output, in about 400 fewer words.
  - **10.1**, Level 2 (step 4) the contract, with the 'Change it' step: Runs cell 1 (stdlib, reads the source): the eight tools as Gemini receives them, the SYSTEM_CALC and check_args excerpts. Runs cell 2 (stdlib): check_args on acme's LV-07. Runs cell 3 (google-genai from lesson 3.3, location global, one call, about Rs 0.3). It sends the eight declarations, jn-03's question and a retrieve result holding LV-07 and NP-03 read from evals/corpus/acme/hr_policy_2026.md, and executes nothing. Change it: delete the prompt line 'Work every figure out with a calculator' (or vague the first line of encashable_days' docstring), re-run cell 3, compare, then restore with git checkout. *The learner sees:* Cell 2: balance 50 from the message accepted, cap 100 from the message refused with the kit's own sentence, cap 45 from LV-07 accepted. Cell 3: the function and arguments Gemini chose, typically encashable_days(balance=50, cap=45). After the edit, the model's own figure in prose or a different call: the prompt and the descriptions are the behavioural knobs.
  - **10.1**, Level 2 (step 5) the one retrieve(): Calls retrieve() from the shell on the gratuity question twice, with passages False and then True. *The learner sees:* 25-word quotes plus rag-api's own answer, against full passage text with no answer. Then rag-api's two rows: query, with tokens and cost; passages, with tokens_out 0 and cost 0.
  - **10.1**, Level 3 (steps 6-7) the floor and the classic loop: Runs chat10, which now prints steps: the direct brain, then langchain on classic tools, both on the gratuity-years lookup. *The learner sees:* Direct: retrieve 'by code'. Langchain: the same fact, more seconds and more rupees. The page says plainly that the agent does not earn its cost here.
  - **10.1**, Level 3 (step 8) switch the agent's tools: Runs make agent-tools TENANT=acme TOOLS=calculators, waits 60 s, and re-runs step 7's cell. *The learner sees:* The same answer, toolset calculators, the query the model wrote, retrieve reading passages, and the cost line as the lane measures it.
  - **10.1**, Level 3 (step 9) questions only an agent answers: Asks, on both brains: (a) 'I earn Rs 52,000 a month and have 7 years 8 months of continuous service. Roughly how much gratuity would I get?' (b) 'Zeta lets people carry 18 days forward. I will have 25 days left; how many carry into next year?' *The learner sees:* (a) Steps retrieve -> gratuity_estimate(52000, 7, 8), and 'Rs 52,000 / 26 x 15 x 8 = Rs 2,40,000, an estimate' written by code with 'your message' as the source, beside whatever the direct brain answered. (b) Either carry_forward(cap=18) refused ('cap = 18 is not written in LV-01 of a handbook passage this turn read') and then acme's 30 used, or 30 at once. Never 18: provenance and the tenant boundary in one cell.
  - **10.1**, Level 4 (step 10) the rows: Reads rag-api's rows and the chat rows for steps 6-9. *The learner sees:* query versus passages events, and per turn the toolset, model_calls and cost. Per question: the direct brain's rupees, the agent's rupees, and whether the figure was answered.
  - **10.1**, Level 5 (step 11) and checklist: Reads the design choices and the cost table. Optionally changes one line of cell 3 to mode ANY (calling modes). Optionally runs the gratuity question through Gemini's built-in code execution (about Rs 0.1). *The learner sees:* A forced call refused by check_args, and a code-execution figure with no clause and no source. The updated 'does not do yet' box: classic still pays rag-api; month totals; on the chat brains a figure may come from any passage, reviewed or not, while the Desk adds the registry (10.6). The check_one_retrieval credit is gone. Checklist rows for every step, and a lane box noting acme stays on calculators (make agent-tools TOOLS=classic reverts).
- **Proof:** python pagekit/check_lesson.py 10.1 and python pagekit/audit_pages.py 10.1 pass (R39 is the known exception). The live cells are rehearsed once on the author's lane and pasted as labelled samples, with ids and project numbers as placeholders. New manifest proof: '`gratuity_estimate` after `retrieve` in `tool_calls`, its figure worked out in code; a number the person did not give refused by `check_args`'.
- **Risks:** Length: the page is about 8,400 words, so the cost demo and the region-bug history go to make room. ### A5. A check before the answer leaves: a verify node in the LangGraph brain (10.2)

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **First phase:** P1 · **After:** A2, A3
- **Reuses:** Gaps plan section 4.3 G2's 10.2 graph, where approve replaces refuse and interrupt() comes before any side effect. verify sits after the agent's final answer, so the two compose: agent -> tools|approve -> agent -> verify -> END. Register GAP-G01 (a verify node before END) and GAP-G02 (10.2: write the verify node). Gaps plan section 5 (langgraph 1.2.12 state semantics confirmed).
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/services/chat/brains.py LangGraphBrain, calculators toolset only. The state becomes a TypedDict over MessagesState with verify_rounds: int. route sends an answer with no tool calls to a verify node instead of END. verify runs calc_tools.verify. If figures are unsupported and verify_rounds < VERIFY_ROUNDS (1), it adds a HumanMessage named 'verify' (e.g. 'Your answer states Rs 2,50,000; no calculation, passage or the person's message gives it; the calculation gave Rs 2,40,000. Correct the answer.') and returns to agent, which is metered like any call. Otherwise it goes to END, and compose appends the code's line. _summary adds a verify step. The classic graph (agent, tools, refuse; brains.py:149-196) stays byte for byte.
  - deploy/commands/tests/test_chat_brains.py: a scripted wrong figure corrected in one round; a second wrong figure ends with the code's line and no third call; the Meter counts the correction call; the classic graph's nodes and checkpoints are unchanged; the calculators graph saves one more checkpoint per turn, and the test states it.
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.2**, Level 3, a new step after step 4 (the refuse node): Runs a scripted turn through the kit's calculators graph in ~/graph-venv. The model's prose says Rs 2,50,000 while gratuity_estimate returned Rs 2,40,000. Change it: in the same cell, compile the graph with the answer routed straight to END (one edge), re-run, then restore. *The learner sees:* The node path agent -> tools -> agent -> verify -> agent -> verify -> END and the corrected answer. Without the edge, the wrong figure goes out, with only the code's line beside it.
  - **10.2**, Level 3 (step 5) the graph on your lane: Asks the gratuity question of brain langgraph, with acme on calculators. *The learner sees:* A verify step in steps, with unsupported [].
  - **10.2**, Level 5: Reads why a deterministic evaluator beats a second model call for figures, why the retry is bounded, and why plan-and-execute is not needed for one-to-three-step turns. *The learner sees:* The evaluator-optimizer pattern in kit terms, and its cost: at most one extra call.
- **Proof:** Offline tests are green. python pagekit/check_lesson.py 10.2 and audit_pages.py 10.2 pass. On a lane, the LangGraph turn shows a verify step with unsupported [].
- **Risks:** The 'verify' HumanMessage stays in the thread and the next turn reads it; the page states this. A mid-thread SystemMessage is avoided because langchain-google-genai is expected to take the system prompt only first; confirm in the pinned 4.4.0 source before coding. A false positive costs at most one extra call. The human-approval workstream (G2) and the LangGraph-depth workstream (G13) also re-cut 10.2.
- **Decision:** VERIFY_ROUNDS: 1 (recommended) or 0 (the code's line only, no extra call). Which 'Change it' 10.2 keeps if G2's approval scene also wants one: this edge, or the approval.
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: (1) VERIFY_ROUNDS read from env (default 1) when the calculators graph is built, so a cell can build the kit's own graph with VERIFY_ROUNDS=0 (no copied graph). (2) _turn() input for the calculators graph resets verify_rounds to 0 every turn (the field has no reducer and would carry across checkpointed turns). (3) Tests add a two-turn case: a corrected turn, then a later turn with a wrong figure still gets one retry. (4) Before coding, confirm in langchain-google-genai 4.4.0 that a mid-thread HumanMessage named 'verify' is sent as a user turn. (after review: A5 issues: verify_rounds carries over on a checkpointed thread; the Change-it edited a copy because the graph compiles in LangGraphBrain.__init__ (brains.py:149-196).)
- **Revised:** lesson_changes: Replace the 10.2 Change-it with a compare: re-run the same scripted cell with VERIFY_ROUNDS=0 (the kit's own setting) and compare; the learner-written node in 10.2 is B4's Change-it (one Change-it per level). Step 5 lane: switch acme to calculators for the step and back (make agent-tools). Add to 10.2 Level 0 the L10.2-S06 pointer sentence: Gemini's function calling returns calls only to declared functions (10.1 shows the declarations and calling modes; I2). (after review: Avoid a copied graph and a second Change-it in 10.2; add the missing L10.2-S06 pointer; A2 calculators scope.)
- **Also:** (final review) VERIFY_ROUNDS defaults to 0, which skips the verify node (the figure line written by code still prints); 1 runs one verify round. test_chat_brains.py sets VERIFY_ROUNDS=1 explicitly. 10.2's core Change-it sets it to 1 and compares the answer and the Meter's model_calls (decision 12); B4's learner-written node goes in K7's go-deeper level.
- **Closes:** GAP-G01, GAP-G02, PRES-03, L10.2-W01, L10.2-S06, L10.4-S04, BENCH-T05_planning_self_correction

### A6. An agent eval that measures each brain against the floor on cost and quality

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit · **First phase:** P1 · **After:** A2, A3
- **Reuses:** Gaps plan section 4.4 G1's handoff_eval pattern: judge.trajectory_metrics, a session per row and run, explains and never gates, an offline self-test in CI. Section 4.1 G8-b: per-question sessions. run_eval.contains. Desk build plan section 1.3: exact McNemar with a pre-registered margin. Register GAP-G05's suggestion: 10-15 rows, half needing a calculator or two documents (8 of 14 here).
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/evals/agent_rows.jsonl (new, 14 author-written acme rows; every figure recomputed by desk_calc in --selftest). Four lookups: gratuity years ('five years'); E3 notice ('60'); EXP-12 travel cap ('40,000'); IT-SEC-04 contractors ('No exception'). Five figure rows: Rs 52,000 for 7 years 8 months ('2,40,000'); 7 years 6 months ('2,10,000', the part-year rule); jn-03 ('45', '60'); notice acknowledged 5 October 2026 ('2026-12-04'); the same plus final wages ('2026-12-04', '2026-12-08', the chain). Two two-document rows from golden.jsonl: jn-10 and jn-08. Two provenance rows: no wage given (must ask, no gratuity figure); zeta's 18 days (acme's '30', 18 never used). One eligibility row: 3 years 2 months, resigning (under five years, no figure). Fields: id, kind, question, must_contain, must_not_contain, reference (tool names in order), args (expected calculator arguments), source, note.
  - deploy/evals/agent_eval.py (new, stdlib). --selftest covers the rows against desk_calc, the scoring maths and the paired test. --live posts each row to /v1/chat for each brain in BRAINS as documind-ui-sa, minted as smoke_chat.py mints. Each row, brain and run gets its own session, aeval-{run8}-{brain}-{row}, within the 64-character rule (agent.py:147). It scores: correct, using run_eval.contains (the gate's matcher); in-order trajectory against the row's reference (judge.trajectory_metrics, judge.py:135-145); argument accuracy from A3's steps; refused-then-corrected; model calls; rupees from limits.cost_inr (model plus rag-api); latency. --runs k gives pass^k. It prints a per-row table, per-brain lines with Wilson intervals, and each agent brain against direct on the same rows: discordant pairs and the exact McNemar p, or 'inconclusive' when there are too few pairs. Every line carries the toolset. Options: --save run.jsonl, and --vertex for an optional groundedness pass through judge.to_frame and judge.evaluate.
  - deploy/evals/paired.py (new, stdlib): exact McNemar on discordant pairs plus the pre-registered margin rule, shared with route_eval (A9). deploy/evals/tests/test_agent_eval.py and test_paired.py (new); CI's documind-dryrun.yml already runs evals/tests.
  - deploy/mk/agents.mk: agent-eval: guard-project, with BRAINS (default 'direct langchain'), ROWS (ids or a kind), RUNS and SAVE. Add a row to deploy/mk/README.md.
  - deploy/evals/judge.py: G8-b exactly as gaps plan section 4.1 designs it (session judge-{run8}-{brain}-{row id}, deploy/evals/tests/test_judge_sessions.py, 7.2's rebuild), unless the evaluation workstream lands it first. agent_eval does not depend on it; it reuses only trajectory_metrics.
- **Proof:** agent_eval.py --selftest covers value matching ('4 December 2026' == '2026-12-04'), the cache-marked repeat and the interval maths; tests green in evals/tests; on a lane, make agent-eval BRAINS="direct langchain" prints 14 rows per brain with first-run rupees and every line's toolset; the four-brain run and rupees recorded in the PR.
- **Risks:** 14 rows give wide intervals, so the page reads rows, not percentages, and calls a verdict inconclusive below a minimum number of discordant pairs. Runs vary between attempts; RUNS=3 for pass^k triples the cost. Estimated cost (the page states the measured figure): about Rs 25-35 for two brains, Rs 60-90 for four. The answer cache (Module 9) can price a repeated direct question at 0, and the line marks it. must_contain on code-written figures favours calculators by design, so lookups are scored on the documents' own words.
- **Decision:** (1) The 14 rows and their wording. (2) The pre-registered rule for switching a tenant to calculators; proposed: no lookup row regresses against classic, and every figure row classic answers stays correct. (3) Who lands G8-b: here, or the evaluation workstream. (4) The margin test's exact form. The Desk plan cites research report section 7.4, which is not in this repository.
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: (1) Cost source is the /v1/chat response field limits.cost_inr (Meter.summary: model plus rag-api), tested against a recorded response. (2) Figures scored by value: each figure row's must_contain is matched by desk_calc.numbers_in/dates_in readings (2,40,000 = 240000 = 2.4 lakh; 4 December 2026 = 2026-12-04) as well as run_eval.contains; the page notes any residual format bias. (3) Answer cache: every repeat run of a row reads rag-api's cache indicator; cached repeats are marked 'cache hit, Rs 0', excluded from pass^k, and cost is reported per row as first-run cost. (4) Method fixed before any run: paired difference with a 95% score (Newcombe) interval, non-inferior when the lower bound > -margin, McNemar p printed beside; the page says 14 rows will usually be inconclusive. (5) Switch rule run: flip the flag, wait SETTINGS_TTL_S (60 s) plus a minute, check every line's toolset before scoring. (6) G8-b removed from A6: E1 owns it. (after review: A6 issues: limits.cost_inr is a response field; format bias in contains(); cache distorts direct; McNemar is not a non-inferiority test; cache mixing during flips; G8-b duplicated with E1.)
- **Revised:** proof revised after review (Proof follows the corrected method; G8-b's proofs move to E1.)
- **Revised:** effort revised to M after review (Re-estimate after removing G8-b.)
- **Revised:** depends_on: A2, A3, E1 (G8-b), E2 (shared paired.py; one implementation). (after review: A6 and E2 both build evals/paired.py and an agent eval; land one paired.py.)
- **Closes:** GAP-G05, GAP-G15, PRES-01, L10.1-W09, L10.4-W02, L10.4-W03, L10.4-S01, L10.4-S04, L10.4-S06, PLAN-G8, BENCH-T18_agent_eval_offline, BENCH-T29_agentic_rag

### A9. Lesson 10.7: routed desks against code only and against one agent, on the same rows (arms B, C and A*)

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **First phase:** P5 · **After:** A6, A8
- **Reuses:** Desk build plan section 1.3 (the ship decision: exact McNemar with a pre-registered margin) and section 4.10 ('make route-eval SPLIT=dev ARM=Astar runs'). route_eval.py's own ARMS, LIVE_ARMS and run_arm, and the ARM= already in mk/agents.mk.
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/evals/route_eval.py: --compare runB.jsonl runC.jsonl [runAstar.jsonl] reads saved runs of one split. Per arm it prints correct_rate, authority_rate, the router's and the answers' rupees, and p95 latency. Then it compares B against C and B against A* on the same rows: discordant pairs, exact McNemar and the pre-registered margin (deploy/evals/paired.py from A6), with the line 'dev split: B's kNN index holds these rows (an upper bound); C and A* use no index'.
  - deploy/evals/route_eval.py: --sample agent, a fixed subset (every figure row and every fifth answer row, by id), so a learner's A* run costs a fraction of the full dev split. Selftest and deploy/evals/tests/test_route_eval.py cases for both options.
  - deploy/mk/agents.mk: pass SAMPLE= through route-eval (agents.mk:205-216), and add a route-compare target that reads three SAVE files.
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.7**, Level 4, a new step after the dev route eval (parts/c.html:178-186): Runs make route-eval SPLIT=dev SAMPLE=agent SAVE=b.jsonl, then the same with ARM=C and with ARM=Astar, each with its own SAVE file, then make route-compare. *The learner sees:* Per-arm lines and the paired verdicts, read as an upper bound for B. On figure rows C cannot compute; on lookups A* pays for a loop it does not need; on fig-04, which spans two desks, A* can chain where routed desks with desk_max_parts 1 cannot. The author's full-split figures are in the PR; the learner's are 'yours'.
  - **10.7**, Level 5: Reads the ship rule (B ships only if non-inferior to C and to A*, route_eval.py:61-64; Desk plan section 1.3) and why people must write the test split first. *The learner sees:* When not to split into specialists, measured.
- **Proof:** route_eval.py --selftest and test_route_eval.py green; three saved runs on the id-list sample and make route-compare on the author's lane; check_lesson and audit_pages on the lesson that holds the step.
- **Risks:** An A* run costs a few flash calls per answer row (agents.mk:211-212), so the sample keeps the learner's run small. Dev contamination inflates B only. With few figure rows the verdict is inconclusive, and the page says so. The gate on the test split waits for the Desk workstream's people-written rows.
- **Decision:** (1) The sample's rule. (2) Run the full dev split on the author's lane only (recommended), or for learners too. (3) The margin test's form (research report section 7.4, not in the repository).

Sample: an explicit id list (all fig-* rows, jn-03, plus every fifth answer row by id), checked by --selftest, not an inferred 'figure row' field. (after review: KIT-03 asks for the A* toolset on a page; '--sample agent' had no field to select figure rows.)

## B. Learners build and steer agents

**Goal.** Every Act V lesson has the learner change the agent and watch its behaviour change: one edit inside an existing level (a tool, a docstring, a system-prompt line, a node, a threshold, a contract, a refusal), the same cell re-run, before and after side by side, the edit put back in the same step. A bench, make agent-turn, runs the kit's own brain from the learner's clone with the real Gemini model against their lane and prints the whole turn (the prompt, each tool call with its arguments, each result, the answer, the rupees), so a change shows in seconds without a redeploy. The 'What the kit does not do yet' boxes stop reading as an unfinished kit: defects that can lose or leak data, mislead the model, fail a deploy or run up cost are fixed in the kit with regression tests, and harmless ones (diagnosability, a comparison's fairness, request strictness) become the learner's Change-it.

### B1. make agent-turn: one real turn of a kit brain from your clone, printed whole

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit · **First phase:** P0
- **Reuses:** Lesson 10.4 step 6's OWN_PY cell (lessons/10-agents/10.4-adapters/build.py), which already builds the kit's three agent brains on the operator's machine with InMemorySaver, GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT, RAG_API_URL and DOCUMIND_IMPERSONATE_SA. Also commands/limits-check.sh's venv, and gaps plan section 3's single chat-pins CI job and one .mk file per module. The bench is not a trace: G3's make trace-ask and make trace (gaps plan section 4.5) stay the cross-service view, and the two words stay distinct.
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/commands/agent_turn.py (new): builds brains.build(BRAIN, InMemorySaver()) from the clone's services/chat, or desk_agent.run() when DESK=handbook|statute|astar, on the operator's machine. On the gcp profile: Gemini on location global through ADC and GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT, the one retrieve() against the lane's documind-api minting as documind-ui-sa (DOCUMIND_IMPERSONATE_SA), tenant acme as tenant_for() picks it. Prints per turn: each model call's tool calls with their arguments (calls from one model message grouped as parallel), each tool result's status, size and first 300 characters, refusals, the answer, the citations' n, and the Meter (model calls, tokens in and out, Rs, rag-api's Rs).
  - Same script, options: SHOW=system prints SYSTEM verbatim with its sha256[:12]; SHOW=tools prints the FunctionDeclarations Gemini receives (langchain_google_genai._function_utils.convert_to_genai_function_declarations over tools.TOOLS; FunctionTool._get_declaration() for ADK); Q may repeat, one turn each on one thread; REPEAT=n runs the conversation on n fresh threads and prints, per turn, k of n for each tool; OFFLINE=1 uses a stand-in rag-api and a scripted model, no credential and Rs 0 (CI and page builds); DOCUMIND_PROFILE=local passes through for an Rs 0 loop on Ollama and Chroma.
  - deploy/commands/agent-turn.sh (new): makes or completes ~/graph-venv from services/chat/requirements.txt as commands/limits-check.sh does, derives RAG_API_URL from the project number and REGION, sets RAG_TIMEOUT_S=90 and the impersonation, runs the script. deploy/commands/agent-tests.sh (new): commands/tests/test_chat_brains.py, test_chat_limits.py and test_agent_turn.py in the same venv with DOCUMIND_REQUIRE_LIBS=1, the net under every Change-it.
  - deploy/mk/agents.mk: targets agent-turn (guard-project unless OFFLINE=1) and agent-tests, both added to the Makefile's .PHONY; deploy/mk/README.md rows; kit comments say 'workshop lesson N.M'.
  - deploy/commands/tests/test_agent_turn.py (new, offline): every brain's trace names each call's arguments and each result's status; REPEAT counts; DESK mode shows a check_args refusal from a scripted model; no question text in any log line the script writes. Added to the chat-pins CI step in .github/workflows/checks.yml and deploy/.github/workflows/documind-dryrun.yml.
  - python tools/kit_index.py, so deploy/INDEX.md lists the new files and the lessons that run them.
- **Proof:** python -m unittest commands/tests/test_agent_turn.py passes offline in the chat pins and in CI. On a lane, make agent-turn PROJECT=documind-ai-YOUR-ID REGION=REGION Q='After how many years of continuous service does gratuity become payable?' SHOW=system,tools prints SYSTEM, both declarations, '-> retrieve(query=..., top_k=5)', '<- retrieve [success] ...', the answer and the Meter's rupees. The build checks that the printed declarations equal convert_to_genai_function_declarations(tools.TOOLS).
- **Risks:** Real-model runs differ, so pages show bench output from a lane only as a labelled sample and assert structure from OFFLINE. convert_to_genai_function_declarations sits in a private module of langchain-google-genai 4.4.0; it is pinned, and the test fails first on an upgrade. The bench reaches the lane as documind-ui-sa, which is on three rosters, so it pins the tenant. ADC needs Vertex AI and Firestore access; a lane owner has both. A lane turn costs about Rs 1 at list price (an estimate: two flash calls of about 2,000 tokens in and 150 out, plus one rag-api answer). Pages print the Meter's figure, never this estimate.
- **Decision:** Approve showing labelled lane samples of real-model bench output on pages (CLAUDE.md's 'a run on a live lane'), with every asserted before and after taken from OFFLINE.
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: (1) Multi-turn input: Q1, Q2, ... Qn (or QFILE=), one thread; a test asserts two questions make two turns on one thread (GNU make keeps only the last Q=). (2) Tenant pinned: tenant_id='acme' set in the context as 10.4's OWN_PY does, printed in the trace header (ui-sa is on three rosters; tenant_for uses limit(1)). (3) SHOW=tools prints what binding actually sends: ChatGoogleGenerativeAI(...).bind_tools(tools.TOOLS).kwargs['tools'] (or a captured request payload); ADK via FunctionTool._get_declaration(); the build compares against that, not against the same converter. (4) KIT-11 dropped from closes. (5) agent-turn.sh fails fast with the ADC login command when ADC is missing; pages state the one-time venv install time. (6) Each probabilistic Change-it page says what the learner may see instead; only OFFLINE before/after is asserted; REPEAT where a rate is the point. (after review: B1 issues: Q repeat through make, circular build check, KIT-11 mapping, tenant pick, ADC friction, probabilistic outcomes.)
- **Revised:** effort revised to M after review (Scope was optimistic.)
- **Also:** (final review) SHOW=tools prints what Gemini receives: convert_to_genai_function_declarations(bound.kwargs['tools']) for LangChain and LangGraph (langchain-google-genai 4.4.0, chat_models.py:3817 and :3925-3937) and FunctionTool._get_declaration() for ADK. B1's SHOW=tools, G1's /v1/chat/contract and I2's declare step are one implementation, compared in CI against a captured request payload.
- **Closes:** GAP-G02, PRES-09, L10.1-W05, L10.1-W06, L10.1-S01, L10.2-W13, L10.2-S06, KIT-11, BENCH-T03_tool_design, BENCH-T21_testing_agents

### B2. Change-it steps as a checked page primitive, and a deploy of an edit that cannot overwrite the kit's image

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** process · **First phase:** P0
- **Reuses:** pagekit's block(), filler() and window() and the build-against-stand-ins pattern. Gaps plan section 3's rule that a kit pull request carries every rebuild it forces (decision 16), extended: a kit change that stops a Change-it diff applying carries that lesson's rebuild.
- **Kit:**
  - pagekit/pagebuild.py: change_it(title, diff, files, rerun_ref, before, after) renders inside the current level, in this order: the diff in a read-only window; a bash window running git -C $DEMO_ROOT apply on the diff as a heredoc, then git diff --stat; 'run the cell from step N again'; the before and after outputs (read only, labelled); a bash window that puts it back with git -C $DEMO_ROOT checkout -- FILES; one Verify row; and a line in the lane box saying whether anything was deployed.
  - pagekit/changeit.py (new): applied(diff) copies deploy/ to a temp directory and applies the diff with git apply, so build.py runs the same cell on the kit and on the copy and asserts the difference.
  - pagekit/check_lesson.py: a new rule with four checks. Every Change-it diff applies with git apply --check to the current deploy/. Its restore line names exactly the diff's files. The block sits inside an existing level, with no new step number. Both outputs are present. pagekit/audit_pages.py: apply and restore windows are bash (copy button), and the diff window is read only.
  - tools/build_workshop_demos.py and tools/check_workshop_demos.py: each Change-it emits demo_NN_change_it.py, which applies the diff, runs the cell, prints, and restores in a finally; the check refuses a Change-it demo without its restore. tools/kit_index.py: INDEX.md maps each kit file a Change-it edits to 'Changed in: N.M'.
  - deploy/Makefile line 77: GIT_SHA gains a -dirty suffix when tracked files differ from HEAD (still 'dev' outside git). make build from a clone holding a Change-it then pushes chat:SHA-dirty and never overwrites chat:SHA, and the next clean make build deploy-services puts the kit's code back.
  - CLAUDE.md, on the author's approval: one rule under 'Writing a lesson page' permitting a Change-it step inside an existing level (one edit in the learner's clone, the same cell re-run, before and after shown, the edit put back in the step), with no exercise grid and no quiz.
- **Proof:** A pagekit self-test lesson builds. check_lesson fails when the diff no longer applies and when the restore line is missing. tools/build_workshop_demos.py --check, tools/check_workshop_demos.py and deploy/workshop_demos/tests/run_tests.py pass. In a dirty clone, make -n build SERVICES=chat prints a tag ending -dirty. python tools/publish_learner.py --check passes.
- **Risks:** A clone with other local edits fails git apply; the page points to the setup's git status and git stash. An edit left in place makes the next lesson's setup refuse its git pull, so every step ends with the restore and the demo restores in a finally. CRLF line endings break git apply on a Windows clone; the workstation clone is Linux. The -dirty suffix changes what pages quoting Makefile:77 print, so check_lesson names those rebuilds.
- **Decision:** (1) Approve the CLAUDE.md line. (2) Choose the rule for the box defects. Recommended: a defect that can lose or leak data, mislead the model, fail a deploy or run up cost is fixed in the kit with a test (B4's refuse words, B14, B15, B16, B17). A harmless one (diagnosability, a comparison's fairness, request strictness) becomes the lesson's Change-it, with its box line reworded to name the step (B5, B6, B11). The alternative is that the kit keeps every defect for learners to fix forward. (3) Decide whether a Change-it may deploy an edited image to the lane. Recommended: only in 12.2, plus optional sub-steps in 10.1 and 11.3, each with its restore deploy named in 'What changed on your lane'.
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: (1) check_lesson: a Change-it diff window carries a class/label prefix ('change-it diff:') that verbatim() skips; the Change-it rule instead checks the diff's context and '-' lines against deploy/ and its '+' lines against the patched copy. (2) Primary path for one-line edits (a docstring line, a SYSTEM sentence, a threshold, durability=, extra='forbid') is a hand edit checked with git diff against the shown diff; the heredoc git apply is the fallback. (3) The Change-it rule runs over every lesson on every kit PR; affected Wix re-uploads ship with the learner publish (extends gaps decision 16). (4) -dirty is scoped to the kit tree: git diff --quiet HEAD -- . from deploy/; and deploy-services checks that the image tag exists (B17's check, so B2 depends on B17). (5) Demo naming demo_NN_change_it_<slug>.py, excluded from the normal step harvest in tools/build_workshop_demos.py. (after review: B2 issues: verbatim rule, paste-and-run, page/kit publish drift, -dirty scope, demo harvest.)
- **Revised:** depends_on: B17 (image-tag existence check). (after review: A deploy-services must not point at a tag never built.)
- **Closes:** GAP-G02, PRES-03, PRES-08, BENCH-T21_testing_agents

### B3. 10.1: write a third tool and watch the model choose it

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** lesson · **First phase:** P1 · **After:** B1, B2, GAP-G01 owner's choice of the chat brains' calculators, live-lane rehearsal (PRES-02 owner)
- **Reuses:** shared/desk_calc.py's notice_end and its selftest, so the arithmetic is already tested. Gaps plan G8's one tool list (section 4.1), so the edit reaches every agent brain. 10.1's CONTRACT_PY and chat10 cells.
- **Kit:**
  - No deploy/ change; the learner's edit is put back. lessons/10-agents/10.1-agent-loop/build.py gains the Change-it: the diff to services/chat/tools.py is applied to a temp kit copy, and CONTRACT_PY and B1's OFFLINE bench run before and after. deploy/workshop_demos/module_10/lesson_10_1/ gains demo_NN_change_it.py.
  - The learner's diff (about 20 lines): @tool notice_end(ack_date: str, days: int) wrapping shared/desk_calc.notice_end, with CalcError re-raised as ToolException and handle_tool_error True. It is appended to TOOLS, and the same function is added to for_adk()'s list, so all three agent brains get it.
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.1**, Level 2 'The contract', step 4, after 'What just happened': Runs make agent-turn SHOW=tools Q='What is the travel reimbursement cap at zeta?' to read the declarations Gemini receives and to see the model try another tenant's documents. Then applies the diff and re-runs step 4's contract cell. *The learner sees:* The two declarations exactly as Gemini receives them. retrieve's arguments carry no tenant, and the answer comes from acme's documents. After the diff: three tools, including notice_end(ack_date: str, days: int) with its docstring's first line and no hidden runtime. The build checks that LangChain's tool_call_schema agrees.
  - **10.1**, Level 3 'The loop', step 7: new 'Change it: a third tool' block: Runs make agent-turn SHOW=system Q='I am a confirmed E3 employee at ACME. My resignation was acknowledged in writing on 6 October 2026. What is my last day of notice?' before and after the diff. Runs it once more with notice_end's first docstring line made vague ('A date helper.'). Then runs the restore line. *The learner sees:* SYSTEM verbatim, the first time on any Module 10 page. Before the diff: tool_calls ['retrieve'] and a date the model worked out itself. After: ['retrieve', 'notice_end'], with the arguments it chose (ack_date 2026-10-06, and days from NP-03's passage) and the date desk_calc computed; the build prints the kit's figure. With the vague docstring, whether the model still picks the tool. 'What just happened' names the gap that 10.6's check_args closes: nothing stops a days value that no passage states.
  - **10.1**, Level 5 'Verify', and 'What changed on your lane': Ticks a tenth checklist row. Optional sub-step: deploys the edit with make build deploy-services SERVICES=chat, asks the question in the UI, then deploys again from the clean clone. *The learner sees:* The row 'Your tool is in the contract and the model chose it'. The lane box says nothing changed, unless the optional deploy ran; its -dirty revision is replaced by the clean deploy. In the UI the caption reads 'tools: retrieve, notice_end'.
- **Proof:** build.py checks both sides. On the kit, a scripted call to notice_end comes back as an error result (no such tool), with refusals ['notice_end']. On the patched copy, the same call returns desk_calc's date with refusals [], and CONTRACT_PY prints three tools. check_lesson's Change-it rule and audit_pages pass. The author's lane rehearsal captures the real model's before, after and vague-docstring runs as labelled samples.
- **Risks:** If the GAP-G01 owner binds notice_end to the chat brains for good, the before-state disappears and the build fails; statutory_deadline is then the tool to add. The wrapper has no provenance check, and the page says so. desk_calc refuses a date with no year, so the docstring asks for YYYY-MM-DD. The model's choice varies, so the lane run is shown as a sample. About Rs 4 for four bench turns (an estimate).
- **Decision:** Which calculator the learner adds: notice_end, unless the GAP-G01 owner binds it, with statutory_deadline as the fallback. Also whether the wrapper copies that owner's provenance check once it exists (recommended, so the learner writes the kit's own pattern).
- **Revised:** lesson_changes: Amend: (1) The vague-docstring experiment runs with REPEAT=5 and a sharper manipulation (a misleading first line, or a second overlapping tool), reporting k of n; the page says a 'still chosen' result is possible. (2) The zeta question prints each citation's source_uri (acme/...) and points out a misattributed cap; one chat10 run on the deployed service shows the roster lookup half. (3) The diff writes the ADK twin in TOOLS order, adds a TIMEOUTS entry (10 s) and the build asserts ADK's declaration of notice_end. (4) The optional deploy prints the full command with SCRIPTS=commands/deploy-chat.sh and states the minutes and two Cloud Builds in 'What changed on your lane'. (5) The Change-it runs in 10.1 step 7 on the classic toolset, before A4's step 8. (after review: B3 issues: n=1 weak manipulation, zeta misattribution, for_adk positional pairing/TIMEOUTS, missing SCRIPTS, ordering with A4.)

### B4. 10.2: add a node that checks the citations before the answer leaves

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **First phase:** P2 · **After:** B1, B2, approval workstream's G2 (if it lands first, rebase the diff onto the approve node), live-lane rehearsal (PRES-02 owner)
- **Reuses:** Gaps plan G8 (section 4.1): _turn() ids and the per-turn ledger runtime.context['cited'], which the node reads. 10.2 step 4's scripted-turn cell. It departs from G2 (section 4.3) only by correcting the refuse words now, because G2 has not landed.
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/services/chat/brains.py refuse(): in a refused mixed turn, an allowed call gets {'error': 'not run: refused with <blocked name> in the same reply'}, and the blocked call keeps 'requires manual approval'. deploy/commands/tests/test_chat_brains.py asserts both. This is dropped if the approval workstream's G2 replaces refuse with approve first.
  - lessons/10-agents/10.2-langgraph/build.py: the graph cell also prints g.draw_mermaid(). The venv cell installs services/chat/requirements.txt, as commands/limits-check.sh does, so langgraph 1.2.12 is pinned and checklist item 1 checks it. The Change-it diff is applied to a temp kit copy for the before and after.
  - The learner's diff: check(state, runtime) in LangGraphBrain.__init__, about 12 lines. It reads the last AI message's [n] markers and compares them with runtime.context['cited']. When a marker names no citation of this turn, it returns the message without that marker plus a short note, under the same id, so add_messages replaces it. route's END becomes 'check', with add_edge('check', END). The diff also adds a test to commands/tests/test_chat_brains.py: a scripted answer cites [3] when one passage came back.
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.2**, Level 2 'The graph', step 3: Builds the venv from the chat image's requirements and runs the graph cell. *The learner sees:* The venv line names langgraph's pin too. The graph appears as node and edge lists and as Mermaid text to paste into any viewer.
  - **10.2**, Level 3 'The refuse node', step 4: new 'Change it: a node before END' block: Applies the diff and re-runs step 3's graph cell, then step 4's cell, which now has a fourth scripted turn citing [1] and [3]. Runs make agent-tests. Runs make agent-turn BRAIN=langgraph Q='What notice period does a confirmed E3 employee serve?' on the lane. Then runs the restore line. *The learner sees:* check among the nodes, and seven edges where there were six; agent -> check -> END in the Mermaid. The fourth turn's path is agent -> tools -> agent -> check, and its answer has lost [3] and carries the note. The mixed turn's error result for retrieve now says 'not run' (the kit fix). The kit's brain tests and the learner's own test are green. On the lane, the real model's turn passes through check with every marker in range.
  - **10.2**, Level 1 definitions, Level 5 box and Verify: Reads a new definitions row for stream_mode='updates'. *The learner sees:* What step 4's stream prints. The box loses 'A refused mixed turn misinforms the model'. A Verify row: 'Your node runs before every answer'.
- **Proof:** The build asserts 6 edges on the kit and 7 with the diff, the scripted fourth answer losing [3], and the mixed turn's 'not run' text. test_chat_brains.py passes in the chat pins and in CI with the refuse-words test. check_lesson's Change-it rule passes. A lane sample of the LangGraph turn through check is captured.
- **Risks:** Real Gemini rarely mis-cites, so the scripted turn carries the demonstration. The node edits only the answer message and keeps the turn's question id, which G8's _summary needs. Under G2, a check node must not run on a paused turn, so it is routed only from agent with no tool calls.
- **Decision:** Remove-and-note with no extra model call (recommended), or send the answer back to the agent once with a correction. The second is an evaluator loop: it costs a model call and needs the Meter check the hand-built node already does.
- **Revised:** lesson_changes: Amend: (1) The page renders the before and after graphs as inline SVG laid out at build time from get_graph() (no viewer paste); Mermaid text stays as a copy. (2) The step-4 cell passes context {'tenant_id': 'acme', 'cited': []}; the learner's node tolerates a missing ledger and the build asserts the 'plain' turn keeps [1]. (3) The node edits only text blocks in place under the same message id (Gemini 3 content blocks/thought signatures); the learner's test includes a block-form answer. (4) Function calling: one definitions row (function_call parts and ids, several calls in one message, calling modes, pointer to 10.1/I2). (5) KIT-11 dropped from closes; tools/kit_index.py added after the brains.py and test edits. (after review: B4 issues: L10.2-W09 wants a picture; REFUSE_PY lacks 'cited' (verified 10.2 build.py:179-197); content blocks; L10.2-S06 depth; KIT-11 mapping; kit_index.)
- **Also:** (final review) The learner-written node moves to K7's optional go-deeper level at the end of 10.2; 10.2's core Change-it is A5's VERIFY_ROUNDS 0 to 1 (decision 12).
- **Also:** (final review, for GAP-G02) B4's learner-written check node stays 10.2's 'add a node' Change-it after D3's re-cut. PR34 places it in the level that runs the offline graph cell, rebased onto the approve node, and the build asserts the extra node and edge. Decision 12's VERIFY_ROUNDS 0-to-1 run is A5's compare cell with no learner edit, as A5's revision says, and does not replace this step. L5 keeps the title 'Implement the main LangGraph workflow' because of this step and D3's approval node.
- **Closes:** GAP-G02, PRES-03, PRES-08, L10.2-W01, L10.2-W05, L10.2-W09, L10.2-W11, L10.2-W13, L10.2-S04, L10.2-S06, KIT-11, BENCH-T21_testing_agents

### B5. 10.3: log what the model asked for, safely, and watch it correct a refused argument

- **Effort:** S · **Kind:** lesson · **First phase:** P2 · **After:** B1, B2, B15, live-lane rehearsal (PRES-02 owner)
- **Reuses:** 10.3 step 3's five-failures cell and its log capture. Gaps plan G8's one error contract: status 'error' is the one refusal marker (section 4.1).
- **Kit:**
  - No deploy/ change beyond B15. lessons/10-agents/10.3-tool-failures/build.py: the Change-it diff to GuardMiddleware.wrap_tool_call is applied to a temp copy and FAIL_PY re-runs with its log capture. Also the step 6 adapter line, the diagnosis table and the box text.
  - The learner's diff (4 lines): logger.info('tool %s args %s', name, safe). safe keeps doc_type, top_k, processing_type, total_pages and num_documents as given, and replaces query with its length and sha256[:8], so a person's words never reach the log.
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.3**, Level 3 'An argument', step 6: Runs the existing pair through the one retrieve(), then one more line through the chat adapter with doc_type 'invoice'. *The learner sees:* The shared retrieve() still empties the pool silently, as its docstring says. The adapter now refuses (B15) with an error result that names the classes acme's documents carry (none yet at this lesson's state) and says 'search with doc_type all'.
  - **10.3**, Level 3 'An argument', step 6: new 'Change it: log what the model asked for' block: Applies the diff and re-runs step 3's five-failures cell. Then runs make agent-turn Q='Search only the invoices: what is the total on the April invoice?' on the lane. Then runs the restore line. *The learner sees:* The bad-argument turn's guard line now shows total_pages='many'. On the lane, the model's first call with doc_type 'invoice', the refusal, its second call without the filter, and then the answer: a real model reading an error result and correcting itself. The log holds the argument that rag-api's row never had, and no question text.
  - **10.3**, Level 4 'Diagnosis', step 7, and the Level 5 box: Reads the updated table and box. *The learner sees:* 'Cannot tell: the arguments' now points to the guard's line. The box line 'The arguments are logged nowhere' is reworded as the kit's choice: arguments can carry a person's words, and step 6 logs the safe ones. 'The tool invites filters...' is gone (B15). A new Verify row.
- **Proof:** The build asserts that the guard line with total_pages='many' appears only with the diff, and that no captured log line holds question text. The adapter's refusal equals B15's text. A lane sample of the two-call self-correction is captured.
- **Risks:** Whether the model passes a doc_type at all is its choice; the question is worded to invite one. Logging arguments is a retention decision, which the page states against the lane's _Default log bucket retention. The guard covers the LangChain brain only, and the page names where LangGraph's ToolNode wrapper and ADK's callback would need the same line.
- **Decision:** None beyond B2's rule: argument logging stays the learner's fix. Revisit if the evaluation workstream needs arguments on the chat row.
- **Revised:** lesson_changes: Amend: (1) Step 7's table row reads 'the lane still cannot tell the arguments; with step 6's edit a local run shows them' (offer deploying the edit as an optional sub-step). (2) safe logs doc_type and processing_type only when they are in the allowed set, otherwise their length. (3) The lane sample asserts a second retrieve call without the filter, depending on B15's handle_tool_error. (after review: B5 issues: unrolled-back edit wording, personal text in logs, refusal must arrive as an error result.)
- **Revised:** depends_on: B1, B2, B15 (including retrieve.handle_tool_error = True), live-lane rehearsal (L2). (after review: Self-correction needs B15's error result.)
- **Closes:** GAP-G02, PRES-03, PRES-08, L10.3-W01, L10.3-W04, L10.3-S08, BENCH-T02_structured_output_tool_contracts, BENCH-T03_tool_design

This is valid in google-adk 2.8.0, whose validator rejects only tools, system_instruction, response_schema and a base_url in this config, with google-genai 2.22.0's ThinkingLevel.LOW.

Its thinking tokens and rupees fall on the lane, while LangChain's stay the same. What remains of the gap is the declarations, both printed as Gemini receives them, and ADK's own instruction line.

OWN_PY prints LangChain's setting from shared/profile.build_llm() (thinking_level='low') and ADK's from runner.app.root_agent.generate_content_config. - **Reuses:** commands/tests/test_desk_rules.py and desk_rules.py's rule that a pattern change bumps RULES_VERSION. The misses the register verified (L10.5-W04), re-checked against today's kit: gate() returns None for both example sentences.
- **Kit:**
  - No deploy/ change. lessons/10-agents/10.5-case-desk/build.py: the Change-it diff to shared/desk_rules.py adds one alternative to the right class's pattern and bumps RULES_VERSION. It is applied to a temp copy; gate() runs on the chosen sentence before and after; commands/tests/test_desk_rules.py runs on the copy.
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.5**, Level 2 'The kit's code', step 5, after 'Do it: the gate on your machine': new 'Change it: a disclosure the lexicon misses' block: Runs the offline gate cell on a sentence today's lexicon misses; the build picks one it has verified, such as 'Can I speak to someone in HR?' or 'A senior colleague keeps asking me out even after I refused.'. Applies the diff, re-runs the cell, runs python -m unittest commands/tests/test_desk_rules.py, then runs the restore line. *The learner sees:* None, then the class and the fixed reply (model none, cost 0). The rules tests stay green, or a near miss now fires, which the page reads as the precision a wider pattern costs. RULES_VERSION changes, as every desk_gate log row would show. 'What just happened' ties this to the box's 'reads words, not meaning', and to recall, which only rows written by people can measure.

  - No deploy/ change beyond B1's DESK mode. lessons/10-agents/10.6-desk-router/build.py: verbatim excerpts, via block(), of desk_agent.system_prompt(), desk_agent._calc(), desk_calc.check_args(), desk_router.prompt(), PROMPT_EXEMPLARS and knn_vote(). Runs make agent-turn DESK=handbook Q='I am an E3 leaving with 50 days of earned leave. How much is encashed?', then a question that omits the balance ('I am an E3 leaving ACME. How many days of earned leave are encashed?'). Applies a diff that deletes the prompt's rule 'Give a calculator only numbers and dates written in the person's question or in a passage...'. With the rule gone, any balance the model brings of its own is refused by check_args ('balance = ... is a fact about the person that their message does not state: ask them for it') as an error result, and the model then asks for it. The offline scripted run refuses an invented balance both with and without the rule.

  - No deploy/ change. lessons/11-memory/11.1-state-history/build.py: the Change-it diff to brains.SYSTEM removes 'Search for every document question, a follow-up too, and' and keeps the citation rule. It is applied to a temp copy, and B1's OFFLINE bench with REPEAT checks the structure.

Q='And if they are still on probation?' REPEAT=3 SHOW=system. Applies the diff, re-runs, then runs the restore line. *The learner sees:* SYSTEM before and after, and, for the follow-up, the k of 3 runs that called retrieve. With the sentence the follow-up searches again; without it, it answers from history more often. This is shown as a lane sample, with the rupees the bench printed. 'What just happened' ties it to step 3's stale copy: a prompt is a probabilistic control, and clearing earlier evidence (G7) is the enforced one. SYSTEM is configuration, not memory.

Stale knowledge itself is H8's lantern reindex step (version 1 -> 2, then the follow-up) and L12; B9 measures only the re-search rate. (after review: B9 issues: make Q= repeat runs only the last question; n=3; stale-knowledge half belongs to H8/L12.)

- **Reuses:** shared/desk_calc.py, which is stdlib only and is copied into the MCP image with shared/. 12.1 step 4's local server. Gaps plan G4's hints (section 4.6), with fastmcp 3.4.7's annotations confirmed in its section 5.

  - No deploy/ change. lessons/12-protocols/12.1-mcp-tools/build.py: the Change-it diff to services/mcp/server.py is applied to a temp copy, and the EXPOSE, DISCOVER and INVOKE cells re-run on it. Excerpts of _audit and of list_documents show the members-only pattern.
  - The learner's diff: @mcp.tool(annotations={'readOnlyHint': True}) def notice_end(ack_date: str, days: int) -> dict. Its first line is _tenant_for(_caller(), None), so only roster members may call it; then desk_calc.notice_end, _audit on success, and CalcError raised as ToolError.
- **Lessons:**
  - **12.1**, Level 2 'Expose', step 3: new 'Change it: a tool of your own' block: Applies the diff and re-runs step 3's cell. *The learner sees:* Five tools. The docstring's first line is notice_end's description, its Args lines are the argument descriptions, and the read-only hint is listed.

Runs the restore line at the end of step 6; 12.2 applies the diff again. *The learner sees:* tools/list names five. desk_calc's date comes back. 'not authenticated' comes from the tool's own identity line. An mcp_call line names notice_end. 'What just happened' ties it to 10.1: one function is a LangChain tool there and an MCP tool here.

- **Risks:** If G4-A lands first, the tool needs a TOOL_POLICY row and the diff is rebased. If G5's tool pins land, the peer filters out the unpinned tool in 12.3. desk_calc imports shared/cases, whose Firestore import is lazy; the build confirms the import adds nothing.
