## D. Human approval and durable execution

**Goal.** Give the agents one real, reversible side-effecting action, withdrawing a document, that runs only after a second person in the same tenant approves it. This lands the author's G2 design (gaps plan §4.3) on today's kit, with the departures stated in D1 and D2. The pause must survive a redeploy and a failed turn. An agent must also be able to hand a question the rules missed to the person the law names. The three fictional BLOCKED names and the refuse node that only fires with a scripted model are removed. Lessons 10.2, 10.3, 10.4, 10.5 and 11.3 prove each behaviour on the learner's lane, not only offline.

### D1. Kit: a real side-effecting tool behind a second person's approval (withdraw_document), replacing the BLOCKED names

- **Effort:** L · **Kind:** kit · **First phase:** P2
- **Reuses:** Gaps plan §4.3 (G2): the target state, the kit-changes table (shared/approvals.py, shared/documind_actions.py, tools.py and brains.py, agent.py, frontend/chat.py, smoke_approval.py, the record's statuses pending to failed), §3's middleware order and chat-requirements rule, §5's API rows (HumanInTheLoopMiddleware, interrupt and Command(resume), require_confirmation, a new message dropping an interrupt), and decisions 2, 3 and 5. Per decision 3 the three fictional names are not built: a delete is irreversible, and email or access changes would need systems the kit lacks. Desk plan §3.1's G2-a shared slice is already in the kit (AUDIT_BUCKET in commands/deploy-chat.sh, storage.tf's chat_audit, the google-cloud-storage and langgraph pins) and is not redone. Departures from G2: (1) the approver is a doc_approver role in shared/roles.py, not a second tenants/{t}/approvers list, because the Desk later landed one role store with its own reader, writer and audit events; (2) the second person is the Desk's documind-evalacme-sa, already an invoker on documind-chat, not a new documind-approver-sa, so there is no new Terraform account, no invoker change (10.1 asserts eight) and no operators-loop change (the Desk plan §3.2 found that would break 12.2's assert); (3) the record follows cases.py's transaction and outbox, so an approval event is never lost; (4) the action tools are offered per tenant behind agent_actions, off by default, following CLAUDE.md's rule for new switches and the Desk's byte-for-byte rule; (5) the offline gate lives in the published kit's commands/tests, not tools/check_approvals.py, so pages cite a test learners can run (L10.1-W12, L12.2-W08). D1 lands in one pull request with D2 and D3, as the gaps plan's G2-a did.
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/services/chat/tools.py: BLOCKED (tools.py:65) is removed. NEEDS_APPROVAL = {'withdraw_document'} takes its place. ACTIONS = [withdraw_document] is an @tool adapter: the tenant comes from the ToolRuntime and the approval id from the thread and call ids, with a 30 s entry in TIMEOUTS. TOOLS stays [retrieve, calculate_processing_cost] (tools.py:191), so 10.1's printed contract and 10.4's declarations do not move.
  - deploy/shared/documind_actions.py (new, gaps plan §4.3), two functions. normalize_name accepts a bare name, tenant/name or the upload URI, and refuses '/', '..' and anything over 200 characters. withdraw_document applies the last lock (the record is approved, and the tenant and name match), then calls approvals.queue() and starts D2's job. It returns the undo command, make restore SOURCE=... The module stays apart from documind_tools.py so the MCP server's imports do not change.
  - deploy/shared/approvals.py (new), laid out like shared/cases.py. Each record is approvals/{approval_id} and carries its tenant. approval_id is the first 32 characters of the sha256 of the thread id and call id, so opening the same call twice gives the same record. open_request() uses create() and refuses more than APPROVAL_MAX_OPEN=3 pending requests per requester. Also held() and list_for(). decide() runs in one transaction: 403 for a self-approval or a caller without the doc_approver role, 404 for another tenant's or an unknown id, 409 when already decided, 410 when expired. Also cancel(), mark_lost() and queue(). expire_at comes from APPROVAL_TTL_H=24 and is checked in code. Each event is written into the record in the same transaction, then flushed to the bucket as cases.py's audit_pending and _flush do. On the local profile the records live in a JSON file beside DOCUMIND_THREADS_DB.
  - deploy/shared/roles.py: 'doc_approver' is added to ROLES. It opens no desk.
  - deploy/commands/lane.py approver, plus commands/tests/test_lane.py's pinned set: grants doc_approver without removing the person's other roles. It refuses a non-member and is audited as role.grant. For a documind-*-sa account it also grants ADMIN_EMAILS the token-creator binding that make desk-operators grants, so lesson 10.2 needs no Desk target.
  - deploy/shared/audit_log.py: approval.request, approval.grant, approval.deny, approval.cancel and doc.withdraw go on a new line after the Desk's. The actor is the person who acted. The target is the approval reference, the tool and the document, never the text the person asked.
  - deploy/services/chat/brains.py, LangChainBrain: a second compiled agent, used only for a tenant whose agent_actions switch is 'approval'. It has HumanInTheLoopMiddleware first (interrupt_on withdraw_document, allowed_decisions approve and reject) and binds TOOLS + ACTIONS. A switch-off tenant keeps today's agent and checkpoint counts, because an after_model hook adds graph nodes (gaps plan §5).
  - deploy/services/chat/brains.py, LangGraphBrain: the refuse node becomes approve. route sends a turn with a gated call there, and interrupt() is called before any side effect. A reject becomes an error ToolMessage for that call only, and an allowed retrieve beside it still runs. ToolNode becomes ToolNode(TOOLS + ACTIONS, wrap_tool_call=limits.timed_tool_call), and the model is bound on each call to that turn's toolset. Both brains gain resume() and paused().
  - deploy/services/chat/brains.py, AdkBrain: _adk_withdraw under FunctionTool(require_confirmation=True), added only when the session service is not in memory (gaps decision 4). Its result gets its own status, so mark_error does not count it as a refusal (gaps §4.1 risk).
  - deploy/services/chat/agent.py, the turn: the context carries tenant_settings/{t}.agent_actions, off by default and read through desk.settings() once a minute. A paused turn opens its record (the question up to 500 characters, the model's reason labelled as the model's, the turn's sources), writes approval.request and answers 200 with status pending_approval and the id. A new message on a paused thread gets 409, because a new message drops the interrupt (gaps §5). The chat row names status and approval_id.
  - deploy/services/chat/agent.py, the routes: /v1/approvals (list, one, approve, reject, cancel) is installed with one line, as desk.install() is. Ids must match ^[0-9a-f]{32}$, reads are point reads on the record's tenant, and a decision resumes the turn with the brain the record names. The lines 10.1 to 11.3 quote (ChatRequest, caller(), thread_config(), the out = brain.answer( call) stay byte for byte where possible.
  - deploy/services/frontend/chat.py, the agent branch and the sidebar only: a waiting card shows the question, the model's reason, the document and the sources. It polls GET /v1/approvals/{id} for the status and gives the requester a Cancel button. A doc_approver gets an Approvals inbox with Approve, Reject and a reason field.
  - deploy/commands/lane.py actions and deploy/mk/agents.mk: make actions TENANT= ACTIONS=approval|off (a merge write to tenant_settings), make approver TENANT= EMAIL=, make approvals TENANT= and make smoke-approval. smoke-approval stays out of smoke-all (gaps decision 2). Also the Makefile's .PHONY and the mk/README.md row.
  - deploy/terraform/firestore_indexes.tf: a TTL on approvals.expire_at, and the composite index list_for needs (tenant, status, requested_at), written in desk.tf's style.
  - deploy/smoke/smoke_approval.py (new): a new session each run. For each brain in BRAINS (langchain, langgraph) it checks the pause, the 409 hold, the 403 self-approval and a reject that leaves the source indexed (read from rag-api's /v1/sources). Then approve, executed, the smoke-lantern question unanswerable, make restore, and the outsider's 403. The requester is documind-ui-sa; the approver is documind-evalacme-sa (DOCUMIND_APPROVER_SA).
  - deploy/commands/tests/test_approvals.py (new), added to the chat-pins step of .github/workflows/checks.yml and deploy/.github/workflows/documind-dryrun.yml. It covers the gaps plan's check_approvals list: the HITL pause, approve and reject; the approve node and a reject beside a retrieve; a resume from a second graph sharing the saver; ADK confirmation with a scripted model, and its exclusion while sessions are in memory; decide()'s 403, 404, 409 and 410; lost; the last lock; normalize_name; the open cap; and switch off meaning no action tool is offered. The BLOCKED cases in commands/tests/test_chat_brains.py move to NEEDS_APPROVAL.
  - Mechanical rebuilds in the same pull request, as pagekit/check_lesson.py names them. 10.3 and 10.4: their BLOCKED and delete_document asserts (10.3's scripted delete_document becomes an unknown tool until D4). 10.5: the roles.py excerpt. 10.1 and 11.1 to 11.3: wherever agent.py or brains.py lines they quote move. 8.3: its emitters assert. Then tools/kit_index.py, tools/build_workshop_demos.py, and deploy/UNOWNED.md rows for the new files.
- **Proof:** Offline: python -m unittest commands/tests/test_approvals.py -v and commands/tests/test_chat_brains.py pass in the chat pins (CI's chat-pins step, DOCUMIND_REQUIRE_LIBS=1). python tools/kit_index.py --check and python tools/publish_learner.py --check pass, and check_lesson.py and audit_pages.py pass for every rebuilt lesson. On a lane with acme's switch off: make smoke-chat is unchanged, and no turn's tool_calls names withdraw_document. After make actions TENANT=acme ACTIONS=approval and make approver for documind-evalacme-sa: make smoke-approval passes on langchain and langgraph (pause, 409, 403, reject, approve, executed, unanswerable, restored, outsider 403).
- **Risks:** A paused thread that gets a new message loses its interrupt (gaps §5), so the hold, the lost status and the expiry carry the design. LangChain and LangGraph share one checkpointer and one thread id, so the turn resumes in the brain the record names, never the sidebar's current choice. chat-sa's project-wide datastore.user (terraform/sa.tf) can write roles and records, so the job's re-check catches bugs, not a compromised chat service. ADK's ToolConfirmation is experimental in google-adk 2.8.0. The approver's assertion goes out on the resumed turn's retrieves, so usage rows name the approver. A real model may not name the right document; normalize_name and a smoke question that names the file keep the smoke reliable. Every quoted chat.py and brains.py line that moves forces a rebuild in this pull request, and G3's trace middleware must later go ahead of HITL in the list.
- **Decision:** (a) Approver store: a doc_approver role in shared/roles.py (recommended), or the gaps plan's separate tenants/{t}/approvers list. (b) Second person: documind-evalacme-sa (recommended), or a new documind-approver-sa with its own token-creator loop. (c) Actor in approval audit events: the acting person's email, as role.grant does (recommended, since who approved is the point of the record), or a reference as case events use, because the bucket refuses deletes.
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: (1) Before building, run require_confirmation with DatabaseSessionService on google-adk 2.8.0; if unsupported (adk.dev/tools-custom/confirmation says so), gate ADK through the kit's approvals record in before_tool_callback or drop the ADK pause and say why; add AdkBrain(actions=True) as the explicit test switch for offline. (2) Restore G2's rules: reject extra gated calls in one message and non-current/missing/already-withdrawn sources in place, before any record opens; tests for both. (3) Send only pending allowed calls to tools after approve; refuse gated names in route() when the tenant's switch is off; tests. (4) decide() clears expire_at when a record leaves pending (TTL removes undecided requests only); make approvals flushes leftover audit events; tests. (5) The resumed turn's status, refusals and answer are stored on the record, readable by the requester only; each resume logs a chat row with its own Meter. (6) Four-eyes live proof: documind-evalacme-sa requests and tries to approve its own request; assert the 403's detail text. (7) No lane.py subcommands: roles via desk_ops roles --add (merge) or make roles ROLES=employee,desk_eval,doc_approver; the switch via desk_ops desk; token grant via make desk-operators. (8) A failed agent_actions read counts as off; holds checked only when the switch is on or a record exists; local profile env switch; pending records marked lost when the switch turns off; resume uses the approval graph regardless. (9) Rebuilds: grep every build.py for changed paths and rerun all (13.2's CHAT_ROW guard, 10.1's 'blocked name' prose); hold the learner publish until D4/D5 merge or rewrite 10.3/10.4 BLOCKED paragraphs in D1's PR; cite Desk plan section 3.4 (not 3.2). (after review: D1 issues: ADK confirmation with durable stores; dropped G2 rules; ToolNode runs all calls; TTL deletes decided records; resume answer unreachable; ambiguous 403; lane.py pinned subcommands (verified test_lane.py:25-26); byte-for-byte rule; rebuild coverage.)
- **Revised:** effort revised to L after review (D7 issue: two rebuild rounds.)
- **Also:** (final review) ADK's confirmation is based on the run D1 plans (require_confirmation with DatabaseSessionService after PR37). Pages do not say it is unsupported on database sessions unless that run shows it; the real caveat is ToolConfirmation's @experimental (tools/tool_confirmation.py:28).
- **Closes:** GAP-G04, L10.2-W03, L10.2-W05, L10.2-W10, L10.3-W02, L10.4-W06, L10.4-S08, L10.5-W02, PLAN-G2, BENCH-T11_hitl_approval_interrupts, BENCH-T12_durable_execution, BENCH-T30_governance_audit_compliance

### D2. Kit: the withdrawal runs as a fixed-argument job that re-checks the approval (documind-withdraw), with make restore as the undo

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **First phase:** P2 · **After:** D1
- **Reuses:** Gaps plan §4.3: the services/ingest/reconcile.py row (--approved, the re-check, doc.withdraw only on success), the reconcile.tf and lesson-12.8.sh rows, the empty-body Jobs API :run pattern of services/ingest/main.py:266-276, and decision 5 (make withdraw-run; make retire writes no doc.withdraw). Departure: the tombstone is reached by calling the --retire branch rather than extracting it into withdraw_source(), so 4.3, 4.4 and 15.4 need no rebuild. terraform/desk.tf's cases_overdue job and invoker are the Terraform template.
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/services/ingest/reconcile.py: --approved claims a queued record, moving it from queued to executing in a Firestore transaction so a doubled start runs once. It re-checks the doc_approver role and the four-eyes rule and builds the URI from the record only. It then runs the --retire branch's tombstone by calling it unchanged, so the lines lessons 4.3 and 15.4 quote stay put. It writes executed with a doc.withdraw event, or failed. --selftest covers the claim.
  - deploy/terraform/reconcile.tf: job documind-withdraw under local.reconcile (the ingest image and account, args --approved --apply, max_retries 0), and roles/run.invoker on that job for documind-chat-sa only, in the style of terraform/desk.tf's cases_overdue_invoker.
  - deploy/commands/deploy-chat.sh: WITHDRAW_JOB and APPROVAL_TTL_H join the chat service's env line, where AUDIT_BUCKET already is. The Makefile's deploy-services passes WITHDRAW_JOB when RECONCILE_JOB=true.
  - deploy/mk/agents.mk: make withdraw-run starts the job by hand after a failed start (gaps decision 5).
  - lessons/08-security/8.3-dlp-guard-audit/build.py and its page: the emitters assert gains doc.withdraw, and the claim 'a withdrawal writes no event' is narrowed to make retire from the shell (gaps decision 5). Check tools/check_lifecycle.py too.
- **Lessons:**
  - **8.3**, Level 5 · Advanced, the audit paragraph and its build assert: Reads which actions write audit events *The learner sees:* make retire from the shell writes no event; a withdrawal a second person approved writes doc.withdraw only after the tombstone succeeds
- **Proof:** As before, plus the 15.3, 15.4 and 8.3 build asserts (two mirror.X( calls, no logging config, no 'audit' in 8.3) pass; restore proven by the worker's ingest_reactivated line.
- **Risks:** That run.invoker on a job allows an empty-body :run is unverified (gaps §5); the first lane run settles it. The withdrawal is asynchronous: the answer says it has started, and the record's status is the truth. RECONCILE_JOB=true must stay on every later make plan, or the plan removes both jobs. A failed job leaves the record failed and the document indexed; make withdraw-run retries it.
- **Decision:** Call the --retire branch (recommended: no rebuild of 4.3, 4.4 or 15.4), or extract withdraw_source() as the gaps plan designed (cleaner code, but 4.3 and 15.4 must be rebuilt).
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: (1) The --retire branch is re-entered through main() with a rebuilt sys.argv (lane.py's _reconcile pattern), success read from the exit code. (2) Keep --selftest's printed line unchanged (4.4 prints it via %%SELFTEST%%) or rebuild 4.4. (3) WITHDRAW_JOB carries the full job resource name (projects/.../locations/REGION/jobs/documind-withdraw) passed by make deploy-services, so 10.1's 14-count assert holds. (4) Proof uses the worker's ingest_reactivated log line or make sources showing the source indexed (make restore prints reconcile_restored). (after review: D2 issues: inline branch, selftest output, REGION, restore output.)
- **Revised:** proof revised after review (Build asserts check_lesson never runs.)
- **Also:** (final review) The unverified-mechanism risk is dropped: the kit already relies on run.invoker allowing a job :run (terraform/batch.tf:84-89, services/ingest/main.py:266-276, terraform/desk.tf:117-121). The first lane run of documind-withdraw confirms it.
- **Closes:** GAP-G04, PLAN-G2, BENCH-T12_durable_execution

### D3. Lesson 10.2: the main LangGraph workflow with a real approval node (pause, a second person, resume from Cloud SQL)

- **Effort:** L · **Kind:** lesson · **First phase:** P2 · **After:** D1, D2
- **Reuses:** Gaps plan §4.3's 10.2 scene row (what the learner does, code and targets, proof), its lane verification steps 1 to 7, and decision 15's proof text. Departures: a UI level opens the lesson (CLAUDE.md's level order; PRES-10). The Change-it step makes G2's sentence about interrupt() coming before any side effect hands-on. The paused checkpoint is read in Cloud SQL. The audit trail G2 placed in an 8.3 scene (G2-d) is read here instead.
- **Kit:**
  - No change in deploy/ beyond D1 and D2. The pull request carries lessons/10-agents/10.2-langgraph (parts and build.py), deploy/workshop_demos/module_10/lesson_10_2 regenerated by tools/build_workshop_demos.py, and the 10.2 rows of course-manifest.json, the course plan and the roadmap. The new manifest proof follows gaps decision 15: 'a withdrawal pauses for a second person, resumes from Cloud SQL and is executed; make restore brings it back'.
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.2**, Level 0 · Theory: the analogy and the 'Step through the graph' interactive: Steps the kit's real graph (agent, route, approve, tools) through five turns the build computes with the kit's LangGraphBrain: a withdrawal that pauses, is approved, is rejected, expires, and one asked beside a retrieve *The learner sees:* The turn stops at approve with a pending record, then goes on to tools after an approval, or gets an error result for that call alone after a reject, while the retrieve beside it runs. In the analogy, the file now waits in the competent authority's tray, and the officer who wrote the noting cannot sign it.
  - **10.2**, Level 1 · Definitions, and the shared setup: Reads the new words: interrupt(), Command(resume), pending_approval, NEEDS_APPROVAL, doc_approver, four eyes, hold, lost, expired and the withdraw job. Then runs one setup window: make roster for documind-evalacme-sa, make approver TENANT=acme for it and for themselves, make actions TENANT=acme ACTIONS=approval, and make smoke-reindex if acme/smoke_note.md is missing *The learner sees:* Both grants printed, acme's agent_actions set to approval, and smoke_note.md indexed
  - **10.2**, New Level 2 · In the UI: a withdrawal that waits: In the Chat page, with Brain set to langgraph, asks 'Withdraw smoke_note.md from acme's documents'. Then approves, from the sidebar inbox and with a reason, a withdrawal that documind-evalacme-sa requested in a cell *The learner sees:* A waiting card with the question, the model's reason labelled as the model's, the document and the sources, and no Approve button for the person who asked. Then the inbox item moves to executed.
  - **10.2**, Level 2 · The graph, in a venv of its own (offline): Builds ~/graph-venv from services/chat/requirements.txt, so langgraph 1.2.12 is pinned. Lists the kit's graph. Runs the scripted withdrawal, streamed with stream_mode='updates' (explained: one update for each node that runs, and __interrupt__ when the turn pauses). Resumes it from a second graph object sharing the saver. Runs python -m unittest commands/tests/test_approvals.py -k langgraph *The learner sees:* agent -> approve, then __interrupt__ with the action request. After Command(resume): approve -> tools -> agent, with the stand-in withdrawal called once. A reject beside a retrieve gives refusals ['withdraw_document'] while the retrieve ran. The tests report OK.
  - **10.2**, Level 2, Change it (inside the same level): Moves the approve node's 'approval asked' log line above interrupt() in their local services/chat/brains.py, re-runs the same cell, then restores the file with git checkout services/chat/brains.py *The learner sees:* The line printed twice, once at the pause and once at the resume, because a resumed node reruns from its first line, so an email or a ticket sent there would go out twice. The page then shows that the kit opens the record outside the graph, under an id made from the thread and the call, so a second open returns the same record (an idempotency key).
  - **10.2**, Level 3 · On your lane (REST): As documind-ui-sa, asks the langgraph brain to withdraw smoke_note.md in session lesson102, sends a second question on that session, and tries to approve its own request. As documind-evalacme-sa, lists the inbox and approves with a reason. Then asks the smoke-lantern question and runs make restore *The learner sees:* status pending_approval with a 32-character id; 409 for the second question; 403 for the self-approval; queued, then executed, with withdrawal_started and the undo command; 'not in the documents' for the lantern question; ingest_reactivated, and the answer back
  - **10.2**, Level 4 · The checkpointer (direct reads): Before the approval, reads the graph state and Cloud SQL for the paused thread. After it, reads the Firestore record, make sources TENANT_ONLY=acme and the audit bucket's listing *The learner sees:* get_state().next naming approve, with one interrupt; a checkpoint_writes row with channel __interrupt__ for thread acme:...:lesson102, so the pause lives in Cloud SQL, not in the instance; the record's statuses, approver and expire_at; smoke_note.md withdrawn, then indexed; and the approval.request, approval.grant and doc.withdraw objects
  - **10.2**, Level 5 · Advanced, Verify it yourself, What changed on your lane: Reads why interrupt() comes before side effects, why 'edit' is not offered for a four-eyes action (an edit would make the approver the author of a new action), the bill and the rewritten gap box. Ticks the checklist and switches acme's agent_actions off *The learner sees:* The resume's model call, estimated in the gaps plan at about Rs 0.48 at flash (3,000 tokens in, 150 out, USD_INR 85), with the figure make usage reads on the rehearsal lane. The gap box: ADK offers no action until its sessions are durable, the withdrawal is asynchronous, chat-sa holds datastore.user, and nothing pushes a request to approvers. Checklist rows for the pause, the 409, the 403, executed, unanswerable then restored, the interrupt row, the audit events and the Change-it's doubled line.
- **Proof:** python pagekit/check_lesson.py 10.2 and python pagekit/audit_pages.py 10.2 pass. build.py runs the kit's LangGraphBrain and asserts the node paths the page and widget print: agent, approve and paused; then approve, tools, agent; and the reject beside a retrieve. tools/build_workshop_demos.py --check and tools/check_workshop_demos.py pass. One rehearsal on a live lane is captured as the page's labelled sample: pending_approval, 409, 403, executed, the lantern question unanswerable, ingest_reactivated.
- **Risks:** 10.2 may run past 90 minutes. The refuse-node step and the delete half of step 5 are replaced, not kept beside the new steps; if the lesson still runs long, the fallback is gaps decision 1's reserved split (the approval scene as its own lesson, with Desk decision 19 renumbering). The model may phrase the request differently or skip the tool; the question names the file. Other workstreams also change 10.2 (LangGraph depth, authoring, the memory step's fragile follow-up L10.2-W06, the setup trim), so land them as one 10.2 pull-request sequence with this one first. Lesson 10.5's make roles line sets the learner's roles exactly, so the learner's doc_approver ends there; later steps use documind-evalacme-sa. A lane planned before the Desk's Terraform has no eval accounts until make plan up; the setup window checks for them.
- **Decision:** Keep the title 'Implement the main LangGraph workflow' (recommended: the workflow is now the production one, and the learner edits its approval node) or retitle it. Take gaps decision 1's split if the rehearsal runs past 90 minutes.
- **Revised:** lesson_changes: Amend: (1) Setup adds make plan up RECONCILE_JOB=true and make deploy-services RECONCILE_JOB=true with a check that documind-withdraw exists. (2) L2 rejects (or cancels) the learner's own request and runs make restore before L3. (3) L4 reads 'paused' from GET /v1/approvals/{id} and checkpoint_writes through the Cloud SQL connector (pg8000, H9's setup brought forward); get_state() stays offline. (4) Cost: the resumed turn's cost_usd from its chat row (D1's resume row); the Rs 0.48 estimate is labelled an assumption or dropped. (5) Add test_chat_brains.py to step 2's offline run and checklist item 1 checks langgraph. (6) Replace the 'Refused whole' paragraph with why a gated call waits on its own. (after review: D3 issues: job infrastructure, step collisions, get_state needs psycopg, cost source, missing promised steps.)
- **Closes:** GAP-G04, PRES-04, PRES-06, L10.2-W01, L10.2-W03, L10.2-W04, L10.2-W05, L10.2-W07, L10.2-W10, L10.2-W11, L10.2-W13, L10.2-S04, L10.5-W02, L10.5-S07, PLAN-G2, BENCH-T11_hitl_approval_interrupts, BENCH-T12_durable_execution, BENCH-T22_streaming_agent_ux

### D4. Lesson 10.3: where each failure of a gated call surfaces (reject, 403, 404, 409, 410, lost), replacing the fictional blocked name and the repeated Module 8 door

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** lesson · **First phase:** P2 · **After:** D1, D2, D3
- **Reuses:** Gaps plan §4.3's 10.3 scene row (the reject, the self-approval and non-approver 403s, the 404 across tenants, 409, 410, lost, the last lock and a name outside the tenant) and decision 15's 10.3 proof. Departures: the gated call's access failures replace step 5's 401 and 403 door, which repeats 8.1 and 8.2 (L10.3-W03), and the lesson gains a UI step (L10.3-W08).
- **Kit:**
  - No change in deploy/ beyond D1 and D2. The cells run make roster and make approver for documind-evalzeta-sa in zeta. The pull request carries lessons/10-agents/10.3-tool-failures, its demos, and the 10.3 rows of the manifest, the course plan and the roadmap. The new manifest proof follows gaps decision 15: refusals non-empty after a rejected withdrawal, a self-approval refused with 403, and a timed-out tool reported.
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.3**, Level 0 · Theory: the 'Where a failure surfaces' panel: Chooses each failure of a gated call *The learner sees:* For a reject, a self-approval, a non-approver, another tenant's approver, an already-decided, an expired and a lost approval: the layer that answers (decide(), the brain or the job), the status, what the requester and the model are told, and the row. The build computes these from shared/approvals.py and the brains. The blocked-name row is gone.
  - **10.3**, Level 2 · Five failures, step 3: Runs the same offline cell; its first failure is now a withdrawal an approver rejects *The learner sees:* An error result for withdraw_document carrying the approver's reason, labelled as the approver's; refusals ['withdraw_document']; the other four failures unchanged
  - **10.3**, New UI step, before step 3: In the Chat page, watches a withdrawal of smoke_note.md that documind-evalacme-sa rejects in a cell *The learner sees:* The waiting card turns to rejected, with the reason. The caption shows 'refused: withdraw_document', and the real model's resumed answer says nothing was withdrawn.
  - **10.3**, Level 3, step 5 becomes 'Access failures of a gated call' (the outsider and no-email door shrinks to a two-line pointer to 8.2): On the lane: documind-ui-sa approves its own request; documind-evalzeta-sa, a doc_approver in zeta, approves acme's request; documind-evalacme-sa approves one that is already decided *The learner sees:* 403 for the self-approval, 404 for the other tenant's approver, 409 for the decided approval
  - **10.3**, Level 3, step 5 offline, and Change it: Runs test_approvals.py -k decide for the expired, lost, outside-the-tenant and last-lock cases. Then sets APPROVAL_TTL_H to 0.0003 in the cell and re-runs it *The learner sees:* 410 after about a second; 409 for a lost turn; a name outside the tenant refused before any record exists; the job refusing a record that is not approved
  - **10.3**, Level 4 · Diagnosis, Level 5, Verify it yourself: Reads the diagnosis table's new approvals row and ticks the checklist *The learner sees:* The approval record's status is the truth for a gated call; a checklist row for each status
- **Proof:** check_lesson.py 10.3 and audit_pages.py 10.3 pass. build.py asserts each panel row against decide() and the brains. A live rehearsal captures the 403, 404 and 409 and the rejected turn's answer. test_approvals.py -k decide passes.
- **Risks:** Dropping the door step loses the 401-versus-403 contrast on this page; the pointer to 8.2 keeps it one click away. The rejected turn's answer comes from a real model and varies, so the checklist checks refusals and statuses, not wording. The approver's reason reaches the model, so it is capped at 200 characters and labelled as the approver's.
- **Decision:** Replace step 5's door with the gated-call access failures (recommended), or keep both.
- **Revised:** lesson_changes: Setup starts with make actions TENANT=acme ACTIONS=approval and a check that documind-evalacme-sa still holds doc_approver; the lesson ends by switching acme off. (after review: D4 issue: 10.2 ends with acme off.)
- **Closes:** GAP-G04, L10.3-W02, L10.3-W03, L10.3-W08, PLAN-G2, BENCH-T11_hitl_approval_interrupts

### D5. Lesson 10.4: three ways to pause for a person (LangChain's HITL middleware, LangGraph's interrupt node, ADK's tool confirmation) mapped to one record, and why ADK holds back on this lane

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** lesson · **First phase:** P2 · **After:** D1, memory workstream: durable ADK sessions (PLAN-GD4), for the ADK lane path only
- **Reuses:** Gaps plan §4.3's 10.4 scene row (HumanInTheLoopMiddleware's interrupt beside FunctionTool(require_confirmation=True), mapped to one record shape, with the ADK brain declining on the lane) and §5's rows for both APIs. Addition: LangGraph's approve node is a third column, so the LangGraph brain is compared on its own (L10.4-S04).
- **Kit:**
  - No change in deploy/ beyond D1 (AdkBrain's durable-session check and _adk_withdraw are D1's). The pull request carries lessons/10-agents/10.4-adapters and its demos.
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.4**, Level 2 · Two adapters over one tool, step 3 ('Four calls that go wrong'): Runs the offline cell, whose first call is now a withdrawal that needs approval, through the LangChain, LangGraph and ADK brains with scripted models *The learner sees:* LangChain's __interrupt__ with an action request (from HumanInTheLoopMiddleware), LangGraph's __interrupt__ from the approve node, and ADK's adk_request_confirmation event (from FunctionTool(require_confirmation=True)). The kit turns each into the same record shape (tool, arguments, question, call id), and ADK's guard path for a gated tool is finally reachable.
  - **10.4**, Level 2, Change it: Flips require_confirmation to False in the local brains.py the cell imports, re-runs, then restores the file *The learner sees:* The ADK tool runs with no confirmation: the gate is one argument, which is why the kit's test pins it
  - **10.4**, Level 3 · Four brains on /health: With acme's switch on, asks the adk brain on the lane to withdraw smoke_note.md *The learner sees:* No withdraw_document among its tools and a refusal in words. The page gives the reason: ADK's sessions live in each gunicorn worker's memory (two workers, min instances 0), so a decision could reach a worker that holds no paused session.
  - **10.4**, Level 5 · Advanced: Reads the new comparison row and the updated gap box *The learner sees:* Pausing for a person needs a durable checkpoint or session: LangChain and LangGraph have one on this lane, and ADK does not yet. The gap box's 'ADK's guard cannot fire yet' is replaced by 'ADK offers no action until its sessions are durable'.
- **Proof:** check_lesson.py 10.4 and audit_pages.py 10.4 pass. build.py runs the three brains offline and asserts the three pause signals and the one record shape. The lane rehearsal shows the adk brain without the tool.
- **Risks:** ADK's ToolConfirmation is experimental in google-adk 2.8.0 and may change. The Change-it step weakens a security setting; the cell restores the file and the test pins it.
- **Decision:** Once durable ADK sessions land (gaps decision 4, or Agent Engine sessions, GAP-G09), whether ADK offers the action on the lane and 10.4 gains a live ADK pause.
- **Revised:** lesson_changes: Offline cell uses D1's AdkBrain(actions=True) switch. L3/L5 say only what is verified: ADK's tool confirmation is not supported on DatabaseSessionService/VertexAiSessionService per ADK docs unless D1's run shows otherwise; the decision depends on that verified run. LangGraph's column also shows its own limits (written by hand), checkpoint count and cost line. (after review: D5 issue and L10.4-S04/S08 notes.)
- **Also:** (final review) Once PR37 makes ADK sessions durable, 10.4's 'ADK offers no action' box is no longer true: PR37 carries the live ADK pause or rewords the box (decision 16). ADK's confirmation caveat is @experimental, not the session service.
- **Closes:** GAP-G04, L10.4-W06, L10.4-W07, L10.4-S04, L10.4-S08, L10.5-S07, PLAN-G2, BENCH-T11_hitl_approval_interrupts

### D6. Lesson 11.3: a paused approval survives the redeploy and resumes on the new revision

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **First phase:** P3 · **After:** D1, D2, D3, memory workstream: an 11.3 restart that survives the next deploy (L11.3-W06), soft
- **Reuses:** Gaps plan §4.3's lane verification steps 4 and 7 (smoke-approval, HOLD=1) and §5's unverified cross-process resume. Departure: decision 5 left an 11.3 step showing a paused approval surviving a redeploy 'optional and not planned'. This plan takes it, because the register asks for it (GAP-G04, L11.3-W04, BENCH-T12) and 11.3 already pays for the redeploy.
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/smoke/smoke_approval.py: a HOLD=1 mode that leaves one pending approval and prints its id (the gaps plan's lane step 7), which the 11.3 cell uses. Otherwise no change beyond D1 and D2. The pull request carries lessons/11-memory/11.3-restart-isolation, its demos, and the 11.3 manifest proof if the author takes it.
- **Lessons:**
  - **11.3**, Level 3 · A conversation across a redeploy (step 4): Before the redeploy, with acme's switch on, asks the langgraph brain to withdraw smoke_note.md (or runs make smoke-approval HOLD=1) and saves the id in ~/lesson113.json. After the serving revision changes: reads GET /v1/approvals/{id}, approves as documind-evalacme-sa, asks the lantern question, runs make restore and switches acme off *The learner sees:* paused true on the new revision; queued, then executed, run by the new revision from the Cloud SQL checkpoint the old one wrote; the lantern question unanswerable, then answerable after the restore
  - **11.3**, Level 4 · The rows behind both (step 6): Reads the paused thread's rows *The learner sees:* The __interrupt__ write dated before the redeploy and the resumed checkpoints after it, in one thread
  - **11.3**, Level 5 · Advanced, and Verify it yourself: Reads why the pause survives and ticks the new rows *The learner sees:* The store is outside the instance; ADK's pause would not survive. New checklist rows: paused true on revision B, executed on revision B.
- **Proof:** check_lesson.py 11.3 and audit_pages.py 11.3 pass. A live rehearsal with two serving revisions shows the approval opened on the first and executed on the second. The gaps plan's §5 row 'a paused turn resumes from a second process sharing the Postgres checkpointer' moves from unverified to confirmed by a run (D9).
- **Risks:** The approval must be decided within APPROVAL_TTL_H (24 h), and nothing else may be sent on that session first (the hold). A cold start after the redeploy adds latency, not failure. The step adds about one agent turn, one resume and one job run to the lesson's bill, read from make usage.
- **Decision:** Extend 11.3's manifest proof with 'a paused approval resumes after the redeploy' (recommended), or keep it as a checklist row.
- **Revised:** lesson_changes: 11.3 setup reruns the doc_approver grant for documind-evalacme-sa (10.6's make roles sets roles exactly and strips it), runs make actions TENANT=acme ACTIONS=approval and checks the withdraw job exists; cost from the chat rows' cost_usd; 10.6's expected output notes the revoke. (after review: D6 issue.)
- **Closes:** GAP-G04, PRES-04, L11.3-W04, BENCH-T12_durable_execution

### D7. Kit and 11.3: thread integrity across failures (repair a thread a failed turn left open, and allow one turn at a time per conversation)

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **First phase:** P2 · **After:** D1
- **Reuses:** limits.repair_thread and its test (commands/tests/test_chat_limits.py:516); gaps plan §5's 'update_state repairs a thread' row; shared/cases.py's token claimed with create() as the lease pattern; D1's hold for a paused thread. Not in the gaps plan.
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/services/chat/limits.py: open_calls(graph, config) beside repair_thread (limits.py:378), and repair_thread made usable at the start of a turn. A thread whose last model message has calls with no tool message, and no pending interrupt, is closed with error tool messages and STOP_ANSWER before the new question.
  - deploy/services/chat/brains.py: LangChainBrain.answer() and LangGraphBrain.answer() repair a thread left open by a failed turn (a 500 from the token mint, a killed worker) before invoking. A thread paused for approval is held instead (D1).
  - deploy/services/chat/agent.py, with a small lease helper in limits.py or a new services/chat/turns.py: one turn at a time per thread. The lease is a Firestore document chat_turns/{sha256(thread id)}, created with create(), with expire_at set to the turn deadline plus a margin. It is deleted when the turn ends and taken over once expired. A second request on a busy thread answers 409 'a turn is already running on this conversation', the same status as D1's hold. The local profile uses an in-process lock.
  - deploy/terraform/firestore_indexes.tf: a TTL on chat_turns.expire_at.
  - deploy/commands/tests/test_chat_brains.py, in the chat-pins CI step: a tool that raises mid-turn leaves an open call; the next turn repairs it and answers; two concurrent turns on one thread give one 409; a stale lease is taken over.
  - Mechanical rebuilds of the lessons whose quoted brains.py, limits.py or agent.py lines move (check_lesson names them), then tools/kit_index.py.
- **Lessons:**
  - **11.3**, Level 2 · Isolation, on the kit's own app (step 3, offline TestClient): Breaks one turn by making the stand-in token mint fail mid-tool (10.3's 500), sends the next question on the same session, then sends two requests on one session at once *The learner sees:* The 500 and the thread's open call listed; then the next turn answered after the repair, with the broken turn closed by the stop sentence; then one 200 and one 409
  - **11.3**, Level 3 (lane): Sends two requests at once to one session with curl and & *The learner sees:* One answer, one 409, and one turn on the thread
  - **11.3**, Level 5 · Advanced: Reads how the kit repairs threads *The learner sees:* limits.repair_thread named (KIT-07): the recursion backstop and a crashed turn share one repair. The gap box adds that rag-api may still bill a call a crash cut short.
- **Proof:** test_chat_brains.py passes in CI's chat pins. Before the fix lands, one real call on the rehearsal lane confirms that Gemini refuses a thread whose function call has no response, which is the defect the repair fixes (about Rs 0.01). check_lesson.py 11.3 passes. On a lane, two concurrent requests to one session give 200 and 409.
- **Risks:** The defect must be confirmed first: if langchain-google-genai or Gemini already tolerates a dangling call, the task shrinks to the lease. The lease adds two Firestore writes a turn, and a lease left by a killed instance blocks its conversation until expire_at (about two minutes). The repair tells the person their last turn did not finish. Quoted lines move again.
- **Decision:** The lease always on, as a correctness fix (recommended), or behind a switch that defaults off (CLAUDE.md's rule for new switches).
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: (1) 11.3's stand-in model raises on an unanswered function call (mirroring the Gemini 400 confirmed first); the page shows the failing turn before the repaired one. (2) A crashed turn closes with its own sentence ('This turn did not finish; nothing from it was used'), tested. (3) Lease failure policy: fail open with a logged line on a Firestore error; lease behind a switch default off unless the author takes it as a fix. (4) Kit change lands in D1's PR (one rebuild round). (5) Reuse note corrected: cases.py does get-then-set on case_tokens inside a transaction. One owner for the lease with H6 and K4 (same per-thread lease): H6. (after review: D7 issues and the H6/K4 duplicate lease.)
- **Closes:** L11.3-W04, KIT-07, BENCH-T12_durable_execution

### D8. Agent-initiated hand-off: the agent offers the person a case when the rules missed a question that belongs to a person (10.5)

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **First phase:** P5 · **After:** D1, Desk workstream: the trim of 10.5 (L10.5-W08), to make room
- **Reuses:** The chat door's case_offer and the Desk page's handling of it (services/chat/desk.py, services/frontend/desk.py), shared/cases.py's draft-and-confirm flow, shared/roles.desks, the routed Desk's own drafting (desk_graph.case_reply), and D1's per-tenant tools. New: the gaps plan has no hand-off item (its decision 1 reserved only a 10.5 split of G2).
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/services/chat/tools.py: offer_case(case_type, reason), an @tool among the per-tenant tools. case_type is a Literal of the six types in shared/desk_law.CASE_TYPES. The tool writes nothing. It checks that the person's roles open the case desk (shared/roles.desks) and that the type's queue is configured (shared/cases.queue_for and configured over tenant_settings case_queues). It appends the type to the turn's context list offers and returns what the model may tell the person. Its docstring does the steering: a complaint about how the person is treated, harassment, a request about their own data, dues unpaid after leaving, or a request for a person is never answered from documents.
  - deploy/services/chat/brains.py: offer_case is offered through D1's per-tenant mechanism when tenant_settings/{t}.agent_handoff is on (LangChain and LangGraph).
  - deploy/services/chat/agent.py: the context carries offers: []. The response carries case_offer, with the same key and shape the chat door sends (services/chat/desk.py), and the chat row names case_offered (the type only, never the reason).
  - deploy/services/frontend/chat.py: a case_offer in an agent answer shows 'Raise this as a case', which opens the Desk page with the type chosen. deploy/services/frontend/desk.py accepts an offered type from an agent answer as it does a gate hit's: the POSH card at once, or the empty form.
  - deploy/commands/lane.py actions gains HANDOFF=on|off (make actions TENANT= HANDOFF=on).
  - deploy/commands/tests/test_cases.py: offer_case writes nothing, is refused without the case desk role or a configured queue, and is not offered with the switch off; chat() lifts case_offer into the response.
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.5**, Level 3 · One fixed reply at every door (step 5), new sub-step 'When the rules miss, the agent hands over': Runs make actions TENANT=acme HANDOFF=on and sends a question gate() lets through (the build asserts it returns None) to /v1/chat with brain langchain. Then asks the same question in the Chat page, presses the button, and raises and confirms the case on the Desk page *The learner sees:* tool_calls ['offer_case'] and case_offer {'case_type': ...}, with the model calls and rupees; nothing written until they press; the confirmed case in its queue's inbox
  - **10.5**, Level 5 · Advanced: Reads how a person comes into a turn *The learner sees:* Three ways: the gate's fixed reply before any model; the agent's offer when the rules miss; a second person's approval when the agent wants to act (10.2). The chat door is tied back to GuardMiddleware and before_tool_callback in 10.3 and 10.4.
  - **10.5**, Verify it yourself, and What changed on your lane: Ticks the new row and switches acme's agent_handoff off *The learner sees:* A checklist row for the offer on a gate miss; one more confirmed case
- **Proof:** test_cases.py passes in CI's chat pins. check_lesson.py 10.5 and audit_pages.py 10.5 pass, and the build asserts gate() returns None for the step's question. On a lane, tool_calls includes offer_case and the response carries case_offer, and make cases TENANT=acme shows only the case the learner confirmed.
- **Risks:** A real model may not call the tool for a given phrasing; the docstring is the lever, and the page shows the captured turn. The model's reason may quote a disclosure, so it goes only to the response, never to a case or a row. A later lexicon fix may catch the chosen question, so the build picks one gate() still lets through. The step adds about 300 words and two cells to a long lesson.
- **Decision:** Offer only, with the person raising the case (recommended), or draft the case on their behalf with cases.draft(source='model') and an empty summary. Also whether the ADK brain gets the tool.
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: (1) For posh, offer_case checks not cases.posh_errors(case_queues) instead of queue_for(); tests cover all six case types. (2) The chat row logs 'sensitive' and a null user for posh, grievance and privacy_request; the tool takes no 'reason' argument (or never echoes it); the gap box names checkpoint retention. (3) Rename the switch CASE_OFFER=on / agent_case_offer (HANDOFF is G1's). (4) List the rebuilds of 10.1-11.3 and 13.2, or land D8's kit half in D1's PR. (after review: D8 issues: posh routing, privacy, name clash, rebuilds.)
- **Closes:** PRES-06, L10.5-W01, L10.5-W02, L10.5-S07, BENCH-T11_hitl_approval_interrupts

## E. Agent evaluation and the Desk measurement

**Goal.** Make Module 10 measure what it claims, on the learner's own lane, with references that can fail. Four parts. (1) An agent task set with multi-step references. Each brain is scored on outcome, trajectory, arguments, citations and rupees against the direct floor, after G8-b gives every judge question its own session. (2) A route set with dev and test rows written by people: escalations, case, clarify, follow-up, multi-intent, injection, Hindi and Hinglish. Its test gates and the B-versus-C-versus-A* ship decision become real, so 10.6's proof can pass instead of failing by design. (3) The 10.5 gate's recall is measured on people's disclosures. (4) The router is calibrated from probe facts and swept thresholds, then watched through a health report and its alert policies.

### E2. An agent task set with multi-step references, and one scorer for outcome, trajectory, citations and rupees

- **Effort:** L · **Kind:** kit · **First phase:** P1 · **After:** E1

Gaps plan section 4.4's evals/handoff_eval.py design: trajectory_metrics, a session per row and run, explains and never gates, an offline self-test CI runs. From route_eval.py: wilson(), rate(), --runs (pass^k) and --save. Also run_eval.contains() and the Desk plan's agent-mode lane proofs (section 4.10). Departs from the gaps plan by leaving judge.py's REFERENCE_TRAJECTORY alone (7.2 quotes it at build.py:95). Multi-step references live in the task set instead.
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/evals/agent_tasks.jsonl (new, 16 tasks). Fields: {id, slice, surfaces, tenant, identity, turns, reference_trajectory, expected_outcome, must_contain, must_not_contain, required_documents, expected_calc, source, author, note}. Slices: - single_hop, 2 tasks: golden lk-06 and lk-01. - two_documents, 3 tasks: jn-01, jn-08 and jn-10, with their must_retrieve slugs as required_documents. - follow_up, 2 tasks: two turns in one session. Turn 2 must call retrieve, as SYSTEM asks (services/chat/brains.py:51-58). - calculator, 3 tasks: jn-03's encashment and notice; a gratuity estimate from a stated wage and years; notice_end from a stated acknowledgement date. - provenance_trap, 2 tasks: a gratuity question with no wage; a person who misstates LV-07's cap. - refusal, 2 tasks: rf-01 and rf-02. - isolation, 2 tasks: an acme caller asking for zeta's or Globex's figure, which goes in must_not_contain. Calculator and trap tasks list surfaces [desk] until tools.TOOLS holds a calculator.
  - deploy/evals/agent_eval.py (new, stdlib). --check validates the set: - every must_contain sits on its line of evals/corpus/<tenant>/; - every required document is in evals/manifest.json; - every expected_calc is recomputed by shared/desk_calc.calculate (gratuity_estimate(52000, 7, 6) = 210000, as desk_calc's selftest pins it); - no question equals, under route_eval.normalise(), a routes.jsonl test row. --selftest runs on canned replies. --live sends each task to POST /v1/chat for every brain in BRAINS, as documind-ui-sa with make judge's token, in session agent-eval-{run8}-{brain}-{task} (at most 64 characters). A follow_up task's two turns share one session.
  - deploy/evals/agent_eval.py scoring: - outcome: the expected outcome, every must_contain matched by run_eval.contains, no must_not_contain; - trajectory: judge.trajectory_metrics (:135-145) against each task's own multi-step reference, plus calls over the reference. The direct brain's literal ['retrieve'] is reported as code, not scored; - required documents cited, and every [n] in range; - refusals, model calls, cost_inr and latency, all from the response's limits block (services/chat/limits.py:164-168). The report gives each brain and each slice with route_eval.rate()'s Wilson intervals, plus Rs per task and Rs per correct answer. Options: --runs k (pass^k), --save and --replay, --compare A B (a paired before/after table), and --vertex for groundedness through judge.to_frame() and evaluate().
  - deploy/evals/paired.py (new, stdlib): the paired 2x2 table and mcnemar_exact(b, c), a two-sided exact binomial test on the discordant pairs. --selftest checks it against worked values. E8 adds the non-inferiority test.
  - deploy/evals/tests/test_agent_eval.py and test_paired.py (new), run by unittest discover -s evals/tests in .github/workflows/checks.yml:57 and deploy/.github/workflows/documind-dryrun.yml:62
  - deploy/mk/agents.mk: agent-eval (BRAINS, SLICE, RUNS, SAVE, REPORT, COMPARE), minting its token as make judge does (Makefile:485-496). agent-eval is appended to the .PHONY line (Makefile:196), so the Makefile keeps the 885 lines that 18.3 counts. Also mk/README.md's agents.mk row, and deploy/UNOWNED.md rows for the three new files until a page quotes them.
- **Proof:** python deploy/evals/agent_eval.py --check and --selftest pass. python -m unittest discover -s deploy/evals/tests passes in both CI workflows. On the author's lane, make agent-eval PROJECT=documind-ai-YOUR-ID BRAINS=direct,langchain,langgraph,adk scores all 16 tasks for all four brains, 18 turns each in distinct sessions, with no 'not scored' line. It prints the per-slice table and each agent brain's paired table against direct.
- **Risks:** - At 16 tasks a real model's variance is large, so every rate carries its interval and pass^k is offered. - A full live run is about 72 turns, an estimated Rs 40 at shared/prices.py's flash rates and 10.4's token counts. The author's measured figure replaces the estimate. - References come from --check, not typing. - Until the Module 10 spine workstream gives the chat brains a calculator, they hold only calculate_processing_cost. Calculator tasks are scored on the Desk surface (E8) until then. - /v1/chat returns tool names, not arguments, so argument checks for the chat brains need --inproc (E3).
- **Decision:** Run groundedness through Vertex AI Evaluation by default, which adds the Evaluation service's cost, or only with --vertex (recommended).

### E3. 10.4: the agent measured against the direct floor, a Change-it with a paired verdict, and a decision table

Precision on first-person questions. The two-turn disclosures the door misses, because a hit checkpoints nothing.

Re-runs make gate-recall and python -m unittest commands/tests/test_desk_rules.py (Rs 0), then runs git checkout on the file. *The learner sees:* The row now fires, and whether any first-person non-escalation question fires with it
  - **10.5 Hand a question to the person the law names**, Level 5 direct reads, after the overdue scan that finds 0 due: Loads a lab copy of acme's queues with the grievance queue's sla_days at 0 (make desk-queues FILE=). Raises and confirms one grievance case as evalacme, runs gcloud run jobs execute documind-cases-overdue, then reloads evals/desk/queues.acme.json. *The learner sees:* A case_overdue line from the job that names the queue and no person, and the case_overdue policy (terraform/desk_alerts.tf:239) opening an incident on the lane's channels

On the author's lane, make gate-recall LIVE=1 prints both recalls per class, and the drill's incident appears.

Or allow a reviewed known-miss list for rows the rules cannot catch without losing precision: on routed tenants rule D and the model check catch them, and single-mode tenants are set to DESK_GATE=on.

### E6. Calibrate the router: probe facts, swept thresholds, measured cost, and a threshold Change-it

- **Effort:** L · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **First phase:** P5 · **After:** E4
- **Reuses:** Desk plan section 4.1 (the probe's four facts decide rule E and MINIMAL). Section 4.8: acceptance rules A to F, and calibration that gives 98% on accepted turns, sends 70% of L1's errors to L2 and caps L2 at 15% to start. Section 1.3's design budgets. Risk 9.
- **Kit:**
  - Run make route-probe (mk/agents.mk:50-51, evals/route_probe.py: five calls, well under Rs 1) on the author's lane and record the four facts in the pull request. They depend on the lane and are not committed (Desk plan section 4.1).
  - deploy/services/chat/desk_router.py, in a reviewed commit: - ACCEPT_VOTES and TAU_OOS (:90, :92) set to make route-calibrate's recommendation (mk/agents.mk:64-65, evals/route_threshold.py, about Rs 2) on the dev rows written by people; - L2_THINKING (:99) set to MINIMAL only if the probe shows gemini-3.6-flash accepts it; - acceptance rule E in accept() (p of 0.80 or more accepts; a top-two margin under 0.15 clarifies) only if the probe shows top-two logprobs on flash-lite at global and the author takes it; - PROMPT_VERSION (:104) bumped if the prompt changes.
  - deploy/evals/route_eval.py --live prints the mean router cost from the Meter's real token counts, and the p50 and p95 router_ms of turns L1 accepted alone, beside the design budgets (Rs 0.03, 1.5 s). E7's test gates share this code.
  - deploy/commands/tests/test_desk.py covers the calibrated constants and, if built, rule E's cases. route_probe.py and route_threshold.py leave the Desk section of deploy/UNOWNED.md once 10.6 quotes them.
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.6 Route each question to one specialist agent**, Level 3 'Two signals and one arbiter, in the code' (parts/c.html:5-26): Reads prompt() and knn_vote() from desk_router.py and PROMPT_EXEMPLARS from desk_routes.py, quoted verbatim through block() *The learner sees:* The fenced question, the examples and the vote the router really runs
  - **10.6 Route each question to one specialist agent**, Level 3, after 'the router on every dev row' (parts/c.html:28-32), 'Change it': Changes ACCEPT_VOTES from 5 to 6 (or TAU_OOS) in the local desk_router.py. Re-runs python deploy/evals/route_eval.py --local and route_threshold.py --local (Rs 0), then runs git checkout on the file. *The learner sees:* The method mix, the share sent to L2 and the accepted accuracy move, while the sweep still recommends the committed values
  - **10.6 Route each question to one specialist agent**, Level 5, before the eval: Runs make route-probe and make route-calibrate *The learner sees:* The four facts (the learner's lane values as placeholders, the author's as a labelled sample) and the sweep table with the committed pair marked as recommended
  - **10.6 Route each question to one specialist agent**, Level 5 'What it costs' (parts/c.html:144-156) and the box (:163, :168-169): Reads the router's cost per turn *The learner sees:* The live eval's measured figure in place of estimates at four characters a token. The Change-it's two --local runs differ in the L2 share.

### E7. The Phase A test split, the test gates and the contamination check, so 10.6's proof can pass

- **Effort:** L · **Kind:** kit · **First phase:** P5 · **After:** E4, E6
- **Reuses:** Desk plan section 5.2: contamination rules 1 to 6, the Phase A column, required_routes.json, route_test_refresh.log, writers and labels, and run cost (about Rs 15 for a pass^3 of routing). Section 1.3 item 3: the test gates and the two design budgets. Section 6.2: 10.6's Verify it yourself. Risks 1 to 3.
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/evals/routes.jsonl: the 392-row Phase A test split of Desk plan section 5.2: - 30 each of handbook, statute, out_of_scope and clarify; - 100 escalation rows, 20 a class, at least a third in Hindi or Hinglish, with 5 globex rows a class; - 80 first-person, 20 near-miss, 15 follow-up, 15 multi-intent; - 15 injection, 6 PII two-turn, 8 calculator, 5 leaver denial, 8 coverage and single mode. The rows are written by people who have not seen the rules, the prompt or the exemplars, and frozen after E6's calibration.
  - deploy/evals/required_routes.json (new): every test escalation and denial id, with a per-class minimum n. Each row must pass on its own, as evals/required.json's ids do for run_eval.py.
  - deploy/evals/route_eval.py, TEST_GATES beside DEV_GATES (:581): - every required row passes; - top-1 route accuracy 95% or more, and per-desk recall 90% or more; - authority_rate 1.00 per desk; - denial 100% with zero retrieve calls; - rows with expect_model_calls_max 0 stay within it; - the design budgets. gate_failures(result, split) (:783-796) picks the set, and --live (:1009) applies the gates to arm B on either split. --contamination --project embeds both splits with text-embedding-005 and fails on a cross-split cosine of 0.95 or more, the half the docstring (:34-35) leaves to a lane. --selftest checks required_routes.json.
  - deploy/evals/route_test_refresh.log (new): a test row whose failure leads to a change moves to dev, and a fresh unseen row replaces it (Desk plan section 5.2, rule 5).
  - deploy/evals/tests/test_route_eval.py: the test gates, the required rows, and the contamination helper on canned vectors
  - Forced rebuild in this pull request: lessons/10-agents/10.6-desk-router/build.py:263 (the test split is empty) and the step-10 test cell's output (:1984, which becomes a run instead of Error 1), with parts/c.html:164 and :186
- **Proof:** python deploy/evals/route_eval.py --selftest prints test rows by route, every required id present and 'no cross-split pair'. route_eval.py --contamination finds no pair at 0.95 or more. python -m unittest discover -s deploy/evals/tests passes. The required rows pass three runs in a row on the author's lane before the freeze. Then make route-eval PROJECT=documind-ai-YOUR-ID SPLIT=test prints PASS for every required escalation row, the test gates met and the design budgets within limits.
- **Risks:** - Writer time again. - A test row must never be tuned on; if it is, it moves to dev. - A test run costs about Rs 5 a pass for routing (392 x Rs 0.0124, the Desk plan's estimate), plus about 80 rag-api answers. - Model variance may fail a required row on a lane even after the author's pass^3. E10's page explains this rather than hiding it.
- **Decision:** As before, plus: commission a separate group of test-split writers who have not seen the rules, prompt, exemplars or E4's dev rows (Desk plan 5.2 rule 3). Forced rebuild list adds 10.6 build.py:1419 (RC_TEST == 1 and 'FAIL route accuracy 0/0 (no rows)').
- **Revised:** decision_needed revised after review (E7 issues: writer independence; missing rebuild.)
- **Also:** (final review) The 392 test rows need a second, sequential writer commission (2-3 weeks elapsed, started once E6 freezes the thresholds), or the interim 180-row split is written by E4's writers under a stated independence exception (decision 15).
- **Closes:** GAP-G05, PRES-04, L10.6-W02, L10.6-W03, L10.6-W12, L10.6-W13, L10.6-S06, PLAN-CP10.6, PLAN-D-gates

### E8. Routed desks against code only and one agent: the paired ship decision, pinned slices, and agent-mode scoring

- **Effort:** L · **Kind:** kit · **First phase:** P5 · **After:** E2, E7
- **Reuses:** Desk plan section 1.3, item 3: B ships only if it is non-inferior to C and A* on correct_rate and authority_rate on the test split, by exact McNemar with a pre-registered 3-point margin; otherwise single mode or the fold-back. Section 4.1 (--arm), 4.9 (arm C) and 4.10 (arm A* and its lane proofs: 'a gratuity question returns an estimate', 'a number that is not in the passages is refused'). Section 5.2's run cost. route_eval.py's run_arm() and --save. Departs from the Desk plan by putting a pinned, cost-bounded slice of the comparison on the learner's page; the Desk plan ran the arms only on the author's lane at M5.
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/evals/paired.py: noninferior(pairs, margin), an exact unconditional test of H0: p_B - p_X <= -margin for paired outcomes. It uses Tango's score statistic, with the nuisance parameter maximised on a grid, and --selftest checks it against published worked examples. MARGIN = 0.03 is committed as a constant, with its date, before any test row is scored, so the rule is pre-registered (Desk plan section 1.3, item 3).
  - deploy/evals/route_eval.py --compare RUN_B RUN_C [RUN_ASTAR] reads saved runs (--save) of the same split and rows, one arm each (run_arm(), :431-443). For correct_rate, authority_rate and pooled escalation it prints the paired 2x2 table, the exact McNemar p and the non-inferiority verdict. It exits 0 only when B is non-inferior to C and to A* on both correct_rate and authority_rate ('B ships'). Otherwise it exits 1 with 'the tenant runs single, or the desk folds back to the direct scoped answer'.
  - deploy/evals/route_slices.json (new): pinned id lists for arms (about 40 test rows, stratified, including the 8 calculator rows), follow_up, multi_intent, injection and hindi_hinglish. route_eval.py --slice NAME works with --live, --predictions and --compare, and --selftest checks that every id exists on one split.
  - deploy/evals/agent_eval.py --surface desk --arms B,C,Astar sends the calculator and provenance_trap tasks to POST /v1/desk with an arm, as each task's eval account through route_eval.mint() and post(). It scores sections[].mode, sections[].calculations and refusals against expected_calc. Each calculation carries its calculator, its args, and where each number was found: 'your message', or a clause with its [n] (desk_agent.py:178-180).
  - deploy/smoke/smoke_desk.py gains one check, the Desk plan's agent-mode lane proof (section 4.10): jn-03 sent to /v1/desk returns a section in mode agent, with a calculator in tool_calls and 'min(50, 45) = 45' in its 'Worked out in code' block.
  - deploy/mk/agents.mk: route-compare (B=, C=, ASTAR=), and SLICE= on route-eval and agent-eval; .PHONY (Makefile:196); mk/README.md. Tests: evals/tests/test_route_eval.py (compare, slices) and test_paired.py (non-inferiority vectors).
- **Proof:** python deploy/evals/paired.py --selftest matches the published examples. python -m unittest discover -s deploy/evals/tests passes. On the author's lane, make route-eval SPLIT=test runs three times with SAVE=, once each for ARM=B, ARM=C and ARM=Astar. make route-compare then prints three paired tables and the ship verdict on the full test split. make agent-eval SURFACE=desk ARMS=B,C,Astar scores the calculator tasks' arguments and provenance. make smoke-desk passes with the agent-mode check.
- **Risks:** - The exact method must match the research report's section 7.4 before the test split is scored. - A 40-row slice cannot show non-inferiority at 3 points, so the learner's run illustrates and the author's full run decides. - A* and agent-mode answers cost a few flash calls each. - Arms C and A* need 10.6's desk_eval accounts, so none of this can run in 10.4. - With a real model in agent mode, the smoke checks only the block that code writes.
- **Decision:** Confirm the 3-point margin and the exact method, both pre-registered in paired.py before the test split is scored. Set the arms slice's size (recommended: about 40 rows; the full comparison stays the author's).
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: (1) MARGIN, its date and the method are committed in paired.py in E2 (before E7's first test-split run); E7's first test run depends on it. (2) Declare the departure from Desk plan 1.3's 'exact McNemar' (McNemar tests equality, not a margin) and print the McNemar p beside Tango's test; 'reuses' corrected. (3) The smoke_desk agent-mode check is A8's: pass on 'mode agent with a calculator in tool_calls', report 'min(50, 45) = 45' as INFO. (4) test_paired.py added to commands/desk-check.sh. (after review: E8 issues: pre-registration timing, undeclared departure, flaky smoke, desk-check claim.)
- **Also:** (final review) paired.py implements one statistic: C3's paired difference with a 95% Newcombe interval, non-inferior when its lower bound is above -3 points, with the exact McNemar p printed beside it (decision 14). C3 and A9 call this same function; PR85 alone pre-registers it, and PR83 lands after PR85.
- **Also:** (final review, for L10.6-S13) Kit: agent_eval.py --surface desk also scores each agent-mode section's tool_calls against the task's reference_trajectory with judge.trajectory_metrics (exact, in-order, any-order), per arm, beside the calculation and provenance scores. test_agent_eval.py gains a scripted desk-trajectory case. Lesson: the page that teaches agent mode (10.7 Level 5 in M3's arms slot, or 10.6 step 10 if there is no split) runs make agent-eval SURFACE=desk ARMS=B,Astar SLICE=calculator. The learner sees per-arm trajectory and calculation rates with intervals, with the author's full run as a labelled sample, and G7's optional TARGET=desk trace of jn-03 sits on the same page. Proof: the author's lane run prints per-arm trajectory metrics for the calculator tasks.
- **Closes:** GAP-G05, L10.6-W07, L10.6-W14, L10.6-S02, L10.6-S13, PLAN-G10-h, PLAN-D-gates, KIT-03, KIT-11, BENCH-T18_agent_eval_offline, BENCH-T21_testing_agents, BENCH-T28_versioning_release

### E9. Watch the router: a health report, its alert policies switched on, and an optional fallback drill

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **First phase:** P5 · **After:** E6
- **Reuses:** Desk plan section 4.11 (G10-i: desk_daily and the router's alert policies). Risk 10: measure router p50 and p95 in shadow. Section 1.3, item 6: shadow mode and desk_daily are what a pilot measures. commands/limits-drill.sh (lesson 10.3) for the drill. Departs from the Desk plan only by adding a report and a drill instead of lowering the production floors.

  - deploy/evals/router_health.py (new, stdlib). It reads rows with gcloud logging read, as evals/usage_rows.py:29-37 does. From the desk and desk_shadow rows, per tenant and event over HOURS, it reports: - routed turns, fallback share, L2 share; - clarify and out_of_scope share against the week before; - chip re-route rate, and grounded-refusal share per desk; - mean router cost, and p50 and p95 router_ms; - which of the three policies would page at the production floor of 100 turns (terraform/desk_alerts.tf:101, :133-134). People and service accounts get separate columns, because the policies' metric counts people only (desk_alerts.tf:42-49). --selftest runs on canned rows.
  - deploy/mk/agents.mk: router-health (HOURS=), and an optional router-drill. The drill is commands/router-drill.sh, in limits-drill's pattern (mk/agents.mk:95-96). It sets DESK_L1_TIMEOUT_S=0.001 on documind-chat, sends a few route-set questions to /v1/desk as evalacme, prints each turn's HTTP 200 and method fallback, and restores the variable from a trap. Also .PHONY (Makefile:196) and mk/README.md.
  - deploy/services/chat/desk_router.py:93: L1_TIMEOUT_S is read from DESK_L1_TIMEOUT_S with 4.0 as the default, so nothing changes unless the drill sets it. Only if the author takes the drill.
  - deploy/commands/tests/test_desk_operations.py: router_health's filters and floors equal desk_alerts.tf's. terraform/desk_alerts.tf keeps its production floors, and its policies' documentation names make router-health.
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.6 Route each question to one specialist agent**, Level 5 direct reads, step 8 (parts/c.html:86-125): Runs make router-health HOURS=2 after the eval and the shadow *The learner sees:* Each share for people and for the eval accounts, and which policy would page on a production tenant's traffic
  - **10.6 Route each question to one specialist agent**, Step 8: Runs make plan up DESK_ROUTER_ALERTS=true DESK_JOB=true, then lists the alert policies whose display names start with 'Desk router' using gcloud alpha monitoring policies list *The learner sees:* The three policies and their PromQL conditions with the 100-turn floors
  - **10.6 Route each question to one specialist agent**, Step 8, optional: Runs make router-drill *The learner sees:* Every turn answers 200 with method fallback. router-health's fallback share jumps for the eval accounts, while the people-only policy stays quiet by design.
  - **10.6 Route each question to one specialist agent**, Box (parts/c.html:170) and What changed on your lane (:218): Reads the lane state *The learner sees:* 'The router's alerts are off' becomes: off by default, switched on in step 8, with DESK_ROUTER_ALERTS=true kept on every later plan
- **Proof:** python deploy/evals/router_health.py --selftest passes, as does the test_desk_operations floors test. terraform -chdir=deploy/terraform validate passes. On the author's lane, make router-health prints the shares, make plan up DESK_ROUTER_ALERTS=true creates the three policies, and the optional drill puts DESK_L1_TIMEOUT_S back on exit.
- **Risks:** - Cloud Monitoring may refuse a PromQL condition on a metric with no data, so the switch stays off by default and goes on only after traffic. - The drill changes a deployed variable, so it refuses to run without the trap. - Desk rows hold no question by design, so online evaluation uses proxy signals (fallback, L2, clarify, chip re-routes, grounded refusals) rather than labels. - E6 and this task both touch desk_router.py's constants, so they share one 10.6 rebuild.
- **Decision:** Add the DESK_L1_TIMEOUT_S drill switch to desk_router.py (recommended: default 4.0, mirroring limits-drill), or skip the drill and keep only the report and the policies.
- **Revised:** lesson_changes: Amend: step 8 reads desk_daily's shares (make desk-views, bq query) first; router_health keeps only what desk_daily lacks (desk_shadow rows, hour windows, the 100-turn floors) and says so. The drill uses questions with no identifier and no case signal (e.g. lk-06). The plan command reads 'make plan up with every switch your lane was applied with', listing GCHAT_DOOR and DESK_GATE_ALERTS. (after review: E9 issues: duplicates desk_daily; drill scope; switch guard.)
- **Closes:** PRES-08, L10.6-W13, L10.6-S07, L10.6-S08, PLAN-D-unowned, KIT-06, BENCH-T19_simulation_online_eval

## F. Agent security on the agent path

**Goal.** Make DocuMind's agents treat retrieved passages, peer replies and third-party MCP tool metadata as untrusted on every path the kit runs, make the guards that already exist actually run on a learner's lane, and teach it honestly. Today the agent path has at most one screen: rag-api screens the search words the model wrote, and only on a revision with ARMOR=on, which answers 500 until the verdict fix lands (services/rag-api/guard.py:15-19). The agents' own model calls go straight to Vertex AI: the chat brains through shared/profile.py build_llm(), the ADK brain through its own client, and the A2A peer and the Desk agent through theirs. Meanwhile 8.3 says Model Armor 'reads every question and every answer'. F lands the author's G5 design (gaps plan section 4.7: the verdict fix, fence and scrub, a context screen, sticky taint, hash-pinned peer tools, a refusal log, an attack set scored by ASR, and lesson 12.5). It departs from that design in three places that the kit now allows or needs: the context screen also covers /v1/passages, a Model Armor screen at the chat door closes the profile.py bypass, and the Desk's doc_type registry becomes an enforced 'reviewed sources only' control for agents. F also runs the Desk's own model guardrail (desk_recall) once on the lane, tests the router's injection-resistance claim, and shows live that the tenant is pinned against the model.

### F1. Fix Model Armor's verdict and make 8.3 say exactly what it screens (G5-a)

- **Effort:** S · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **First phase:** P0
- **Reuses:** Gaps plan section 3 phase 1.1 (G5-a together with 8.3); the first clause of section 4.7's kit row 'services/rag-api/guard.py (G5-a)' and its 8.3 scene row; section 5's row 'FilterResult has no match_state'. One departure: _injected() and check_context() from the same kit row move to F5, where they are first called. That way the learner kit never ships an unused function, and 8.3's pull request changes one function.
- **Kit:**
  - services/rag-api/guard.py:15-19: _blocked() reads result.sanitization_result.filter_match_state == modelarmor_v1.FilterMatchState.MATCH_FOUND. google-cloud-modelarmor 0.7.1 (services/rag-api/requirements.txt:16) has no match_state on FilterResult, so ARMOR=on answers 500 today (gaps plan section 5: 'Confirmed, source and run').
  - commands/tests/test_guard_verdict.py (new): a fake modelarmor_v1 put in sys.modules, shaped like 0.7.1 (FilterResult is a oneof wrapper with no match_state; SanitizationResult carries filter_match_state). MATCH_FOUND blocks and NO_MATCH_FOUND passes. It runs in CI's shared interpreter, because .github/workflows/checks.yml discovers deploy/commands/tests, and it needs no Model Armor library.
  - Then python tools/kit_index.py and its --check. Rebuild 8.3, whose _blocked excerpt is at build.py:63. check_lesson confirms 6.3 needs nothing, since it quotes only guard.py's client lines.
- **Lessons:**
  - **8.3**, Level 0, the paragraph distinguishing DLP from Model Armor (parts/a.html:86): Reads the corrected scope. *The learner sees:* On a revision with ARMOR=on, Model Armor reads the questions and answers that pass through rag-api's /v1/query and /v1/stream, and the question on /v1/passages. The chat brains, the ADK brain, the A2A peer and the Desk's router and agent call Gemini directly (shared/profile.py build_llm, services/agent/agent.py) and meet no screen. The passages a model reads meet no screen either. A pointer leads to 12.5.
  - **8.3**, Level 3: step 5 (the guard excerpt) and step 6 (four probes to the candidate): Runs make candidate ARMOR=on and the same four-probe cell, then removes the tag as the page already does. *The learner sees:* The _blocked excerpt now reads filter_match_state, with one sentence on why. A labelled sample from a live lane replaces the stub's output. The plain question gets 200 (before the fix it got 500). The English injection gets 400 prompt_blocked. The Hinglish one gets 400 or is recorded as a finding. The PAN question is answered.
  - **8.3**, Level 5, the 'What the kit does not do yet' box: Reads two new points that the build asserts. *The learner sees:* First, 'The agent brains' own model calls meet no screen' (the build asserts there is no modelarmor import under services/chat or services/agent). Second, 'Retrieved passages are not screened' (the build asserts there is no screen_context in rag-api's main.py). F6 and F5 rewrite these lines when they land.
- **Proof:** test_guard_verdict.py green in checks.yml, plus an isolated venv step that installs google-cloud-modelarmor==0.7.1 and builds real SanitizeUserPromptResponse objects with MATCH_FOUND and NO_MATCH_FOUND (the sys.modules fake stays as the shared-interpreter fallback). Live: make candidate ARMOR=on prints 200 and 400 prompt_blocked. The sanitize price comes from Google's published Model Armor pricing (dated) or the lane's billing export, not make usage (rag-api rows only). live_verified comes from E11's reviewed live-run file. check_lesson/audit_pages 8.3 pass.

The sanitize price is unverified, so the cost line comes from make usage after the rehearsal. The candidate revision keeps ARMOR=on in its configuration until the next deploy; the page already says so. A missing roles/modelarmor.user still fails closed, as a 500.

The build assert (no modelarmor import) supports only this wording. (after review: F1 issue: 'meet no screen' overclaims.)
