(after review: H11 issue.)
- **Also:** (final review) Roadmap anchors: batch E's row is at line 203 and the count sentence at line 207; match by pattern.
- **Closes:** GAP-G08, L10.2-S05, L10.2-S07, L10.4-S09, L11.1-S05, L11.2-W04, PLAN-G7, PLAN-GD1, BENCH-T08_context_engineering

## I. Google Cloud's agent stack

**Goal.** Put Google Cloud's own agent stack on the learner's lane next to the kit's self-built one, so every 'Google gives you this' in Act V becomes a run with a measured number instead of a sentence. That covers six things: Gemini's function-calling modes and built-in tools set against the kit's custom tools (10.1); ADK's dev UI and evaluator on the kit's ADK brain (10.4); managed sessions and Memory Bank for the ADK brain (Module 11); the A2A peer deployed to Vertex AI Agent Engine (Agent Runtime) and compared with its Cloud Run deployment on callers, identity, durability, trace and cost (12.3); the four brains on the Rs 0 laptop lane (10.4); and, on the Advanced track, the brains through the model gateway (gaps plan G9, 18.1). One probe session settles every API, region, IAM and price fact first (I1). Every new switch defaults off, so a lane that turns nothing on behaves exactly as today.

### I1. Probe Google's agent stack on one lane before any page claims it

- **Effort:** L · **Kind:** process · **First phase:** P0
- **Reuses:** gaps plan section 5 (its Confirmed source / Confirmed run / Unverified convention) and its scratch-venv method
- **Kit:**
  - tools/probe_agent_stack.py (new; authoring repo, never published): one scripted run on the author's lane. It records each fact I2-I9 rest on as Confirmed (a dated run) or Unverified, then deletes everything it created.
  - Gemini at location global (gemini-3.6-flash): checks each FunctionCallingConfig mode (AUTO, ANY with allowed_function_names, NONE, VALIDATED). Checks whether one request may carry function_declarations plus google_search, or function_declarations plus code_execution; ADK 2.8.0's flows/llm_flows/agent_transfer.py says the Gemini API refuses built-in search beside function calling. Checks url_context on Gemini 3.
  - Agent Engine sessions: create an empty instance in us-central1 and in asia-south1 with client.agent_engines.create(), the call `adk deploy agent_engine` itself makes in cli_deploy.py. As documind-chat-sa, create a session with a 32-hex id and user id 'acme:<email>', append an event, confirm a get under another user id is refused and a ttl is honoured, and confirm whether roles/aiplatform.user (sa.tf chat_roles) is enough. Also Memory Bank ingest and retrieve in each region, and its allowed-topics setting.
  - Agent Engine runtime: deploy a two-line ADK agent with an .agent_engine_config.json naming a custom service_account and min_instances 0. Record whether min_instances 0 is accepted, whether idle time is billed, and the first-call latency. Record the service agent's actAs grant, and whether the runtime's metadata server mints an ID token for a Cloud Run audience (the path the peer's _mcp_headers uses). Record the spans the default telemetry writes (GOOGLE_CLOUD_AGENT_ENGINE_ENABLE_TELEMETRY=true, message content off) and whether the A2A route answers. Then delete it.
  - Laptop: does Ollama accept tools for gemma3:4b through ChatOllama.bind_tools, and through LiteLlm('ollama_chat/gemma3:4b') (brains.py:250-252)? If not, which small model does?
  - Dependency resolution: google-cloud-aiplatform pinned beside services/chat/requirements.txt must resolve with no sqlalchemy, because the 10.4, 11.1, 11.2 and 11.3 builds assert none. Also resolve google-adk[eval]==2.8.0 and google-cloud-aiplatform[agent_engines] in ~/graph-venv.
  - Prices, each with the date it was read, for shared/prices.py: Agent Platform compute, memory and storage; Sessions; Memory Bank; Code Execution; Grounding with Google Search per search query with its free allowance. On 2026-10-05 grounding read as 5,000 free queries a month across Gemini 3 models, then USD 14 per 1,000. Sources disagree on the 2026 Agent Platform SKUs, so no price is stated until it is re-read.
  - A facts table in the plan, in the gaps plan's section 5 format (API, version or service, status, evidence), cited by I2-I9.
- **Proof:** The facts table lists every API, region, IAM and price fact that I2-I9 cite. Each is either Confirmed by a dated run on the author's lane or Unverified, with the task step that settles it. The vertexai client lists no Agent Engine instance left behind by the probe.
- **Risks:** Costs a few rupees of runtime and storage. During 2026 Google renamed Agent Engine to Agent Runtime and Agent Platform and changed its prices and region lists, so every row carries its date. Memory Bank may be Preview, or absent, in asia-south1.
- **Decision:** Which lane runs the probe (recommended: the author's lane, never a learner's), and whether the asia-south1 result sets the default for AGENT_ENGINE_LOCATION.
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: session and memory rows run through VertexAiSessionService and VertexAiMemoryBankService (caller session_id, missing-id get, ttl kwarg, add_events_to_memory with wait_for_completion False and extraction latency) plus a raw read under the owner's credentials; isolation recorded as ADK's client-side check. Add rows: Code Execution sandbox create/delete, region, preview status, network isolation; GKE Agent Sandbox; Agent Identity user-delegated credentials; whether aiplatform.user lets every lane SA query an instance and whether per-resource IAM exists; which adk eval final-response metric runs; Memory Bank delete/purge API. (after review: I1 issues.)
- **Revised:** effort revised to M after review (Scope.)
- **Also:** (final review) Resolves google-cloud-aiplatform against H2's requirements (sqlalchemy pinned directly), without the 'no sqlalchemy' criterion. Split into I1a (Gemini calling modes, built-in tools, Ollama and dependency resolution: M) and I1b (Agent Engine runtime, sessions, Memory Bank and the sandbox: L); I5-I7 depend on I1b.
- **Closes:** (enables later tasks)

### I2. 10.1: the contract as Gemini receives it - four calling modes and three built-in tools

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **First phase:** P1 · **After:** I1, A (the calculator that replaces calculate_processing_cost), G (10.1's SYSTEM prompt excerpt), L (captured lane outputs)
- **Reuses:** Nothing from the gaps plan. Reuses the graph-venv pin line from 10.2 and 10.4, and 10.1's existing tool_call_schema build check.
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/commands/gemini_tools.py (new), step `declare`: prints the function declarations the LangChain brain sends for tools.TOOLS. These are the tools ChatGoogleGenerativeAI(...).bind_tools(TOOLS) converts, using langchain-google-genai 4.4.0's _function_utils.convert_to_genai_function_declarations. It shows the name, the docstring's first line as the description, the parameters, and that runtime is absent.
  - Same script, step `modes`: one google-genai call per mode on gemini-3.6-flash at location global, with thinking low, the kit's SYSTEM as system instruction, and no tool executed. The modes are AUTO, ANY, ANY with allowed_function_names=['calculate_processing_cost'] (or A's calculator once it replaces that tool, read at build time), NONE, and VALIDATED. It prints the parts returned (function_call name and args, or the first words of text), the tokens, and the rupees priced through shared/prices.py.
  - Same script, step `builtin`: runs code_execution and prints executable_code and code_execution_result. Runs google_search and prints web_search_queries, grounding chunk titles and URIs, and the queries billed. Runs url_context on a public statute page and prints url_context_metadata. Then a combination probe sends function declarations plus google_search in one request and prints the API's reply verbatim.
  - deploy/mk/agents.mk: a one-line `gemini-tools: guard-project` target with STEP=declare|modes|builtin; added to .PHONY; a row in deploy/mk/README.md.
  - deploy/shared/prices.py: named constants for the grounding price per search query and the free monthly allowance, with the date I1 read them, plus usd_grounding(queries). The equality check test_chat_limits.py makes against cost.py's FALLBACK is untouched.
  - deploy/commands/tests/test_gemini_tools.py (new, in the chat-pins CI job), using a fake genai client: each mode builds the right ToolConfig (ANY carries allowed_function_names; VALIDATED has no LangChain tool_choice spelling, as _tool_choice_to_tool_config confirms); `declare` output equals LangChain's tool_call_schema for every tool; the grounding arithmetic is right.
  - python tools/kit_index.py, so INDEX maps gemini_tools.py to 10.1.
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.1**, Level 1 'The contract: what the model reads of each tool': a second 'Do it' after the ast cell: Runs `make gemini-tools STEP=declare` in ~/graph-venv, which this step creates with 10.2's pin line if it is missing. *The learner sees:* The JSON Gemini receives for each tool: the name, the docstring's first line as the description, the parameters, and no runtime and no tenant. These are the bytes the build checked against tool_call_schema, now printed rather than parsed from source.
  - **10.1**, New direct-API level after 'The loop: the model chooses its tools': 'Gemini directly: four calling modes, three built-in tools': Runs `STEP=modes` on the cost question, then `STEP=builtin`. Change it: edits allowed_function_names (or the mode) in one place, re-runs STEP=modes and compares. *The learner sees:* Four calling modes behaving differently: AUTO leaves the choice to the model; ANY with an allowed name forces that call; NONE answers in text with no call; VALIDATED constrains to calls or text. Code execution: Python that Gemini wrote and ran itself, where nothing checks where its numbers came from (the contrast with the kit's argument checks). Google Search grounding: web sources and a per-query price. URL context: a public page read by the model. The combination probe's reply, which explains why ADK wraps google_search in an agent tool. A three-row table (custom tool, built-in tool, computer use) saying which one the tenant agent uses, and why.
  - **10.2**, Level 0, the sentence 'Gemini's function calling returns calls to the functions it was given, nothing else': Follows a link to 10.1's modes level. *The learner sees:* Why the refuse node needs a declared tool: Gemini predicts calls only to declared names, and ANY and VALIDATED constrain those calls further.
- **Proof:** On a live lane, `make gemini-tools STEP=modes` prints a function_call to the allowed calculator under ANY and no function_call under NONE. `STEP=builtin` prints an executable_code part with its result, at least one grounding web source, and the combination probe's verbatim reply. test_gemini_tools.py is green in CI. check_lesson.py 10.1 and audit_pages.py 10.1 pass, and the captured outputs are labelled as the lane's.
- **Risks:** Under AUTO the choice is the model's, so the page shows one captured run. Grounding is billed per search query after the free allowance. A public URL can move. The combination probe's answer can change with model versions, so the page dates it. Built-in tools must stay out of the deployed tenant agent.
- **Decision:** Whether any built-in tool ships in the deployed ADK brain behind a default-off switch (for example ADK_WEB_SEARCH using GoogleSearchTool(bypass_multi_tools_limit=True)). Recommended: no. Keep built-in tools demo-only, because web text in a tenant HR agent changes the citation contract and is workstream F's injection surface.
- **Revised:** lesson_changes: Amend: the venv uses 10.4's seven pins (or adds langchain-google-genai and google-genai with a build assert); page text on descriptions is set from STEP=declare's output (the whole dedented docstring incl. Args), correcting 10.1 b.html:36 in the same PR; STEP=declare prints what bind_tools bound (tool_call_schema, no runtime); the combination level is written around the lane's dated reply and explains GoogleSearchTool(bypass_multi_tools_limit=True) as ADK's handling for APIs that refuse; demo regeneration/review, checklist and lane-box rows added; the 10.2 pointer is its own PR. (after review: I2 issues.)
- **Also:** (final review) The declarations shown are those B1's single printer produces, which is what Gemini receives.
- **Also:** (final review, for BENCH-T01_agent_loop_function_calling) STEP=modes gains a fifth call: AUTO on a question with two independent lookups (e.g. 'What notice does a confirmed E3 serve, and what is the travel reimbursement cap?'), printing every function_call part of the one response (name, args, and id when the API returns one) and the part count. The 'Gemini directly' level shows the captured response labelled as the lane's. It says that several parts in one response are parallel calls the loop runs together (pointing to 10.1's ledger-lock cell from A4), and that the count is the model's choice. test_gemini_tools.py asserts the printer lists every part of a scripted two-part response.
- **Closes:** GAP-G09, L10.1-W05, L10.2-S06, BENCH-T01_agent_loop_function_calling, BENCH-T23_code_execution_sandbox

### I3. 10.4: ADK's own dev UI and evaluator on the kit's ADK brain, and its thinking level measured

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **First phase:** P1 · **After:** I1, E (agent eval set rows), A (decision table row), L (captured outputs)
- **Reuses:** Nothing from the gaps plan. Reuses G8-a's for_adk() and REQUEST contract, and 10.4's graph-venv.
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/agents/documind_adk/__init__.py and agent.py (new): root_agent = brains.AdkBrain().runner.agent. In google-adk 2.8.0 the Runner keeps app.root_agent as .agent, so ADK's tools drive the deployed brain's own agent, not a copy. A dev-only before_agent_callback fills tools.REQUEST (tools.py:83) with ADK_DEV_TENANT (default acme), an empty assertion, a fresh citation ledger and a limits.Meter, so retrieve() reaches rag-api as DOCUMIND_IMPERSONATE_SA (documind-ui-sa, as 10.1's shell cell does). The module refuses to import when K_SERVICE is set.
  - deploy/agents/README.md (new): what each package is, and that neither one runs on Cloud Run.
  - deploy/mk/agents.mk: an `adk-web` target (PYTHONPATH=. ~/graph-venv/bin/adk web agents --port 8000; LOCAL=1 adds DOCUMIND_PROFILE=local) and an `adk-eval` target (adk eval agents/documind_adk evals/adk/documind.evalset.json --config_file_path evals/adk/test_config.json --print_detailed_results). The recipe moves to commands/adk-dev.sh if it grows past a few lines.
  - deploy/evals/adk/documind.evalset.json (new): six to eight cases from E's agent eval set (fallback: golden rows): a single-hop policy answer, a figure, an unanswerable question, and a zeta request. Each has a reference answer and an expected tool trajectory that pins only the arguments the question fixes (a calculator's inputs), never the words of retrieve's query.
  - deploy/evals/adk/test_config.json (new): tool_trajectory_avg_score with match_type IN_ORDER, a final-response metric that I1 confirms runs on Vertex, and num_runs 2.
  - deploy/services/chat/brains.py: ADK_THINKING_LEVEL, default unset (today's behaviour), passed to AdkBrain's LlmAgent (brains.py:262-267) as generate_content_config with thinking_config, which ADK 2.8.0's llm_agent.py accepts there. The kit PR rebuilds every lesson whose quoted lines move; check_lesson.py names them.
  - deploy/commands/tests/test_adk_dev.py (new, chat-pins CI job): the package's root agent has AdkBrain's tools, callbacks and instruction; the import fails under K_SERVICE; the callback sets REQUEST on every invocation; the evalset loads with ADK's EvalSet model.
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.4**, New level after 'The loop, call by call': 'ADK's own tools: the dev UI and the evaluator': Runs `make adk-web`, opens port 8000 (Cloud Shell web preview), picks documind_adk, asks the gratuity question and opens the event and trace panes. Then runs `make adk-eval`. *The learner sees:* Each event of the turn: the model's function_call with its arguments, the function_response with numbered citations, the answer, and the tokens per call. These are the events the previous cell read from the session, now in ADK's UI. Then adk eval's per-case trajectory and response scores. ADK's trajectory_evaluator.py matches tool arguments as well as names, while judge.py's three matches compare names only, so a pinned search query fails because the model writes its own words.
  - **10.4**, Same level: a 'Change it' step: Sets ADK_THINKING_LEVEL=low (the LangChain brains' setting in shared/profile.py), then re-runs the loop cell and the eval. *The learner sees:* The ADK brain's thoughts tokens and cost line move toward LangChain's. The page's third reason for the cost gap becomes a measured difference rather than an assertion.
  - **10.4**, Part c, the 'Why the adapters differ' table: Reads one new row. *The learner sees:* Where each framework runs in production, with pointers to 11.2 (managed sessions) and 12.3 (Agent Engine).
- **Proof:** On a live lane, `make adk-eval` prints a score per case and an overall result. While `make adk-web` runs, `curl -s localhost:8000/list-apps` lists documind_adk. The captured loop cell after the change shows fewer thoughts tokens than before. test_adk_dev.py is green, and check_lesson.py and audit_pages.py pass for 10.4 and every rebuilt lesson.
- **Risks:** adk web on Cloud Shell needs web preview on port 8000. The dev callback must never load on Cloud Run, so it has a guard and a test. Argument-exact trajectories are brittle, so only inputs the question fixes are pinned. The eval's judge is Gemini, and its cost is stated from the lane. google-adk[eval] may pull large dependencies into graph-venv; I1 pins them.
- **Decision:** Where adk eval lives: recommended 7.2 beside judge.py (10.4 is already 9,318 visible words and gains I4 and A7 levels); 10.4 keeps adk web plus the thinking Change-it. Set a word budget for 10.4.
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: per-case metric stated; retrieve scored with rubric_based_tool_use_quality_v1 (or pinned with full args and the 0 shown); the proof requires one case that fails on a wrong calculator argument; num_runs dropped (run adk eval twice in the target or AgentEvaluator.evaluate(num_runs=2) from commands/); agents/documind_adk/agent.py puts services/chat on sys.path and commands/adk-dev.sh exports GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT, RAG_API_URL, DOCUMIND_IMPERSONATE_SA; test_adk_dev.py runs in the chat-pins step with google-adk[eval] in that venv only and the FRAMEWORKS skip pattern; ADK_THINKING_LEVEL is the one switch A7's Change-it uses. (after review: I3 issues.)
- **Revised:** decision_needed revised after review (I3 issue: 10.4 length.)
- **Closes:** GAP-G09, L10.4-W06, L10.4-W07, L10.4-S03

### I4. 10.4: the same four brains on the Rs 0 laptop lane

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **First phase:** P2 · **After:** I1 (Ollama tool support), I3 (the adk-web target), B (the change-it convention for docstrings)
- **Reuses:** The kit's chat-local target, and the measured retrieval floors in tools/check_local_lane.py (36 of 47 found, 0 leaks).
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/commands/chat-local-check.sh (new) and a `make chat-local-check` target beside chat-local (Makefile:563-571). It starts chat-local on 127.0.0.1:8081 in the background, waits for /health, and posts the notice-period question once per brain on a fresh session. It prints brain, tool_calls, citation count, cost_usd and latency, stops the server, and exits non-zero if any brain errors.
  - deploy/shared/profile.py (quoted by no lesson): if I1 finds Ollama refuses tools for gemma3:4b, DOCUMIND_LOCAL_AGENT_MODEL becomes the agent brains' local model (build_llm(agent=True)), and brains.py's local LiteLlm reads the same name. The direct brain's one grounded call, lesson 1.1 and lesson 18.2 keep gemma3:4b.
  - deploy/services/chat/requirements-local.txt: pins re-checked for litellm's ollama_chat tool calling.
  - deploy/commands/tests/test_local_profile.py (new, CI, no Ollama needed): under DOCUMIND_PROFILE=local, the LangChain and LangGraph brains get ChatOllama, the ADK brain gets a LiteLlm on ollama_chat/<model>, retrieve() takes the Chroma path with the tenant filter, and prices.usd() returns 0.
  - I3's `make adk-web LOCAL=1`: ADK's dev UI over the local profile, for Rs 0.
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.4**, New level after I3's level: 'The same adapters on the Rs 0 lane': On a laptop with Ollama, runs `make chat-local` in one terminal and `make chat-local-check` in another. Change it: sharpens the first line of retrieve's docstring (the doc_type values the corpus can honour) and re-runs the same check. *The learner sees:* All four brains answer from the same chunk ids through documind_tools' BM25 evidence gate, at Rs 0 on each row. Two seams changed: LangChain's model comes from build_llm() (ChatOllama), and ADK's comes through LiteLlm, the adapter ADK needs for any non-Gemini model. The small model makes tool-choice mistakes, and the docstring change moves them at no cost.
  - **10.1**, Level 0, the sentence saying retrieve() 'reads a Chroma directory on the laptop lane': Follows a pointer to 10.4's Rs 0 level. *The learner sees:* Where to iterate on tool descriptions without a bill.
- **Proof:** On the author's laptop, `make chat-local-check` prints all four brains with cost_usd 0.0, and non-empty tool_calls for the three agent brains on the notice-period question. test_local_profile.py is green in CI. check_lesson.py 10.4 and audit_pages.py 10.4 pass.
- **Risks:** gemma3:4b may not take tools in Ollama, which means a second local model and its download. Cloud Shell may not run a 4B model usefully, so the level is laptop-only and says so. A small model's tool choice varies by run, so the page labels one captured run. The local direct brain makes one grounded call, so its row shows one model call at Rs 0.
- **Decision:** Placement in 10.4 (recommended: it compares the adapters on a second model) or in 10.1. Also the local agent model, if gemma3:4b cannot call tools.
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: chat-local-check targets an already-running server (CHAT_URL, default 127.0.0.1:8081); DOCUMIND_LOCAL_AGENT_MODEL planned from the start with a tools-capable small model and its download size, stating the default change on the 1.1 and 6.4 lanes; the agent model chosen inside profile.py with a separate builder (no quoted brains.py lines move) or the rebuild list from check_lesson; Change-it reworded to 'the doc_type line of retrieve's docstring'; chunk ids shown, only tenant-scoped citations asserted; a CI venv installs requirements.txt plus requirements-local.txt (or stubs with asserted construction arguments). (after review: I4 issues.)
- **Closes:** L10.1-S08, KIT-13, BENCH-T07_framework_choice

### I5. 11.2 and 11.3: managed sessions for the ADK brain (VertexAiSessionService) so its conversations survive

- **Effort:** L · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **First phase:** P4 · **After:** I1 (regions, IAM, resolution), H (the retention TTL; the durable-store decision against gap decision 4; G7 compaction stays compatible because the service stores compaction metadata), L (captured outputs)
- **Reuses:** gaps plan section 6 decision 4 (the deferred durable-ADK item, taken with a different store); section 3's one-requirements-set rule (line 29 unchanged, SQLAlchemy out); G8-a's thread id and REQUEST
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/commands/agent_engine.py (new). `create`: makes an empty Agent Engine instance named documind-sessions in AGENT_ENGINE_LOCATION (default us-central1 unless I1 settles asia-south1), idempotent by display name, and prints its id. `sessions --tenant --user`: lists a person's sessions with each event's author, function_call names and text. `delete-sessions --user`: the erasure helper H's route reuses. `delete`: tears the instance down.
  - deploy/mk/memory.mk (created by whichever lands first, this task or H's G7, with its mk/README.md row): `agent-engine`, `agent-engine-sessions` and `agent-engine-down`, added to .PHONY.
  - deploy/services/chat/brains.py `_sessions()` (brains.py:273-284): an empty ADK_SESSIONS keeps today's behaviour. `vertex` returns VertexAiSessionService(project, location=AGENT_ENGINE_LOCATION, agent_engine_id=AGENT_ENGINE_ID). The docstring's 'Agent Engine sessions are its production answer' (brains.py:17-21) becomes the switch it names.
  - brains.py AdkBrain.answer (lines 286-302): on vertex, the session id becomes a 32-hex digest of the thread id, because the service accepts only ^[A-Za-z0-9_-]+$ (_SESSION_ID_PATTERN in google-adk 2.8.0's vertex_ai_session_service.py). The user id becomes 'tenant:email', so get_session itself refuses another person's session ('does not belong to user'). create_session passes a ttl from ADK_SESSION_TTL, the retention H sets.
  - Data-region rule: a tenant whose shared/tenancy.policy_for() returns 'in' keeps the in-memory store unless AGENT_ENGINE_LOCATION is an India region. This is logged once as adk_sessions_local_policy.
  - deploy/services/chat/requirements.txt: google-cloud-aiplatform==<I1's pin> on its own line, with no extras unless I1 shows the sessions client needs [agent-engines]. google-adk==2.8.0 stays byte for byte. A CI step resolves the set and fails if sqlalchemy appears, so DatabaseSessionService still cannot open, as 11.2's build asserts.
  - deploy/commands/deploy-chat.sh and the Makefile's deploy-services: ADK_SESSIONS, AGENT_ENGINE_ID and AGENT_ENGINE_LOCATION travel through the existing CHAT_EXTRA_ENV hook, only when set. The default deploy is unchanged.
  - deploy/terraform/sa.tf: unchanged if chat-sa's roles/aiplatform.user covers reasoningEngines sessions (I1). Otherwise, the narrow role I1 names, granted per account.
  - deploy/commands/tests/test_chat_brains.py: the id mapping is deterministic and matches the pattern; two tenants never share an id; 'in' tenants fall back to memory; the service is built from the env (constructor stubbed); the default env leaves _sessions() as it is today.
  - The build asserts in 10.4, 11.1, 11.2 and 11.3 that read _sessions() and the docstring move in the kit PR. Scene content follows, one lesson per PR: 11.2, then 11.3.
- **Lessons:**
  - **11.2**, New level after 'What your lane runs': 'Configure: managed sessions for the ADK brain': Runs `make agent-engine`, redeploys the chat service with ADK_SESSIONS=vertex, asks the ADK brain two turns, then runs `make agent-engine-sessions`. *The learner sees:* The instance id, and the session (a 32-hex id under acme:<email>) with its events: question, function call, function response, answer. They sit in Google's store, beside the Cloud SQL checkpoint tables the lesson already reads. The session's expiry, and the storage price read from the pricing page. The previous level's line 'ADK sessions in memory' is gone.
  - **11.3**, Level 'A conversation across a redeploy': Runs the same redeploy cell with ADK_SESSIONS=vertex. *The learner sees:* Both brains keep the conversation after the new revision serves. Before showing the fix, the text gives the real cause of the old loss: two gunicorn workers and up to ten instances, each with its own InMemorySessionService.
  - **11.3**, Level 'Another person finds nothing': Runs one cell that asks the service for the step's session under another user id. *The learner sees:* The service's own refusal, 'does not belong to user', beside the LangGraph thread-key isolation.
  - **11.3**, Checklist and 'What changed on your lane': Ticks the new rows. *The learner sees:* The documind-sessions instance holding the ADK brain's sessions, and how to turn it off (unset ADK_SESSIONS, then `make agent-engine-down`).
- **Proof:** On a live lane with ADK_SESSIONS=vertex, 11.3's redeploy cell shows the ADK brain continuing the conversation after the new revision serves, `make agent-engine-sessions` lists that session's events, and the cross-user read prints 'does not belong to user'. test_chat_brains.py is green. The resolved requirements contain no sqlalchemy. 10.4, 11.1, 11.2 and 11.3 rebuild and pass check_lesson.py and audit_pages.py.
- **Risks:** Conversations end up in two stores (LangGraph in Cloud SQL, ADK in Agent Engine), which doubles H's erasure surface. Residency: the 'in' rule covers the managed store, but the Cloud SQL checkpointer already sits in var.region (us-central1, cloudsql.tf:29) for every tenant while Firestore is in asia-south1; this gap is flagged to H. Per-turn latency: get_session lists every event and each event is one append, so it is measured on the lane. Sessions billing moved to a storage SKU during 2026. Whether chat-sa's role covers sessions is unverified until I1.
- **Decision:** Which durable store holds ADK sessions. Recommended: VertexAiSessionService, because it needs no migration job and no SQLAlchemy, adds no connections to the db-f1-micro whose pool sizing 11.2 already strains, is shared across workers and instances, has expiry built in, and is the production answer brains.py's own docstring names. The alternative is gap decision 4's google-adk[db] on the same Cloud SQL (workstream H), which keeps one store and delivers the course plan row's 'same instance' literally. Also the default session TTL (workstream H).
- **Revised:** lesson_changes: 11.3's level reads 'ADK's session service checks the owner after the read'; the raw API returns the session to an owner credential; boundary = the chat service's verified user id plus IAM. CHAT_ADK_ENV computed separately and appended at lesson-12.8.sh:32 (coordinated with I9), so Makefile:353 and 11.1 build.py:94 stay. (after review: I5 issues: client-side isolation; Makefile assert (verified 11.1 build.py:94).)
- **Also:** (final review) Rewritten as a second backend beside H2: ADK_SESSIONS=db (the default, H2's DatabaseSessionService) or vertex (VertexAiSessionService). The 'fails if sqlalchemy appears' CI step and the 'line 29 byte for byte' clause are dropped; the build follows H2's flipped asserts, and H5's erasure path covers both stores.
- **Closes:** GAP-G09, L10.4-W06, L10.4-W07, L10.4-S02, L11.2-W01, L11.2-W03, L11.3-W03, L11.3-W05, PLAN-GD4, PLAN-CP11, BENCH-T25_managed_deployment

### I6. Module 11: Memory Bank, an ADK brain that remembers a person across sessions

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **First phase:** P4 · **After:** I5, I1 (Memory Bank regions and topics API), H (the long-term memory level and the erase route), F (memory poisoning through a retrieved document)
- **Reuses:** Nothing in the gaps plan (G1-G9 has no long-term memory item). The register's GAP-G08 suggestion of a Memory Bank pointer becomes a run.
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/services/chat/brains.py: ADK_MEMORY, default off, requires ADK_SESSIONS=vertex. When on, the Runner gets VertexAiMemoryBankService(project, location, agent_engine_id) on the documind-sessions instance, and the ADK agent gets PreloadMemoryTool(), so a person's memories reach each model call without a model decision. After the answer, the turn's events go to add_events_to_memory under scope {app_name: documind, user_id: tenant:email}, without waiting for extraction (I1 confirms the call returns first). Extraction is limited to allowed topics: work facts a person states about themselves (office, contract type, tenure). Grievances are never stored; the 10.5 gate answers those before any brain runs.
  - deploy/commands/agent_engine.py: `memories --tenant --user` lists the stored facts; `forget --user` deletes every memory in that scope, and H's erase route calls the same function. Targets `memories` and `memories-forget` go in deploy/mk/memory.mk.
  - The same data-region rule as I5: no memory for an 'in' tenant unless Memory Bank exists in an India region (I1).
  - deploy/commands/tests/test_chat_brains.py: memory is off by default; when on, the scope is tenant-first, the preload tool is present, and ingestion never blocks the turn.
- **Lessons:**
  - **11.1 (or 11.4 if gap decision 1 adds it): H's long-term memory level**, The long-term memory level H adds, as the managed column beside H's PostgresStore column: With ADK_MEMORY=on, tells the ADK brain in session A: 'I am on a fixed-term contract in the Bengaluru office'. Asks a gratuity question in session B. Runs `make memories`, then `make memories-forget`, and asks again in session C. *The learner sees:* Session B's answer applies the fixed-term rule without being told again. `make memories` shows the extracted fact under acme:<email>. After forget, the fact is gone and session C no longer uses it. The extraction's model tokens are priced from the lane.
  - **11.1**, Level 0 taxonomy: Reads a fourth row. *The learner sees:* Long-term memory (Memory Bank or a store): what it keeps, who scopes it, how long it lasts, and how it is erased (with H).
- **Proof:** On a live lane, session B's answer uses session A's fact, `make memories` lists it, and after `make memories-forget` session C's answer does not use it. Tests are green. The Module 11 page builds and passes check_lesson.py and audit_pages.py.
- **Risks:** Memory Bank may be absent in asia-south1. Each ingestion is a billed model call. A remembered fact can be stale or wrong, which is why forget is shown. A planted fact could become a memory (workstream F). Memories are personal data under the DPDP Act, so retention and erasure apply (workstream H).
- **Decision:** Whether the lane's ADK brain gets long-term memory at all (recommended: yes, default off, shown in one Module 11 level), and which topics may be remembered.
- **Revised:** lesson_changes: Order A, then make memories until the fact is listed, then B (or wait_for_completion for the demo, with measured latency). The allowed-topics list is named as the control; a probe shows a gate-missed disclosure is not extracted. Add ADK_MEMORY_TTL (default from H's retention), a table row or Change-it swapping PreloadMemoryTool for load_memory, and the delete API in I1. (after review: I6 issues.)
- **Closes:** GAP-G09, BENCH-T10_long_term_memory

### I7. 12.3: the same A2A peer on Vertex AI Agent Engine - who may call it, as whom it calls the lane, and its trace

- **Effort:** L · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **First phase:** P4 · **After:** I1 (custom service account, actAs, min_instances, the global-location fix), I3 (deploy/agents and make adk-web), I5 (commands/agent_engine.py), J (12.3's A2A changes land in sequence), G (G3's trace of the Cloud Run peer, compared once it lands), C (G1 keeps the Cloud Run peer as its handoff target), K (G6-b caps the Cloud Run peer)
- **Reuses:** gaps plan section 5's confirmed ADK facts (RunConfig.max_llm_calls reads ADK_MAX_LLM_CALLS; header_provider runs on every tool call); G6-b's cap of 12; the caller-graph rule check_authz.py enforces
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/services/agent/peer.py (new, split from agent.py): INSTRUCTION, _mcp_headers and build_agent (agent.py:54-116). The model becomes an ADK Gemini whose client is pinned to location global, using the GlobalGemini pattern google-adk 2.8.0 documents in google_llm.py. This is needed because the Agent Engine image sets GOOGLE_CLOUD_LOCATION to the deploy region (the Dockerfile template in cli_deploy.py), and Gemini 3.x is served only from global. agent.py keeps to_a2a and imports peer.py. The Dockerfile copies the directory as before. The kit PR re-derives 12.3's excerpts.
  - deploy/agents/documind_peer/__init__.py and agent.py (new): root_agent = build_agent(), for `make adk-web`. The peer then runs on the learner's machine against the deployed MCP server, as DOCUMIND_IMPERSONATE_SA.
  - deploy/commands/agent_engine.py (I5's script). `deploy-peer`: stages peer.py, the peer's requirements.txt, a generated agent.py and a .env (MCP_URL, AGENT_MODEL, ADK_MAX_LLM_CALLS=12 as G6-b's cap on this host, message content kept off spans), then runs `adk deploy agent_engine` with an .agent_engine_config.json naming documind-agentengine-sa, min_instances 0 if I1 confirms it, and max_instances 1. `ask-peer`: sends one question through the instance's query API and prints the events. `peer-trace`: lists the latest trace's spans (invoke_agent, call_llm, execute_tool) through the Cloud Trace API. `sandbox-run`: creates one Code Execution sandbox in that instance with a short ttl, runs a two-line computation, prints stdout and deletes it. (ADK's AgentEngineSandboxCodeExecutor defaults the ttl to 31536000s and creates an instance when none is named.) `delete-peer`: removes the deployment.
  - ~/graph-venv gains google-cloud-aiplatform[agent_engines] for the deploy, at I1's pin. The chat image does not.
  - deploy/terraform/sa.tf: documind-agentengine-sa with aiplatform.user (for Gemini), logging.logWriter and cloudtrace.agent, and the Agent Engine service agent allowed to act as it, as I1 finds. run.invoker on documind-mcp is bound in the deploy script, the way lesson-7.2.sh binds the other invokers.
  - deploy/commands/lane.py roster_plan: the new account goes on acme's roster only, as agent-sa does.
  - deploy/mk/protocols.mk (created by the first of C, J, F or this task to land): `agent-engine-peer`, `ask-peer-ae`, `peer-trace`, `sandbox-run`, `smoke-agent-engine` and `agent-engine-peer-down`.
  - deploy/smoke/smoke_agent_engine.py (new): an acme question answered with a citation, a zeta question refused by the roster, and a trace present for the turn.
  - deploy/commands/checks/check_authz.py (authoring only): the caller graph gains the edge documind-agentengine-sa -> documind-mcp and asserts the account is on one roster. Pages cite only the learner's make targets.
- **Lessons:**
  - **12.3**, New UI level after 'The peer's permissions, as the kit writes them': 'The peer in adk web': Runs `make adk-web`, picks documind_peer and asks the gratuity question. *The learner sees:* The peer's instruction in the request, each MCP tool call with its arguments, the server's results and the answer. The peer runs on the learner's machine against the deployed server, speaking as documind-ui-sa. adk web's trace pane is a dev-only route, and the Agent Engine image deletes it.
  - **12.3**, New level after 'One task, traced': 'The same peer on Agent Engine': Runs `make agent-engine-peer`, `make ask-peer-ae`, `make peer-trace` and `make smoke-agent-engine`, optionally `make sandbox-run`, then `make agent-engine-peer-down`. *The learner sees:* A comparison table filled from the lane, one row per question. Who may call it: run.invoker and an ID token on Cloud Run; aiplatform permissions and an OAuth token on Agent Engine (which also serves A2A), and which lane accounts hold those. As whom it calls the lane: documind-agent-sa or documind-agentengine-sa, each on one roster. Sessions: in memory per instance, or the instance's managed sessions. Memory. First-call latency, measured. Observability: log lines, or a span tree in Cloud Trace with message content off. Cost: request time, or Agent Platform compute and storage, read from the pricing page. Control: your own image and routes, or Google's image running ADK's api_server. GKE (18.3) is named as the third host and Gemini Enterprise as the employee-facing surface, neither run. Gemini's built-in code execution (from 10.1) is set against the Agent Engine sandbox the cell created and removed.
  - **12.3**, Checklist, the 'does not do yet' box, and 'What changed on your lane': Ticks the new rows. *The learner sees:* An Agent Engine peer created and removed (or kept, if min_instances 0 is confirmed), and the 'Nothing records who asked' gap set against what the managed runtime records.
- **Proof:** On a live lane, `make smoke-agent-engine` passes (acme answered with a citation, zeta refused by the roster), `make peer-trace` lists invoke_agent, call_llm and execute_tool spans for the turn, and `make agent-engine-peer-down` leaves no instance. check_authz.py passes with the new edge. 12.3 builds and passes check_lesson.py and audit_pages.py.
- **Risks:** The runtime identity and its ID-token minting stay unverified until I1. If min_instances cannot be 0 there is an idle cost, covered by the teardown target and a cost line from the lane. The deploy takes minutes. ADK's auto-create can leave a stray instance. 12.3 grows: it is the shortest Act V page today (about 4,500 words), but J and C also add to it. Naming drifts across docs and CLI output (Agent Engine, Agent Runtime, Agent Platform).
- **Decision:** Placement: a level in 12.3 (recommended; its title promises a trace and the permissions) or a new Core lesson, for example 12.6 'Run the peer on Vertex AI Agent Engine and compare it with Cloud Run' (M's lesson-count decision). Also whether to keep the Agent Engine peer after the lesson or tear it down (recommended: tear it down unless min_instances 0 is confirmed).
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: check_authz reads agent.py and the split module; the new account joins lesson-7.2.sh's invoker loop and sa.tf's caller-graph line (account created by Terraform); Agent Engine's wider caller set (any aiplatform.user: ui-, api-, ingest-, chat-, agent-sa) presented as a finding, teardown default; google-adk[mcp]==2.8.0 and google-cloud-aiplatform[agent-engines] in graph-venv; adk-web exports MCP_URL and DOCUMIND_IMPERSONATE_SA; module renamed services/agent/peer_agent.py (G1 owns services/chat/peer.py); prefer new lesson 12.6 or cap 12.3's added words. (after review: I7 issues.)
- **Also:** (final review, for BENCH-T25_managed_deployment) Add a third column to 12.3's comparison table: GKE Autopilot. Fill it from documentation and from the kit's own terraform/gke.tf and deploy/gke/vllm-deployment.yaml rather than from a run, and label every cell 'documented, not run on your lane'. Rows: who may call it (an internal or IAP-fronted load balancer instead of run.invoker); as whom it calls the lane (Workload Identity Federation binding a Kubernetes service account to documind-agent-sa); sessions (self-managed, as on Cloud Run); scaling and idle cost (the pod resources you request plus the cluster fee, priced in INR from the dated GKE pricing page); observability; and control (sidecars, GPUs, long-lived connections). End the table with a 'choose this host when' row giving one condition for each of the three hosts, and point the GKE column to 18.3.
- **Also:** (final review, for GAP-G09) Remove 'Optional' from I7 and PR65, and take I7 out of P4's 'recorded as deferred' list. The 12.3 level 'The same peer on Agent Engine' always ships. It stays rehearsal-gated for learners only: a learner who cannot deploy reads the author's dated capture of every cell. Before make agent-engine-peer-down, add two cells on the same instance: (1) Run make ask-peer-ae twice with one session id. Then run python commands/agent_engine.py sessions --tenant acme --user <email> to list that managed session's events (question, function_call, function_response, answer). Show a read of the same session under another user id, which the service refuses. (2) Do one Memory Bank write and read: add_events_to_memory on that session, then agent_engine.py memories, with the extraction's tokens priced from the lane. The comparison table's Sessions and Memory rows cite these results. Agent Engine, its managed sessions and Memory Bank then each show a lane run on a page, even if PR63 and PR64 are deferred. If I1's probe fails a fact (region, actAs, min_instances 0), the level states which fact failed and keeps the cells that ran. Proof adds: the session's events and one stored memory are listed before teardown.
- **Closes:** GAP-G09, L10.4-W07, L12.3-W02, L12.3-W05, L12.3-W06, BENCH-T07_framework_choice, BENCH-T23_code_execution_sandbox, BENCH-T25_managed_deployment

### I9. G9 PR B: the agent brains through the model gateway, in two pull requests

- **Effort:** L · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **First phase:** P4 · **After:** I8, F (G5 fence, for B2 only), K (retries move to the gateway when max_retries=0)
- **Reuses:** gaps plan section 4.9 PR B and section 6 decision 14 (the default door stays vertex; a sensitive agent turn is a tool-less small-model answer; no tags from the chat; the peer stays on Gemini as the third door)

  - B1, as gaps plan section 4.9 PR B. New shared/gateway.py: the route map, a cached token, a per-turn ledger reporting into G6's Meter, backend_for(tenant) read from tenant_settings and cached 60 s, chat_model() with use_responses_api=False and max_retries=0, adk_model() through LiteLlm with an llm_client that adds the token, and route_of(exc). brains.build(name, checkpointer, llm=None, backend='vertex'). agent.py chooses the backend per turn after the tenant check, caches brains per (brain, backend), names the gateway_* fields in the row literal, and maps a gateway failure to a 502.
  - B1: requirements gain langchain-openai, openai and litellm at section 4.9's pins (re-resolved, no sqlalchemy). The Makefile gets CHAT_BACKEND ?= vertex, and deploy-gateway binds chat-sa. deploy/mk/serving.mk gets `chat-backend` and `smoke-chat-gateway`. New commands/chat-backend.sh. smoke/smoke_chat.py gets its DOCUMIND_EXPECT_GATEWAY section. terraform/gateway.tf names chat-sa. deploy/commands/checks/check_authz.py and check_gateway_chat.py's framework tier are updated.
  - B1 departure from section 4.9: a RESTRICTED request that carries tools fails closed as a 502 naming gateway_route documind-sensitive, an outcome the section's proof already accepts. B1 therefore needs only G8-a and G6-a, which have landed.
  - B2: documind_router.toolless() flattens a RESTRICTED tool turn into the last user turn as fenced evidence plus one sentence, using F's G5 fence (shared/spotlight.py), as section 4.9 designs.

  - **18.1**, Section 4.9's new level: the chat's agent brains through the same door: Runs `make chat-backend CHAT_BACKEND=gateway`, asks the LangChain and ADK brains the gratuity question, then asks a question carrying a PAN, then runs `make chat-backend CHAT_BACKEND=vertex`. *The learner sees:* Chat rows with model_backend gateway and gateway_cost_usd. The PAN turn is refused as a 502 naming documind-sensitive (after B1), then answered by the tool-less small model over fenced evidence (after B2), never by Gemini. The default door is restored at the end.
  - **10.4**, Part c framework table (A's decision table): Reads one row. *The learner sees:* Which door the model sits behind is a per-tenant setting (CHAT_BACKEND or the tenant pin), taught in 18.1; the brains do not change.

The PAN turn is a 502 with gateway_route documind-sensitive after B1, and a tool-less fenced answer after B2, never a 200 from Gemini. gateway_cost_usd appears on the chat row. After `make chat-backend CHAT_BACKEND=vertex`, `make smoke-chat` is green. 10.1-10.4, 11.1-11.3, 12.2 and 13.2 re-run with no text change.

Until B2 lands, sensitive tool turns show a visible 502, which the page states. Retries and fallback move to the gateway, and K's tool-error work must account for that.

Wording: mk/serving.mk and check_gateway_chat.py are new (created in B1 and I8). (after review: I9 issue: no fail-closed mechanism.)

- **Closes:** PLAN-G9, BENCH-T07_framework_choice

## J. Protocols depth: MCP and A2A

**Goal.** Turn Module 12 from three read-and-run tours of a tools-only MCP server and a one-shot A2A peer into the module where the learner connects their own MCP host to their deployed server, hardens that server to the MCP spec, and sees the A2A peer do visibly more. The spec hardening is a 401 with RFC 9728 metadata, a default-deny policy, a per-caller budget, an Origin/Host guard and audited refusals. The learner also uses MCP resources and prompts and runs the same server at Rs 0. The peer publishes real skills, streams its steps, carries a follow-up by contextId, respects a 12-call cap and records who asked. All of it goes into 12.1-12.3 with no new lesson. It reuses gaps plan G4 (section 4.6) and G6-b (section 4.2), with the departures each task states. The hardening is a default-off switch (MCP_HARDEN), so 12.2's deploy finally changes something visible.

### J1. Spec-shaped MCP door, default-deny policy, tool hints, output schemas and refused-call log in services/mcp (gaps plan G4-A, behind MCP_HARDEN, default off)

- **Effort:** L · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **First phase:** P4 · **After:** operations workstream (13.1 owner): agree the replacement wrong-filter cause before merge, tool-failures workstream (10.3): the chat service's tools.py doc_type docstring stays its fix
- **Reuses:** Gaps plan G4 (plan/agents-mcp-gaps-plan-2026-09-29.md section 4.6): the target state, the 12.1 scene row, and the kit-change rows for server.py, tool_policy.py, tool_schemas.py, smoke_mcp.py/smoke_media.py/test_smoke_mcp_setup.py and test_mcp_door.py. ### J2. MCP beyond tools: a documents resource, a cited_answer prompt, and make mcp-local in the kit

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit · **First phase:** P4 · **After:** J1
- **Reuses:** Gaps plan G4 section 4.6: the make mcp-local row (the local corpus, port 8121) and its local-profile proof. Nothing else comes from the plan, because G4 stopped at tools. The library support was checked in the installed fastmcp 3.4.7: the resource() and prompt() decorators in server/server.py, and the proxy's forwarding of resources and prompts (server/providers/proxy.py), which J4's bridge relies on.
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/services/mcp/server.py: @mcp.resource('documind://documents') returns the caller's indexed documents through the same tenant_id-field query as list_documents. Both share one helper, and tools/check_authz.py's lift at :583-652 follows it. @mcp.prompt(name='cited_answer') takes one argument, question, and returns a user message. The message tells the model to call retrieve, answer only from its passages and cite each claim as [n] with file and page, or else say plainly that the corpus cannot answer. The server instructions name both.
  - deploy/services/mcp/tool_policy.py: a 'member' row for the resource and a 'verified' row for the prompt. fastmcp 3.4.7's AuthMiddleware already covers resources and prompts (on_list_resources, on_read_resource, on_list_prompts and on_get_prompt in server/middleware/authorization.py).
  - deploy/mk/protocols.mk (new): whichever J pull request lands first creates this file, adds its deploy/mk/README.md row and puts its targets in .PHONY in deploy/Makefile. J2 adds mcp-local. It runs python -m shared.local_corpus acme, then uvicorn services.mcp.server:app on 127.0.0.1:8121 with DOCUMIND_PROFILE=local and LOCAL_TENANT=acme. It needs langchain-chroma==1.1.0 and chromadb==1.5.9 from services/chat/requirements-local.txt and no Ollama, because the local retrieve is BM25 behind the evidence gate with no model call.
  - deploy/commands/tests/test_mcp_primitives.py (new), added to J1's MCP CI venv. It checks: resources/list names one resource; resources/read returns only the caller's tenant (acme never lists acme_eu_*); prompts/get renders the question; every resource and prompt has a policy row; the local profile serves the tools, the resource and the prompt with no token, behind the Origin guard.
  - python tools/kit_index.py
- **Proof:** test_mcp_primitives.py passes in CI. After make mcp-local, fastmcp's client lists four tools, one resource and one prompt with no token, and curl -H 'Origin: http://evil.example' gets 403. tools/check_authz.py passes. check_lesson still passes for 12.1 and 12.2, whose panels cover tools only.
- **Risks:** Prompts and resources are server-written text that a host shows its model, so the injection workstream's G5 pins should hash them as well as the tools. Host support differs. Gemini CLI documents prompts as slash commands and resources as @server://path, and Claude Desktop attaches both; the proof therefore uses fastmcp's client, and a host run is a captured sample. chromadb is a large install in Cloud Shell.
- **Decision:** Which resource and prompt to add (recommended: documind://documents and cited_answer).
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: the resource becomes a template documind://documents/{tenant} checked against the roster, refusing a multi-roster caller who names no tenant; J4's bridge exposes documind://documents and rewrites it to the pinned tenant; multi-roster cases in tests. The MCP CI venv installs langchain-chroma==1.1.0 and chromadb==1.5.9 and builds the local corpus (or the local case moves to its own step). mk/protocols.mk is created by whichever PR from any workstream lands first; agree its mk/README.md row across workstreams. (after review: J2 issues.)
- **Closes:** GAP-G10, L12.1-W03, L12.1-W09, L12.1-S03, L12.1-S08, PLAN-CP12, BENCH-T26_mcp

  - **12.1**, hero: Reads the corrected promise. *The learner sees:* fastmcp's client named as the one make smoke-mcp uses, ADK's McpToolset named as the mcp library's client of the same protocol (fixing the accuracy slip), and resources, prompts, the door and the Rs 0 lane named.

With the door on, a tokenless call is answered HTTP 401 with resource_metadata before any JSON-RPC runs. resources/read and prompts/get outcomes are listed too, each the kit's own server's answer at build time.
  - **12.1**, Level 1 (step 2) definitions: Reads the table. *The learner sees:* New rows: resource, prompt, annotations, output schema and structuredContent, isError against a JSON-RPC error object, initialize and protocol version, Mcp-Session-Id and stateless, Origin guard, stdio.
  - **12.1**, Level 2 (step 3) Expose: Runs the in-memory listing. *The learner sees:* Capabilities {tools, resources, prompts}; the instructions field and what a host does with it; each tool's hints and output schema; the documind://documents resource and the cited_answer prompt, both excerpted with block().
  - **12.1**, Change it, inside step 5 (Discover): Pastes a ten-line @mcp.tool, notice_end, that wraps calc_tools.notice_end (the calculator A2 binds in the chat brains) into services/mcp/server.py, restarts the local server and re-runs the same discover cell. With MCP_HARDEN on, default deny refuses the call until the learner adds its TOOL_POLICY row; then the call answers. The restore line removes both. *The learner sees:* tools/list naming five tools, the call refused by default deny, then the same call answering with a date computed by code.
  - **12.1**, Level 3 (steps 4-5) Start and Discover: Starts the kit's server locally with MCP_HARDEN=on and sends raw requests. *The learner sees:* HTTP 401 with WWW-Authenticate resource_metadata for a tokenless POST, and the metadata JSON naming accounts.google.com. 403 for Origin: http://evil.example and 421 for Host: evil.example. initialize showing the version the client offered and the one the server chose, with no Mcp-Session-Id because the server is stateless. -32601 for an unknown method, as a JSON-RPC error object. One line that MCP dropped JSON-RPC batching in 2025-06-18.
  - **12.1**, Level 3 (step 6) Invoke, and the stop cell: Calls retrieve, reads the resource, gets the prompt, then lists the tools as the outsider. *The learner sees:* structuredContent validated against retrieve's output schema, acme's documents from the resource, the rendered prompt, and one tool for the outsider. The log shows an mcp_refused line for each refused call beside the mcp_call line.
  - **12.1**, new Level 3 step: the Rs 0 lane: Runs make mcp-local and the same discover and invoke cells with no token. Optionally runs make mcp-connect CLIENT=gemini-cli LOCAL=1 and asks Gemini CLI a question. *The learner sees:* /health reporting profile local, and retrieve answering from the Chroma stand-in through documind_tools' BM25 evidence gate. In the optional step, a model choosing the tool in a real host, at Rs 0 on the server side.
  - **12.1**, Level 5 box, the Verify checklist, What changed on your lane, and the proof: Runs the new checklist rows. *The learner sees:* What is now fixed or switchable, and what the kit does not use and why: sampling, elicitation, roots, list_changed, progress, cancellation and tasks, plus people signing in over OAuth. The new proof reads: tools/list names four with hints and schemas; an unauthenticated call gets 401 with resource_metadata; retrieve answers a local client on both profiles.
- **Proof:** python pagekit/check_lesson.py 12.1 and audit_pages.py 12.1 pass. build.py asserts the 401 with resource_metadata, Origin 403, Host 421, capabilities with resources and prompts, no Mcp-Session-Id, the fifth tool hidden and then listed, and the local profile answering. The live outputs come from J9's rehearsal.
- **Risks:** The Change-it leaves a dirty checkout that would block the next session's git pull, so the step ends with git checkout and the setup's stash note applies. The local server binds 127.0.0.1:8121, as step 4 already does, so only one server can run at a time. Gemini CLI bills its own model tokens, which the learner reads from /stats.

The first is a tools/call the tool refuses: retrieve with a doc_type class no acme document carries (B15's refusal). It comes back as a result with isError true, the ToolError's text in content[0], and no structuredContent. The second is a request the server rejects before any tool runs (an unknown method). ### J4. Per-caller budget, tenant-pinned stdio bridge and make mcp-connect for a real MCP host (gaps plan G4-B)

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit · **First phase:** P4 · **After:** J1, J2
- **Reuses:** Gaps plan G4 section 4.6, the G4-B rows: rate_limit.py, commands/mcp_bridge.py, lane.py mcp-connect, mk/protocols.mk with mcp-connect and mcp-bridge-check, the lesson-7.2.sh line, smoke_mcp checks 5-10 and test_mcp_bridge.py. Also decisions 6 and 7, and section 5's rows for create_proxy and a proxy middleware that injects and refuses an argument. Departures: the budget runs only with MCP_HARDEN=on; the client label is new; the bridge also forwards resources and prompts; the audit grant is optional.
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/services/mcp/rate_limit.py (new): CallBudget counts tool calls per verified email per instance in a fixed 60 s window (MCP_RATE_PER_MIN, default 60; 0 disables it). A refused call gets a readable ToolError naming retry_after, not HTTP 429. It logs mcp_rate_limited and mcp_denied and prunes past 4,096 keys. It sits in J1's middleware list between RefusalLog and AuthMiddleware and is active only with MCP_HARDEN=on. /health adds rate_per_min.
  - deploy/commands/mcp_bridge.py (new): a stdio proxy for desktop and terminal hosts, built on fastmcp's create_proxy. It mints an ID token as documind-ui-sa for the server's URL (gcloud auth print-identity-token --include-email --impersonate-service-account ... --audiences ...), caches it for 45 minutes and re-mints once on a 401. A middleware pins DOCUMIND_TENANT (acme): it injects the tenant argument and refuses any other. The bridge forwards J2's resource and prompt. It sends the host's clientInfo name as X-DocuMind-Client, and server.py logs that as a client label in mcp_call; the label is never trusted.
  - deploy/commands/lane.py: an mcp-connect subcommand that prints the configuration block with absolute paths for CLIENT=gemini-cli (an mcpServers entry in ~/.gemini/settings.json: command, args, env, trust false), claude-desktop, claude-code or cursor. With LOCAL=1 it prints an httpUrl block for make mcp-local. It writes no file.
  - deploy/mk/protocols.mk: mcp-connect and mcp-bridge-check (the bridge answers through fastmcp's stdio client and refuses zeta), both in .PHONY, plus their deploy/mk/README.md row.
  - deploy/commands/deploy-mcp.sh: |MCP_RATE_PER_MIN=${MCP_RATE_PER_MIN:-60}. deploy/Makefile: MCP_RATE_PER_MIN ?= 60, passed through deploy-services.
  - deploy/smoke/smoke_mcp.py: a skipped list and six new checks. 5: a 401 with resource_metadata, or a skip naming Cloud Run. 6: the metadata's resource. 7: hints and schemas. 8: the outsider lists one tool. 9: an optional burst. 10: the bridge answers acme and refuses zeta. Checks 5-9 skip with a line when /health says the door is off.
  - deploy/commands/tests/test_mcp_bridge.py (new), plus budget cases in test_mcp_door.py: the pin, the single re-mint, resource and prompt forwarding, retry_after, and 0 disabling the budget. All run in J1's MCP CI venv.
  - Decision 9 (optional): deploy/terraform/storage.tf gains an mcp_audit member, roles/storage.objectCreator for documind-mcp-sa on the audit bucket beside chat_audit. lesson-7.2.sh sets AUDIT_BUCKET=$PROJECT-audit, so _audit writes its query.submit object. A lane gets the grant at its next make plan and make up.
  - python tools/kit_index.py
- **Proof:** test_mcp_bridge.py and the budget cases pass in CI. On a lane with MCP_HARDEN=on: make smoke-mcp reports 0 failed, and any skip names its door; make mcp-bridge-check is green. The learner prints the block with make mcp-connect CLIENT=gemini-cli TENANT=acme and pastes it. Gemini CLI then gives a cited gratuity answer, the bridge refuses zeta, and an mcp_call line carries client gemini-cli. make smoke-agent and make smoke-media stay green.
- **Risks:** G4 section 5 lists host stdio support as unverified. Gemini CLI's own docs (https://github.com/google-gemini/gemini-cli/blob/main/docs/tools/mcp-server.md) document command, args, env, cwd, httpUrl, headers, trust and includeTools, MCP prompts as slash commands and resources as @server://path. They also document an authProviderType service_account_impersonation, but its targetAudience is described as an IAP OAuth client id. A direct httpUrl connection to this IAM-gated server is therefore unverified and would lose the tenant pin, so the bridge stays the path. The budget is per instance, so up to ten times the limit at ten instances, and agent-sa's budget is shared by every A2A caller and by G1's handoffs. The pin stops the model, not the operator: ui-sa stays on three rosters. A host on a laptop needs gcloud and Python with fastmcp.
- **Decision:** (1) The host for 12.2's live cell (gaps plan decision 6): Gemini CLI from Cloud Shell (recommended: preinstalled, signs in with the learner's Google account, supports prompts and resources), or Claude Desktop / Claude Code. (2) Decision 9: give documind-mcp-sa objectCreator on the audit bucket so 12.2 can read a real audit object (recommended), or keep MCP's record in Cloud Logging and say why. A single-tenant documind-desktop-sa stays deferred, as decision 6 says.
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: mcp-connect added to test_lane.py's pinned set (:25-26) in the same PR; the audit-bucket decision gets a J-local name (J4-audit) and AUDIT_BUCKET is set only with the storage.tf grant; the bridge rewrites resources/read to the pinned tenant, sets X-DocuMind-Client via an httpx event hook reading a ContextVar, and pins through a custom middleware (fastmcp's AuthMiddleware skips stdio); the host step names its runtime (Cloud Shell path; Node.js 20+ elsewhere). (after review: J4 issues.)
- **Closes:** GAP-G10, PRES-08, L10.5-S06, L12.1-W02, L12.1-W03, L12.1-W07, L12.1-W10, L12.2-W01, L12.2-W07, L12.2-S05, PLAN-G4, PLAN-CP12, BENCH-T26_mcp
