- **Reuses:** Gaps plan G4 section 4.6's 12.2 scene row: the metadata read behind Cloud Run with an invoker token, the outsider seeing one tool, retry_after, a desktop client through the bridge, and the gate naming any door that answered first. Also decisions 6 and 7.
- **Kit:**
  - None beyond J1, J2, J4 and J10. build.py runs the kit's server behind its Cloud Run stand-in twice, MCP_HARDEN off then on, with the verifier stubbed, and runs the bridge through fastmcp's stdio client.
- **Lessons:**
  - **12.2**, Level 0 (step 1), the analogy and the panel: Chooses a caller, a tool, a tenant and the door state in the panel. *The learner sees:* Five checks in one picture: Cloud Run IAM, the server's door (401 with RFC 9728 metadata), the default-deny policy, the budget and the roster. Behind them sits the deputy, now named the confused-deputy guard. The server decides for the caller, then asks rag-api as itself, because the MCP spec forbids passing the caller's token through. The office-tower analogy stays.
  - **12.2**, Level 1 (step 2) definitions: Reads the table. *The learner sees:* Rows for protected resource metadata, authorization server, audience and resource indicator, token passthrough, default deny, budget, bridge, stdio and confused deputy. 8.2's 401/403 pair is cited in one line instead of taught again.
  - **12.2**, first hands-on level, the UI: your own agent: Runs make mcp-connect CLIENT=gemini-cli TENANT=acme, pastes the block into ~/.gemini/settings.json, opens gemini and types /mcp. Asks the notice-period question and approves the retrieve call. Then asks for tenant zeta, attaches @documind://documents and reads /stats. *The learner sees:* documind connected with four tools and the cited_answer prompt. The confirmation dialog shows the arguments Gemini chose. A cited answer comes from acme's handbook, and the bridge refuses zeta. /stats gives the turn's tokens, priced in rupees at the host model's list price.
  - **12.2**, Level 2, kit excerpts: Reads the verbatim excerpts. *The learner sees:* All via block(): the door (verifier and RemoteAuthProvider), tool_policy.py, rate_limit.py, the bridge's tenant pin, and lesson-7.2.sh's MCP_HARDEN line and build-if-missing guard. Also list_documents' tenant_id-field query ('a naming convention is not a boundary'), corpus_stats' field filter, _audit with its audit-bucket row, and smoke_mcp.py's outsider check.
  - **12.2**, Level 3, REST. Change it: switch the door on: Runs the doors cell, then make build deploy-services SERVICES=mcp SCRIPTS=commands/deploy-mcp.sh MCP_HARDEN=on, reads the revision back and re-runs the same doors cell. Reads the metadata with an invoker token, runs a burst and runs make smoke-mcp. Rolls back by traffic to the previous revision, then forward with --to-latest. *The learner sees:* Before: an email-less token gets a tool error, and the outsider lists four tools and is refused by the roster. After: the same token gets HTTP 401 with resource_metadata (or Cloud Run's own refusal, named), the outsider lists one tool and is refused by policy, and the 61st call of a burst gets retry_after. The Cloud Run rows do not change: 403 with no token, 401 for a wrong audience, and a token minted for documind-agent's address refused. The cell also times the first call after idle. The rollback and roll-forward leave the latest revision at 100 percent, with the F44 trap explained.
  - **12.2**, Level 4, direct reads: both sides: Reads documind-mcp's and documind-api's logs since step 5, runs make check-callers, and lists the newest MCP audit object. *The learner sees:* mcp_call naming documind-ui-sa with client gemini-cli, and mcp_refused, mcp_denied, mcp_unauthenticated and mcp_rate_limited lines. rag-api's rows name documind-mcp-sa. The live invokers equal the caller graph. With decision 9's grant, the query.submit audit object appears; without it, the page says why none is written.
  - **12.2**, Level 5, Verify checklist, What changed on your lane, footer: Runs the rewritten checklist. *The learner sees:* Ingress all compared with internal and internal-and-load-balancing with IAP. Cold starts and the timeout chain (RAG_TIMEOUT_S 90 under documind-mcp's --timeout=120). Why the audience is checked twice, including Cloud Run custom audiences. content, structuredContent and isError. The propagation options rag-api does not have yet: a delegation header like 10.6's X-DocuMind-Principal, RFC 8693 token exchange, people over OAuth. Costs. The lane box lists the documind-mcp revision with MCP_HARDEN=on and the learner's Gemini CLI settings. The proof reads: make smoke-mcp green with the door on; another tenant refused; a cited answer in your own MCP host.
- **Proof:** check_lesson 12.2 and audit_pages 12.2 pass. build.py asserts the before/after doors rows, the bridge's zeta refusal and each new log line. The live outputs (Gemini CLI, Cloud Run's statuses, the audit object) are captured in J9.
- **Risks:** Cloud Run may answer first; the page states this and the smoke's skips name the door. Gemini CLI versions drift in Cloud Shell, and its tokens bill whichever account it signs in with. After 12.2 the lane runs hardened. 12.3's peer still passes (its token carries an email and agent-sa is on acme), and 16.1's smoke-media passes as ui-sa, but a later make up turns the door off. The host step needs Cloud Shell, or a laptop with gcloud and Python.
- **Decision:** 12.2's manifest proof (the recommended text above, following gaps plan decision 15), and whether the host step is required or optional for learners without Cloud Shell.
- **Revised:** lesson_changes: Amend: show token counts and which account bills them (rupees only with a dated, cited price for the named model and auth mode); manual-checkpoint adaptations for the interactive gemini session and the settings paste; the host step is required with Cloud Shell as the path and a labelled captured sample otherwise; after J10's guard, use make deploy-services alone for the MCP_HARDEN flip; the Change-it is the learner editing the flag in their copy of lesson-7.2.sh (not a canned target); the door and outsider live only here. (after review: J5 issues and PRES-03/PRES-07 notes.)
- **Also:** (final review, for L12.2-W04) Kit, behind MCP_HARDEN: services/mcp/server.py passes the verified caller to rag-api on every call, as X-DocuMind-Principal through documind_tools.retrieve. rag-api accepts that header only when the verified bearer is documind-mcp-sa. This is a DELEGATES rule modelled on services/chat/delegation.py, in a new rag-api module so auth.py's quoted lines stay put. rag-api then runs enforce_membership on the principal instead of the deputy and writes user=<principal> and delegate=documind-mcp-sa on its usage row. From any other caller the header gets a 403 with a logged reason. Tests go in test_mcp_door.py and rag-api's suite. Lesson: Level 4 reads rag-api's row for the same MCP call before the door is on (user documind-mcp-sa) and after (user documind-ui-sa, delegate documind-mcp-sa). It then sends the header straight to rag-api as documind-ui-sa and reads the 403. Level 5's list of options shrinks to RFC 8693 token exchange.
- **Closes:** GAP-G02, GAP-G10, PRES-03, PRES-07, PRES-10, L10.5-S06, L12.1-W02, L12.1-W06, L12.1-S05, L12.1-S07, L12.2-W01, L12.2-W02, L12.2-W03, L12.2-W04, L12.2-W06, L12.2-S01, L12.2-S02, L12.2-S03, L12.2-S04, L12.2-S05, L12.2-S06, L12.2-S07, PLAN-CP12, KIT-11, KIT-12, BENCH-T26_mcp

### J6. A2A peer beyond one synchronous task: an explicit card with three skills, a streaming switch, and a log line naming who asked with the task's tokens and rupees

- **Effort:** L · **Kind:** kit · **First phase:** P4 · **After:** J7, tracing workstream (G3): its planned 12.3 cell and peer instrumentation rebase on this agent.py
- **Reuses:** Gaps plan G6 section 4.2's constraints on agent.py, with one departure: G6 avoided a new log call only because 12.3's build asserted the page's 'logs its start-up only'. G1 section 4.4's note that AgentTool declares the agent's description; the description stays inventory-friendly for the multi-agent workstream. Section 5's rows on to_a2a building a RunConfig per request and on a2a-sdk telemetry.
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/services/agent/agent.py: AGENT_CARD is built in code from SELF_URL with a2a.types (a2a-sdk 1.1.2, already pinned). It carries the name documind_peer, a description, a version from AGENT_VERSION (the deployed commit), one JSONRPC interface, capabilities.streaming = (A2A_STREAMING == 'on'; default off) and text/plain modes. It lists three AgentSkill entries, each with tags and one example: cited_answers (retrieve), corpus_inventory (list_documents, corpus_stats) and processing_cost (calculate_processing_cost). The card is passed as to_a2a(agent, ..., agent_card=AGENT_CARD), so it no longer depends on ADK's tokenless start-up listing. to_a2a accepts agent_card, task_store and runner in the locally installed google-adk 2.7.1; re-read this in the pinned 2.8.0.
  - deploy/services/agent/agent.py: _WhoAsked, a pure-ASGI wrapper, verifies the caller's bearer token with google.oauth2.id_token.verify_oauth2_token for audience SELF_URL. It uses google-auth, which is already pinned, and no kit import and no iap.identity, so 8.1's who_calls list and tools/check_authz.py's no-roster rule still hold. It keeps the email in a ContextVar and never refuses; IAM stays the only door. With MCP_AUTH=none it records 'local'.
  - deploy/services/agent/agent.py: before_model and after_model callbacks on the LlmAgent count model calls and sum usage_metadata, and _mcp_headers counts the tokens it mints. after_agent writes one line: {event: a2a_task, caller, context, task, model_calls, tokens_in, tokens_out, mints, cost_inr}. It prices at gemini-3.6-flash list prices (USD 1.50 / 7.50 per 1M tokens) and USD_INR 85, constants declared in the file. The comments 12.3 quotes move to workshop numbering.
  - deploy/commands/deploy-agent.sh: |A2A_STREAMING=${A2A_STREAMING:-off}|AGENT_VERSION=$GIT_SHA. deploy/Makefile: A2A_STREAMING ?= off, passed through deploy-services.
  - deploy/mk/protocols.mk and deploy/commands/agent-stream.sh (new, with the traffic check from commands/limits-drill.sh): make agent-stream sets A2A_STREAMING=on on documind-agent with gcloud run services update, and STREAMING=off puts it back.
  - deploy/smoke/smoke_agent.py: check 2 requires the skill ids {cited_answers, corpus_inventory, processing_cost} and prints each id with its name. A new check sends message/stream when the card advertises streaming, and otherwise prints a SKIP naming A2A_STREAMING.
  - deploy/commands/tests/test_a2a_peer.py (new) runs in a new agent-pins CI venv (pip install -r deploy/services/agent/requirements.txt), with the scripted Gemini that 12.3's build already uses. It checks: the card's skills, and that each skill's tool is among those the kit's server lists in memory; streaming advertised only with A2A_STREAMING=on, and message/stream refused otherwise; a streamed task's events in order (working events with the function_call and function_response before the artifact, then completed); a follow-up with the same contextId seeing the first turn; tasks/get returning the task; the a2a_task line naming the verified caller and the tokens under both send and stream.
  - tools/check_authz.py: the message at :394 becomes 'the peer verifies its caller only for its log, keeps no roster, and IAM stays its only refusal'; the assertion itself still holds. Then python tools/kit_index.py.
- **Lessons:**
  - **12.3**, build.py:92 and :430-438 (a forced rebuild of the asserts; J8 rewrites the page): Runs the card cell. *The learner sees:* The card's version is the learner's own commit and its skills list three names; the box item 'the card lists no tools' is gone.
- **Proof:** test_a2a_peer.py passes in the agent-pins CI venv, and tools/check_authz.py passes. On a lane, after make build deploy-services SERVICES=agent SCRIPTS=commands/deploy-agent.sh: the card lists three skills with streaming False, and make smoke-agent is green with the skills check and a streaming SKIP. After make agent-stream, the streaming check passes. documind-agent's log has an a2a_task line naming documind-ui-sa with tokens and rupees.
- **Risks:** Several things must be checked in google-adk 2.8.0 and a2a-sdk 1.1.2 source in 12.3's build venv: that a provided card is served as given; that message/stream is refused when the card says streaming False; which JSON-RPC names the binding accepts (A2A 1.0's SendStreamingMessage and GetTask, or the 0.3 names message/stream and tasks/get the smoke already uses); and whether the ContextVar reaches callbacks that the executor runs. Tasks (InMemoryTaskStore) and contextId sessions (InMemorySessionService) live in one instance's memory, with --max-instances=5, so tasks/get or a follow-up can land on another instance and miss. The tracing workstream's G3 planned to keep agent.py at one log call with no request.headers, and to prove 'documind-ui-sa in no span and no row'. That proof's wording must change, because this line names the caller on purpose.
- **Decision:** (1) Whether the peer records who asked (recommended). (2) Streaming as a default-off switch that 12.3 turns on (recommended). (3) The card's version as the deployed commit.
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: cost_inr prices candidates plus thoughts as output (test holds the constants equal to shared/prices.py); _WhoAsked passes lifespan scopes through, /health stays on the inner app, a2a_task logged from a finally block (or failed tasks named as having no line); 12.3 build.py:80 serve-excerpt assert and the 'agent.py imports' line join J6's rebuilds. G6-b from J7 only. (after review: J6 issues.)
- **Revised:** effort revised to L after review (Scope.)
- **Closes:** GAP-G10, PRES-08, L12.3-W02, L12.3-W04, L12.3-W05, L12.3-W09, L12.3-W11, L12.3-S01, L12.3-S03, L12.3-S04, L12.3-S07, L12.3-S08, BENCH-T27_a2a

### J7. Cap the A2A peer at 12 model calls and drill it live (gaps plan G6-b), with 10.3's two sentences rebuilt

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **First phase:** P0
- **Reuses:** Gaps plan G6 section 4.2: the 12.3 scene row, the agent.py and lesson-8.4.sh:21 rows, and the smoke_agent health requirement. Section 5's row that to_a2a turns LlmCallsLimitExceededError into a failed task. commands/limits-drill.sh's trap-and-traffic pattern. Departure: the over-cap task is shown live; section 5 marks it unverified and G6 planned it offline only. Land J7 first, because the multi-agent workstream's G1 needs the cap as a hard prerequisite.
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/services/agent/agent.py: os.environ.setdefault('ADK_MAX_LLM_CALLS', '12') after MODEL, and max_llm_calls on /health. The RunConfig that to_a2a builds per request reads it; ADK's default is 500. No new import and no log call.
  - deploy/commands/deploy-agent.sh: --set-env-vars appends |ADK_MAX_LLM_CALLS=12.
  - deploy/smoke/smoke_agent.py: /health must show max_llm_calls 12, and a DOCUMIND_EXPECT_FAILED mode serves the drill.
  - deploy/commands/agent-drill.sh (new, modelled on commands/limits-drill.sh) and make agent-drill in deploy/mk/protocols.mk. If J7 lands first it creates that file, its deploy/mk/README.md row and its .PHONY entry. The drill sets ADK_MAX_LLM_CALLS=1 on documind-agent, checks that the latest revision takes all the traffic and sends one task expecting state failed. It restores 12 from a trap and sends the task again, expecting completed.
  - deploy/commands/tests/test_smoke_agent_expectations.py: the health and failed-mode cases. Then python tools/kit_index.py.
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.3**, build.py:109 (the assert that agent.py has no max_llm_calls), parts/b.html:63 and parts/c.html:49 (a forced rebuild in the tool-failures workstream's lesson): Reads the drill's paragraph and the Level 5 box. *The learner sees:* 'The A2A peer caps a task at 12 model calls (lesson 12.3)' in place of 'publishes no cap yet, so ADK's own default of 500 applies'.
- **Proof:** test_smoke_agent_expectations.py passes. check_lesson and audit_pages pass for 10.3 and 12.3. On a lane: make build deploy-services SERVICES=agent SCRIPTS=commands/deploy-agent.sh, after which make smoke-agent shows max_llm_calls 12. make agent-drill ends one task failed at a cap of 1, restores 12, and the same task then completes.
- **Risks:** The drill caps the real peer for about a minute; the trap restores it. 12 is the chat service's number, not one measured on the peer. Whether the failed task's status message names the limit stays unverified until the drill runs. 10.3 belongs to the tool-failures workstream, and this pull request changes two of its sentences and one assert.
- **Revised:** lesson_changes: Add to 10.3: the PEER_KEYS stand-in parses 12 from agent.py's setdefault; build.py:299's expected make limits line becomes 'model calls a task 12'; the lane proof adds 'make limits shows the peer's 12'. J7 is the single owner of G6-b (B14, C1, K10 consume it). (after review: J7 issue (verified 10.3 build.py:292-299).)
- **Also:** (final review) The 10.3 make-limits assert is at build.py:298. J7 owns the peer's call cap; C1, B14 and K10 refer to it.
- **Closes:** GAP-G10, L10.3-S05, L12.3-W02, L12.3-W07, PLAN-G6, BENCH-T27_a2a

### J8. Lesson 12.3 rewritten: a card with skills, a task streamed step by step, its lifecycle and context, who asked, what it cost, and the cap

- **Effort:** L · **Kind:** lesson · **First phase:** P4 · **After:** J1, J6, J7, J10
- **Reuses:** Gaps plan G6 section 4.2's 12.3 scene (the cap), and G1 section 4.4's 12.3 footer and lane-box sentence (chat-sa's binding is the one 12.4 uses). G3 section 4.5's third 12.3 cell is left to the tracing workstream, and G5 section 4.7's 12.3 sentence (tool_filter pins) to the injection workstream.
- **Kit:**
  - None beyond J1, J6, J7 and J10. build.py serves the kit's agent.py with the image's pins and a scripted Gemini, and captures the stream, the tasks/get states, a contextId follow-up and a failed task at a cap of 1.
- **Lessons:**
  - **12.3**, Level 0 (step 1) and the follow-one-task panel: Chooses who sends the task, what it asks, how it arrives (blocking, streamed or polled) and a follow-up. *The learner sees:* A task as a lifecycle (submitted, working, completed or failed), the card as a contract of skills, and contextId threading tasks into a conversation. Each hop is what the kit's peer and server did at build time.
  - **12.3**, Level 1 (step 2) definitions: Reads the table. *The learner sees:* Rows for contextId, task states, artifact against history, capabilities and skill. The A2A 1.0 method names (SendMessage, SendStreamingMessage, GetTask) appear beside the 0.3 names the smoke sends (message/send, message/stream, tasks/get), reconciled.
  - **12.3**, Level 2 (step 3) permissions: Runs the permissions cell. *The learner sees:* The peer's INSTRUCTION and the card's three skills, both via block(), and the cap of 12. A timeout chain read from the kit's files: the cells' 180 s, the peer's --timeout=300, McpToolset's 120 s and sse_read_timeout of 300 s, documind-mcp's --timeout=120 and RAG_TIMEOUT_S 90, shrinking inward.
  - **12.3**, Level 3 (steps 4-5): the card and the gate: Reads the card with and without a token and runs make smoke-agent. *The learner sees:* Three skills with ids and names, the version equal to the learner's commit, and streaming False. The ids the smoke prints match the names the card shows. smoke/smoke_agent.py is named, and its skills check and outsider check are excerpted.
  - **12.3**, Level 3. Change it: streaming: Runs the stream cell, then make agent-stream, re-reads the card and re-runs the same stream cell. *The learner sees:* Before: the request is refused as an unsupported capability. After: streaming True, and events printed with time offsets as they arrive: submitted, working, the call to retrieve with the arguments Gemini chose, the result with its citation count, the artifact, completed.
  - **12.3**, Level 4, new: lifecycle and context: Sends a non-blocking task and polls it with GetTask, then sends 'And for a fixed-term employee?' with the first task's contextId. *The learner sees:* The states observed while polling, and a follow-up answered from the earlier turn. A note explains that tasks and sessions live in one instance's memory, as the ADK brain's did in 11.3.
  - **12.3**, Level 4 (step 6): one task traced: Reads the peer's log beside documind-mcp's and rag-api's. *The learner sees:* An a2a_task line naming documind-ui-sa with the task's model calls, tokens, token mints and measured rupees. documind-mcp still sees documind-agent-sa, and rag-api still sees documind-mcp-sa. A decoded token shows its one-hour lifetime.
  - **12.3**, Level 4, new: the cap: Runs make agent-drill. *The learner sees:* A task ending failed at a cap of 1, the cap restored to 12, and the same task completing.
  - **12.3**, Level 5, Verify checklist, What changed on your lane, footer: Runs the new checklist rows. *The learner sees:* Identity propagation options: attribution at the peer's door, a card's security schemes with per-user tokens, and RFC 8693 token exchange. The rewritten gaps box: no kit agent calls the peer yet (12.4); no span crosses the hops yet (13.1); the peer takes tool metadata verbatim (12.5 pins it); tasks and sessions live in instance memory; push notifications, cancel, input-required and the authenticated extended card are unused; Agent Engine is named as a managed host. The proof reads: the card lists its skills; a streamed task shows its tool calls and completes; a task over the peer's cap ends failed.
- **Proof:** check_lesson 12.3 and audit_pages 12.3 pass. build.py asserts the three skills, the stream's event order, the GetTask states, the contextId follow-up, the failed task at a cap of 1, and the a2a_task line naming the caller. The live outputs are captured in J9.
- **Risks:** Live tool choice differs from the build's scripted Gemini; J9 captures a real run. The A2A method names must match what a2a-sdk 1.1.2 accepts. A follow-up or a GetTask can land on another instance. The drill caps the real peer briefly. The page grows, so Level 4 keeps one cell per behaviour.
- **Decision:** 12.3's manifest proof text (recommended above).
- **Revised:** lesson_changes: Amend: the timeout table presents the kit's two inversions (cells 180 s vs peer --timeout=300; McpToolset 120 s equals documind-mcp's --timeout=120) as findings, or the kit numbers are aligned first; the gaps box names G1/G5 (or their workstreams) until 12.4/12.5 are in the manifest; Level 2 opens with a streamed (or captured streamed) task; one task needs two tools (corpus_stats then retrieve). (after review: J8 issues and PRES-10/L12.3-W08/L12.3-S06 notes.)
- **Also:** (final review, for L12.3-W09) Level 4 step 6 ('one task traced') also prints the completed task's artifact beside its history. The history holds each function_call with its arguments and each function_response in full, and the zeta refusal arrives without documind-agent-sa's email (J1). build.py asserts that no email address appears anywhere in the task. The Level 5 box replaces C6's kept line 'The history carries raw results to the caller' with a stated design choice: A2A returns the task history, every tool call and its full result, to the caller Cloud Run admitted. The answer is the artifact, a caller that needs only the answer reads that, and 12.5 treats the history as an outward channel.
- **Also:** (final review, for L12.1-W06) (1) Kit: services/mcp/server.py's docstring stops calling calculate_processing_cost 'the tool-chaining demo (7.3)' and names 12.3's chain instead. (2) 12.3 Level 4: the two-tool task becomes a dependent chain on the kit's own pinned tools, in which the second call's argument is read from the first call's result. Example: 'Which document type in our corpus holds the most chunks? Search only that type for what it says about notice periods.' The learner sees corpus_stats, then retrieve with doc_type equal to the largest by_doc_type entry. The build asserts that argument on the scripted run, and J9 captures the real model's two calls. (3) Level 5: one sentence saying the MCP server and the peer stay read-only by gaps decision 3, so a remote caller's model can never trigger a side effect, and that the Act's acting tool is 10.2's withdraw_document behind a second person's approval (D1, D3).
- **Closes:** GAP-G02, GAP-G10, PRES-03, PRES-07, PRES-09, PRES-10, L12.1-W06, L12.3-W02, L12.3-W04, L12.3-W05, L12.3-W07, L12.3-W08, L12.3-W09, L12.3-W11, L12.3-S01, L12.3-S02, L12.3-S03, L12.3-S04, L12.3-S05, L12.3-S06, L12.3-S07, L12.3-S08, KIT-11, BENCH-T27_a2a

### J9. Rehearse 12.1-12.3 on a live lane, replace stand-in outputs with labelled captured samples, and update Module 12's proofs, plan rows and statuses

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** process · **First phase:** P6 · **After:** J3, J5, J8
- **Reuses:** Gaps plan section 3's after-every-kit-pull-request checklist and decision 16's publish rule. The roadmap's rule that a scene is done once its proof has been rehearsed on a live lane.
- **Kit:**
  - course-manifest.json: the 12.1-12.3 proofs, rewritten per gaps plan decision 15. Status moves from Drafted to In review, and to Done at the author's call.
  - plan/course-plan-v5-story-2026-09-22.md rows 192-194: 12.1's target becomes make mcp-local; 12.2 gains the connected host; 12.3 gains streaming and the cap. plan/course-roadmap-v5-2026-09-22.md rows 137-139, with its .html and .pdf.
  - deploy/workshop_demos/module_12/lesson_12_1 to lesson_12_3 regenerated with python tools/build_workshop_demos.py, then --check, tools/check_workshop_demos.py and deploy/workshop_demos/tests/run_tests.py. Each changed digest is reviewed in tools/workshop_demo_reviewed.json, and live verification is recorded for the rehearsed demos.
  - python tools/leakscan.py over the captured outputs, then python tools/publish_learner.py --check. The publish itself happens only with the author's explicit go-ahead.
- **Lessons:**
  - **12.1, 12.2, 12.3**, every expected-output window of a live cell, and the setup claim on these pages: Compares their own run with the page. *The learner sees:* Outputs captured on a live lane, with lane values as placeholders, labelled as captured on a live lane with the note that the learner's words and numbers will differ. The setup's claim that every output shown is what the lane prints becomes true for Module 12; stand-in labels remain only on the offline panels.
- **Proof:** Each Module 12 proof is observed once on a live lane and recorded in the pull request with the lane's run log; the pages carry placeholders. check_lesson and audit_pages pass for 12.1-12.3, and the demo checks and leakscan pass.
- **Risks:** Real values could leak into pages, which leakscan guards. Gemini's words differ from run to run, so samples are labelled. Facts the stand-ins assumed may prove wrong on the lane: whether Cloud Run admits an email-less token, the metadata read, the A2A method names, and Gemini CLI's handling of prompts and resources. Any such finding must flow back into J3, J5 and J8 before merge. The rehearsal spends a few rupees, read from make usage and the a2a_task lines.
- **Decision:** Which lane runs the rehearsal, and whether the captured samples replace the build's stand-in outputs (CLAUDE.md allows 'a run on a live lane') or sit beside them.
- **Revised:** kit_changes: The live rehearsal is a pre-merge step of each lesson's PR (J3, J5, J8) on its branch with kit changes deployed; J9 keeps statuses and plan/roadmap rows. Demo regeneration and digest review move into J1, J3, J5, J6, J7, J8, J10. Captured transcripts are fixtures beside build.py; build.py substitutes placeholders, asserts the same shapes, and leakscan runs over them. (after review: J9 issues.)
- **Closes:** PRES-02, L12.1-W05, L12.2-W05, L12.3-W08, PLAN-CP12, PLAN-RM-Done

### J10. Kit hygiene Module 12 cites: a deploy that builds when it must, a caller-graph check learners can run, a real isolation test for the peer, and the script names

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **First phase:** P0
- **Reuses:** Gaps plan section 3's rule that kit comments say 'workshop lesson N.M', and G4's lesson-7.2.sh comment row. No gaps-plan section covers these defects; they come from the 12.2 and 12.3 boxes themselves.

  - deploy/commands/check_callers.py (new) and make check-callers in deploy/mk/protocols.mk. It reads the caller graph in deploy/terraform/sa.tf (the table at :102-106) and each script's invoker loop, then each service's live roles/run.invoker members (gcloud run services get-iam-policy). It prints 'the same' or DIFFERENT per service and removes nothing.
  - deploy/commands/tests/test_check_callers.py (new, against a fake gcloud) and deploy/commands/tests/test_agent_isolation.py (new, stdlib only: agent.py imports nothing from shared or services, and its Dockerfile copies only services/agent).

A rename touches the Makefile SCRIPTS (:184-186), the 12.1-12.3 builds, 13.3 build.py:95-134, terraform/sa.tf's caller-graph comments, tools/check_authz.py and the index via kit_index.py. Keeping them means one sentence on each 12.x page saying they carry the earlier course's numbering.

The box items 'the deploy block does not build' and 'bindings are only ever added' are replaced by the build guard and the live check, and the comparison with lesson-12.8.sh is gone.

On a lane, make check-callers prints 'the same' for documind-mcp and documind-agent, and make deploy-services SERVICES=mcp SCRIPTS=commands/deploy-mcp.sh deploys a new commit without a prior make build. check_lesson passes for 12.2 and 12.3, and for 13.3 if the scripts are renamed. No page under lessons/12-protocols names check_authz.py or check_auth_wiring.py.
