## K. Reliability, streaming and LangGraph depth

**Goal.** Make the agent's resilience and LangGraph's power visible on the learner's own lane. The kit gains six things. (1) Recoverable tool errors: a refused argument names its fix, and the token mint becomes data. (2) Retries with backoff and a circuit breaker on the one retrieve(). (3) A labelled fallback to the direct brain on a model error. (4) Live drills for a dead search, a broken model and every turn limit. (5) A thread that heals after an interrupted turn and answers 409 while a turn runs. (6) POST /v1/chat/stream behind the Desk's chat door, with Streamlit showing each step and token. Lessons 10.2, 10.3, 10.4, 11.2 and 11.3 then teach recovery with a real Gemini instead of '(the model explains what it read)'. They also teach LangGraph's rendering, stream modes, custom state and reducers, fan-out, subgraphs, time travel and thread repair on the kit's own graphs. The workstream reuses the gaps plan's G6 and G8 designs and adds what G1-G9 never planned: streaming, LangGraph depth, retries and fallback.

### K1. Make tool errors recoverable: a filter that empties the pool becomes an argument error the model can correct, the token mint becomes data, and refusals get a kind

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **First phase:** P1
- **Reuses:** Gaps plan G8 section 4.1: the one error contract (status 'error' marks a refusal, a payload error is data) and the SYSTEM-sentence pattern. G6 section 4.2: the MCP server pops a new key so its contract holds (server.py:164). Departs from G8's target state ('any other exception, such as the token mint, fails the turn'). A turn lost to a 500 is worse than a model saying search is unavailable, and K3's fallback cannot help because the direct brain mints the same token.
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/services/chat/tools.py: search() raises ToolException when doc_type is not 'all' and rag-api reports pool 0 ('doc_type X matched none of your organisation's documents, so nothing was searched. Call retrieve again with doc_type all.'). That is an argument refusal under G8's contract. retrieve gets handle_tool_error = True, as calculate_processing_cost has at tools.py:190; otherwise ToolNode re-raises the exception and the LangGraph turn fails. retrieve's doc_type docstring line (tools.py:107) says to filter only when the person asks for one type, and that a type with no documents is refused.
  - deploy/shared/documind_tools.py: the ID-token mint moves inside the try in retrieve() and _passages() (today line 149, before the try at 156). A mint failure becomes the same payload error as an unreachable rag-api: {'error': 'document retrieval is unavailable', 'reason': 'token'}. out['pool'] carries rag-api's stages.pool (RAGResponse.stages, services/rag-api/schemas.py:49). /v1/passages carries it under usage.stages, so the detection survives the agent-value workstream's adapter move.
  - deploy/services/mcp/server.py: pops 'pool' beside 'usage' before return out, so the MCP results that 12.1 and 12.2 show are unchanged (G6's precedent at server.py:164).
  - deploy/services/chat/brains.py: SYSTEM keeps its sentences (10.2's build asserts 'If a tool refuses, say so plainly and explain what') and gains two more. The first says: correct a rejected argument from the error and call the tool again once. The second says: when search is unavailable or times out, say so and answer nothing from memory. _summary keeps its four keys. Each brain's answer() adds refusal_kinds (policy for a BLOCKED or approval name, argument for a validation error or the tool's own ToolException, unknown_tool for a name not in TOOLS) and tool_args (each call's arguments, with free text reduced to its length and an 8-character hash). ADK builds both from its events.
  - deploy/services/chat/agent.py: the response gains refusal_kinds, and the chat row literal names refusal_kinds and tool_args one by one, because the page guards read the row by regex.
  - deploy/services/frontend/chat.py: the agent caption names the kind, for example 'refused: retrieve (argument)'.
  - deploy/commands/tests/test_chat_brains.py: new cases. A filtered empty pool is an argument refusal in all three agent brains, and the next call with 'all' answers. A failing mint is a payload error with refusals [] in all three brains (today it raises). The row's tool_args never holds the query text. The MCP retrieve's keys are unchanged.
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.3**, Level 2, step 3 (five failures); ships in K12's 10.3 PR: Runs the existing offline cell, now looped over the langchain, langgraph and adk brains. It has two more failures: the token mint raising, and two calls in one model message where one is cut at its budget. *The learner sees:* A brain-by-failure table of result status and refusal kind, with the full error text instead of today's 72-character cut, so 'Input should be a valid integer' shows. The mint failure comes back as data with refusals []. For the parallel pair, the turn waits only for the call that answers within budget, the cut call is a payload error, and only the answered call's citations are numbered.
  - **10.3**, New Level 3 step 'Recovery with a real model', replacing step 6's direct retrieve() call; ships in K12's PR: Over REST, asks each agent brain on the lane 'Search only the invoices: what is the total payable on invoice INV-2026-0412?', then reads the chat rows' tool_args. Then runs the step's local cell once: the kit's LangChain brain built on the machine with build_llm() at location global, against the lane's rag-api as documind-ui-sa, with the same question. *The learner sees:* For each brain: tool_calls ['retrieve', 'retrieve'], refusals ['retrieve'], refusal_kinds ['argument'] and a cited answer. The model read the refusal and searched again with 'all'. The rows show doc_type invoice, then all, so the filter is finally logged. Gemini's words are captured once on a lane as a labelled sample, and the checks are structural.
  - **10.3**, Same step, a 'Change it' inside the level: In the clone, replaces the ToolException's text with 'invalid argument', re-runs the step's local cell, compares the two runs, then restores the file with git checkout. *The learner sees:* Whether the model still recovers when the error no longer names the fix. Typically it makes one refused call and says it could not search, where before it made two calls and gave a cited answer. The lesson: an error's text is part of the tool's contract with the model.
  - **10.3**, Level 5, 'What the kit does not do yet' box: Reads the box. *The learner sees:* The items for arguments logged nowhere, refusals mixing policy and mistakes, filters the corpus cannot honour, and the mint outside the try are gone. The build's asserts at build.py:103, :107 and :112 flip with the kit.
- **Proof:** test_chat_brains.py passes in CI's chat-pins step with the new cases. 10.3's build runs the extended offline cell on the kit and asserts the kinds, the full error text and the mint-as-data line. On a lane, make smoke-chat stays at 6 passed, and the invoice question gives two retrieves with refusal kind argument and a cited answer from each agent brain (captured once). check_lesson and audit_pages pass for 10.3 and for whatever check_lesson names among 10.1, 10.4, 12.1, 12.2 and 18.4.
- **Risks:** Gemini may skip the invoice filter even when asked; the page explains both outcomes. A lane already relabelled by 10.6's doc-types will not empty the pool; 10.3 assumes the unknown state, as its step 6 does today, and doc-types RESET=1 restores it for a rehearsal. Every filtered empty pool now costs one more model call. The agent-value workstream also edits SYSTEM, so merge both sets of sentences in one PR. tool_args must stay free of personal text: only an allow-list (doc_type, top_k, processing_type) is logged by value. Quoted lines move in 10.1, 10.3 and 10.4 (tools.py, brains.py), in 10.1, 10.3 and 18.4 (documind_tools.py) and in 12.1 and 12.2 (server.py). The 10.3 scene content ships in K12's PR.
- **Decision:** First, how to treat a filter that empties the pool. Options: (a) an argument refusal the model corrects (recommended: visible, teachable and model-driven); (b) code retries once without the filter (cheaper, but invisible); (c) leave it as data. Second, whether a mint failure becomes data (recommended) or stays turn-ending as a 503 instead of a 500.
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: raise only when cache_hit is 'none' and 'pool' is in stages (test a cache hit); 10.3's venv adds google-adk==2.8.0 with its install time stated (or reuses limits-check's venv); the Change-it is captured on a lane before writing (pick a contrast the error text decides if Gemini recovers anyway); the cut call costs its full budget in wall time (the build prints the measured time); tool_args hashes free text with an HMAC per-service secret or logs only length; server.py pops 'reason' and 'pool' and the MCP error path is tested. (after review: K1 issues.)
- **Also:** (final review) No tool_args on the chat row (decision 3: log rows carry no arguments). The 10.3 step that read them moves to B5's Change-it, which reads arguments from make agent-turn.
- **Also:** (final review) In shared/documind_tools.py the token mint is at :149 and its try at :155.
- **Closes:** GAP-G11, L10.1-S03, L10.3-W01, L10.3-W04, L10.3-S04, L10.3-S06, L10.3-S07, L10.3-S08, L10.3-S09, BENCH-T13_tool_failures_reliability

### K2. Retry rag-api with backoff and add a circuit breaker on the one retrieve(); measure the model client's own retries

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **First phase:** P1 · **After:** K1
- **Reuses:** Nothing from G1-G9: G6 names 'two attempts instead of six mean less tolerance of 429 bursts' only as a risk. It keeps G6 section 4.2's rule that a tool call is cut at its budget, and uses G1 section 4.4's CHAT_EXTRA_ENV switch.
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/shared/documind_tools.py: new setting RAG_RETRIES, default 0 (today's behaviour). When set, retrieve() retries once on a connection error or a 429/502/503/504 from rag-api. It never retries a read timeout, because that call may still be running and billing. Backoff is RAG_BACKOFF_S 0.5 x 2^attempt with full jitter, and is skipped when it would start after RAG_TIMEOUT_S. Each retry is logged ('retrieve retry 1 after 503 (backoff 0.62s)') and counted in out['retries']. New setting RAG_BREAKER, default off. Three consecutive transport failures within 30 s open the breaker for 15 s; while it is open, retrieve() returns the payload error at once with circuit_open true. After 15 s one probe call is let through (half-open).
  - deploy/services/chat/tools.py and deploy/services/chat/limits.py: search() records retries and a circuit-open call on the turn's Meter (tool_retries, circuit_open), and Meter.summary() and row() carry them. deploy/services/chat/agent.py names both in the row literal.
  - deploy/services/mcp/server.py: pops 'retries' beside 'usage' and 'pool'.
  - deploy/Makefile and deploy/commands/deploy-chat.sh: RETRIES ?= off. When on, it appends |RAG_RETRIES=1|RAG_BREAKER=on to CHAT_EXTRA_ENV (G1's pattern after Makefile:353), so a later make deploy-services keeps it.
  - deploy/commands/tests/test_rag_retry.py (new; stdlib, CI's shared interpreter): a stub rag-api answering 503 once and then 200 gives one retry and an answer; a read timeout is never retried; the breaker opens after three failures and the fourth call returns in under 50 ms; a retry never outlives timed_tool_call's budget.
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.3**, Level 2, step 3; ships in K12's PR: Runs an offline retry cell: the step's stub rag-api answers 503 once, then goes down. *The learner sees:* The retry line with its backoff, and the answer that followed. Then three slow failures, the breaker opening, and the fourth call returning at once with circuit_open, which saves the turn's time budget during an outage.
  - **10.3**, Level 3, 'A turn that will not stop'; ships in K12's PR: Runs an offline cell that points google-genai's own client (API-key mode, no credential) at a local stub answering 503 and then 200, with the two attempts the kit sets (CHAT_MODEL_ATTEMPTS). *The learner sees:* The client's retry and the measured gap between the two attempts. This is the 'about 2 s of backoff' in 10.3's deadline sum, now seen rather than stated.
  - **10.3**, Level 5, 'Why failures are handled this way': Reads why only an idempotent read is retried (never a timed-out call, never a write), and why the breaker is per worker. *The learner sees:* The kit's own precedents: 4.1's Pub/Sub backoff and 17.1's ask_pairs retries.
- **Proof:** test_rag_retry.py passes offline and in CI. 10.3's build runs both cells and asserts one retry with backoff under 1 s, the breaker open on the fourth call in under 50 ms, and two client attempts. On a lane after make deploy-services RETRIES=on, make smoke-chat stays green and healthy rows show tool_retries [].
- **Risks:** A retry after a 5xx that rag-api had in fact completed bills twice. Breaker state is per gunicorn worker (two per instance, Dockerfile:17) and per instance. The shared retrieve() also serves the MCP server and the Desk; they are unchanged unless their own env sets the variables. 10.1, 10.3 and 18.4 quote documind_tools.py, so they need rebuilds. The client-retry cell must match google-genai 2.22.0's behaviour. Lands after K1 because both edit documind_tools.py.
- **Decision:** Whether retries are a switch defaulting off (recommended, per the new-switches rule) or a fix with RAG_RETRIES=1 by default. Whether the breaker ships at all (recommended: ship it, off by default, and teach it in 10.3).
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: every switch line appends to CHAT_EXTRA_ENV and is made sticky (read the live service's values or a lane settings file the Makefile includes); list the G1 interaction in K15; retrieve() takes a deadline or may-continue callable (the chat passes meter.left() and limits.may_commit) and a test proves no retry starts after abandonment; the Desk is affected (decide whether Desk retrieval may retry); server.py pops every new key; the page says 'circuit breaker' and contrasts it with 13.3's budget breaker. (after review: K2 issues.)
- **Closes:** GAP-G11, L10.3-S03, L10.3-S12, BENCH-T12_durable_execution, BENCH-T13_tool_failures_reliability

### K3. Fall back to the direct brain on a model error, labelled, with the thread kept whole; make fault-drill trips a dead search and a broken model on the lane

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **First phase:** P1 · **After:** K1
- **Reuses:** The Desk's existing fallback (desk_graph.py:266-279, tested in test_desk.py:1710). G6 section 4.2's limits-drill.sh: trap restore, latest-revision check and the DOCUMIND_EXPECT_STOP pattern. repair_thread (limits.py:378).
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/services/chat/agent.py: new switch CHAT_FALLBACK, default off. chat() wraps brain.answer(). On an exception that limits.is_model_error() recognises, it repairs the agent thread, answers with DirectBrain under the same Meter, and returns brain 'direct' with fallback {from, reason}. The row literal names fallback_from and fallback_reason. Any other exception still raises a 500, and with the switch off test_other_model_errors_still_fail_the_turn stays green.
  - deploy/services/chat/limits.py: is_model_error(exc) beside is_timeout. It recognises Vertex and google-genai API errors by class name: ResourceExhausted, ServiceUnavailable, InternalServerError, NotFound, ClientError, ServerError and ChatGoogleGenerativeAIError. repair_thread(graph, config, as_node, final=None) gains a final message, so the fallback answer, not STOP_ANSWER, ends the agent thread and the next agent turn reads it.
  - deploy/services/chat/brains.py: LangChainBrain and LangGraphBrain expose repair(config, final). AdkBrain's repair does nothing, because its sessions are in memory.
  - deploy/services/frontend/chat.py: the caption reads, for example, 'answered by direct (fallback from langchain: NotFound)'.
  - deploy/Makefile: FALLBACK ?= off; when on, it appends |CHAT_FALLBACK=on to CHAT_EXTRA_ENV.
  - deploy/commands/fault-drill.sh (new) and a fault-drill target in deploy/mk/agents.mk. FAULT=search_timeout sets RAG_TIMEOUT_S=0.5 on documind-chat. FAULT=model_error sets CHAT_MODEL to a model name that does not exist, plus CHAT_FALLBACK=on. Each run checks that the latest revision takes all traffic, runs the smoke expecting the fault, and restores the service from a trap, as limits-drill.sh does.
  - deploy/smoke/smoke_chat.py: new mode DOCUMIND_EXPECT_FAULT. For search_timeout: a 200, retrieve called, refusals [], no citations, no [n] marker. For model_error: brain direct, fallback.from naming the brain asked for, and citations present.
  - deploy/commands/tests/test_chat_fallback.py (new; CI's chat-pins step): a scripted model raising a NotFound or 429 class gives a 200 from direct with fallback set, the agent thread whole with the direct answer last, and the next agent turn answering. With the switch off, the same error is a 500.
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.3**, New Level 3 step 'Faults on your lane'; ships in K12's PR: Runs make fault-drill FAULT=search_timeout, then reads rag-api's rows for those searches. *The learner sees:* Each agent brain answering HTTP 200 with tool_calls ['retrieve'], refusals [], no citations, and its own reasoned 'search is unavailable' reply from Gemini. rag-api's rows show the abandoned searches answered and billed, though no turn used them. The restored smoke passes.
  - **10.3**, Same step: Runs make fault-drill FAULT=model_error. *The learner sees:* Every agent brain answered by direct, labelled with the brain it fell back from and the error class. The Level 1 UI caption shows the same. The 'before' case, a 500, is shown offline from the kit's test, so the drill never breaks the lane twice.
  - **10.3**, Level 5: Reads the Desk's own fallback (desk_graph._agent_answer, desk_graph.py:266-279) beside the chat's new one. *The learner sees:* The pattern the chat brains now follow: a model error is not the turn's fault, so the floor answers and the row says so.
- **Proof:** test_chat_fallback.py passes in CI. On a lane, make fault-drill FAULT=model_error prints a PASS fallback line for each agent brain, then the restored make smoke-chat's 6 passed. FAULT=search_timeout prints a PASS line for each agent brain with citations 0 and refusals []. Both runs are captured once. check_lesson and audit_pages pass for 10.3.
- **Risks:** A fallback hides a broken model configuration unless the row, the caption and a later alert on the fallback share say so. The direct answer lacks the agent's tools, so it is labelled. Both the failed calls and the direct call land on the row. The drill changes the real documind-chat for a minute or two and must restore it. The exact error class Vertex returns for an unknown model must be confirmed on a lane. ADK raises google.genai errors, not LangChain's. K1 must land first so that a mint failure never reaches the fallback.
- **Decision:** Whether to fall back on model errors only (recommended), or also after model_calls and recursion_limit stops when time and budget remain (G11's suggestion; it costs one more rag-api answer after the stop). Which lesson turns CHAT_FALLBACK on (recommended: 10.3, with make deploy-services FALLBACK=on).
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: fall back only while meter.left() is above a floor, cap the fallback's retrieve timeout at the time left, and extend the time-bound test; ADK's fallback answer is appended to the session as an event (or the gap named); fault-drill.sh gains HOLD=1 (or a tagged revision for the UI step); the page says an unknown model name proves only the plumbing. (after review: K3 issues.)
- **Closes:** GAP-G11, L10.3-W01, L10.3-S10, L10.3-S12, BENCH-T13_tool_failures_reliability

### K4. Heal a thread at the next turn's start, refuse a second turn while one runs, and teach repair_thread

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **First phase:** P1 · **After:** K3
- **Reuses:** G6 section 4.2's repair_thread and its verification ('a repaired thread after a forced recursion limit'); section 5's 'GraphRecursionError is a RecursionError; update_state repairs a thread' (confirmed). Departs from G6's scope, which repairs only after GraphRecursionError. A scratch probe on langgraph 1.2.11 showed that a stream closed after the agent's tool call saves next ('tools',) with an open call. The next turn then starts from the input and sends the model Human, AI-with-call, Human. Nothing in the kit heals that thread today.
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/services/chat/limits.py: new heal(graph, config, as_node), which reads get_state(config) once. A thread paused at an interrupt (state.interrupts, the approval workstream's G2) is left alone. A thread with open tool calls, state.next set and a latest checkpoint younger than TURN_DEADLINE_S + 20 s raises TurnInFlight. In an older one, each open call is answered with {'error': 'the previous turn stopped before this call ran'} through update_state, as repair_thread does, and the line 'healed thread: N open calls' is logged. heal() and repair_thread share a new open_calls(messages) helper.
  - deploy/services/chat/brains.py: LangChainBrain.answer and LangGraphBrain.answer, and K5's stream, call limits.heal() before the new input is applied. LangGraph drops a pending next on new input, as the scratch probe showed. The Meter records healed_calls.
  - deploy/services/chat/agent.py: TurnInFlight becomes HTTP 409 'a turn is already running on this conversation'. The row literal names healed_calls.
  - deploy/commands/tests/test_chat_limits.py: new cases. An interrupted thread (an AI message whose call has no ToolMessage, next ('tools',), an old checkpoint) answers on the next turn and leaves a whole thread. A fresh one gives 409. A thread with an interrupt is untouched. The forced-recursion test stays green.
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.3**, Level 3, 'A turn that will not stop'; ships in K12's PR: Runs an offline cell that trips the framework's backstop on the LangGraph brain, with recursion_limit patched to 3 as the kit's test does. *The learner sees:* The open call the stopped turn left behind; repair_thread answering it with update_state(..., as_node='agent'); the stop sentence; and the next turn on the thread answering. update_state and as_node are defined on the page.
  - **10.3**, Same step, a 'Change it' inside the level: In the clone, comments out limits.repair_thread(...) in LangGraphBrain.answer, re-runs the step's local cell (the brain with Gemini and a tiny recursion limit, then a second question on the same thread), and restores the file. *The learner sees:* Gemini refusing the history that contains the unanswered function call (the exact 400 captured once on a lane): the reason the repair exists.
- **Proof:** test_chat_limits.py passes in CI with the new cases. 10.3's build runs the backstop cell and asserts that every asked call is answered after the repair. The Change-it's 400 is captured once on a lane.
- **Risks:** The in-flight window (checkpoint ts compared with the deadline plus 20 s) leaves a race for two requests arriving within milliseconds, and it relies on instance clocks. The approval workstream's paused turns must be detected first, or an approval would be healed away. Each turn costs one extra checkpoint read. Gemini's exact 400 for an unanswered call is unverified until a lane run. 11.3 quotes agent.py from ChatRequest to caller(), and the 409 path must not move those lines. The 11.3 content ships in K13.
- **Decision:** Whether a concurrent turn gets a 409 (recommended) or is queued. Whether heal is always on as a fix (recommended) or sits behind a switch.
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: concurrency uses a real per-thread lease taken at turn start (H6's lease; one owner) answering 409 while held; heal() repairs only threads whose lease is free; the Change-it bypasses heal or ages the checkpoint and compares heal with and without repair; the 409 text reads 'running or recently interrupted, retry in N s' and stays behind a switch until the lease is proven. (after review: K4 issues: TurnInFlight misses concurrency; Change-it contradicts heal.)
- **Also:** (final review) K4 owns the heal; K3, D7 and H6 build on it.
- **Closes:** L11.3-W04, KIT-07, BENCH-T12_durable_execution, BENCH-T13_tool_failures_reliability

### K5. Stream the agent's steps and tokens: POST /v1/chat/stream behind the Desk's chat door, and the Streamlit agent branch showing each step live

- **Effort:** L · **Kind:** kit · **First phase:** P2 · **After:** K1, K4
- **Reuses:** 6.3's SSE events (rag-api main.py:442-527: citation, token, done) and the frontend's stream_answer reader; rag-api desk_door.py's SSE gate reply; G8 section 4.1's escaping of agent answers (render_with_citations(html.escape(...))); G1 section 4.4's CHAT_EXTRA_ENV switch. New: the gaps plan has no streaming item (the verifier found none in G1-G9).
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/services/chat/brains.py: a stream(question, *, config, context) generator on LangChainBrain and LangGraphBrain, over graph.stream(..., stream_mode=['updates', 'messages', 'custom']). It yields step; tool_call {name, args}; tool_result {name, status, kind, citations, timed_out}; token {t}, from the text blocks of the model node's chunks; and done, with the same four keys as answer(). AdkBrain.stream runs runner.run_async with RunConfig(max_llm_calls=..., streaming_mode=StreamingMode.SSE): partial text events become tokens, and function calls and responses become steps. StreamingMode.SSE and Event.partial were confirmed in google-adk 2.7.1; confirm them in 2.8.0. DirectBrain.stream yields its retrieve step and its answer as one token.
  - deploy/services/chat/tools.py and deploy/services/chat/limits.py: search() and the Meter's model seams emit custom events through langgraph.config.get_stream_writer() when a stream is open ('rag-api answered in N s, k passages'; 'model call 2 of 12, Rs N.NN, N s left'); otherwise they do nothing. The scratch probe confirmed the writer works from timed_tool_call's pool thread, because the context is copied.
  - deploy/services/chat/agent.py: POST /v1/chat/stream behind CHAT_STREAM, default off, which answers 404. The route uses chat()'s identity check, roster, brain_for, Meter and thread_config, plus K4's heal. The turn runs in a dedicated worker thread that feeds a queue, and the SSE generator relays the queue. If the client goes away, the route calls meter.stop('cancelled'), so the turn ends at its next model call with the thread whole and its row logged; STOPS gains 'cancelled'. Each turn logs one chat row with the same named fields and surface 'chat_stream'. K3's fallback emits a fallback event.
  - deploy/services/chat/desk.py: ChatDoor covers both paths (PATH becomes {'/v1/chat': json, '/v1/chat/stream': sse}). A gate hit on the stream path is answered as one token event and a done event, the way rag-api's desk_door.py:221-225 answers /v1/stream, so the new route opens no way around lesson 10.5's hard gate. The shadow hook runs on both paths.
  - deploy/services/frontend/chat.py, the agent branch only (lines 141-177): posts to /v1/chat/stream. On a 404 (CHAT_STREAM off, or an older image) it uses /v1/chat for the rest of the session. st.status lists each step as it arrives ('retrieve(query=..., doc_type=all) -> 5 passages, N s'; 'retrieve refused (argument)'; 'model call 2 of 12'). Tokens fill a placeholder, escaped as today. Citations and the caption render at done.
  - deploy/Makefile and deploy/mk/agents.mk: STREAM ?= off appends |CHAT_STREAM=on to CHAT_EXTRA_ENV; new targets make stream (deploy-services with STREAM=on) and make smoke-stream. deploy/smoke/smoke_chat_stream.py (new): for each agent brain, a retrieve tool_call before the first token, a done with citations n = 1..k, and one row with surface chat_stream. It stays out of smoke-all until the switch defaults on (the reasoning of gaps-plan decision 2).
  - deploy/commands/tests/test_chat_stream.py (new; CI's chat-pins step): event order for the three agent brains with scripted models; done equal to /v1/chat's answer for the same script; a client gone after the first event leaves a whole thread and exactly one row (the probe's failure mode, now tested); a gate hit on the stream path answers token and done with no brain built; a stopped turn's done carries stopped_by; CHAT_STREAM off gives 404.
- **Proof:** test_chat_stream.py passes in CI. On a lane after make stream, make smoke-stream passes for the three agent brains, and a gate-hit question through /v1/chat/stream answers with model none and cost 0. check_lesson and audit_pages pass for 10.5 and 10.6 (desk.py quoted) and for 6.3, 6.4 and 8.1 (frontend chat.py quoted). The UI levels ship in K6, K12 and K14.
- **Risks:** Any question-taking route the ChatDoor does not cover would bypass 10.5's gate, so the door test is load-bearing. Token streaming from ChatGoogleGenerativeAI 4.4.0 inside create_agent's wrap_model_call is confirmed only by a scratch probe with a fake streaming model on langgraph 1.2.11; re-confirm in the chat pins. Gemini 3 content blocks and thought signatures may appear in chunks. A token stream cannot be screened whole: rag-api holds tokens when ARMOR=on, so any later output screen must hold the chat's tokens too, and the security workstream's planned scrub must run per chunk. gunicorn's 120 s worker timeout bounds the stream; the turn ends by about 103 s. A user who retries during a cancelled turn's last step gets K4's 409. The Desk page's /v1/desk stays blocking. Rebuilds: desk.py (10.5, 10.6) and frontend chat.py (6.3, 6.4, 8.1).
- **Decision:** Whether CHAT_STREAM is off by default and turned on by make stream in 10.2's setup (recommended, per the rule), or on by default once proved on a lane. Whether a disconnect cancels the turn at its next model call (recommended: it saves cost and keeps the thread whole) or lets the turn run to completion.
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: get_stream_writer() lookup wrapped in try/except (RuntimeError outside a run) with tests that ADK and offline turns are unchanged; risk corrected (UvicornWorker's -t is a heartbeat; the outer bound is Cloud Run --timeout=300); cancel gets a lane check that closes a stream and reads stopped_by 'cancelled' (or is labelled unverified) and is shown once in a UI level; smoke-stream requires a tool_call before done; the switch is sticky and re-asserted in each UI level's setup with a visible 'streaming off' notice. (after review: K5 issues.)
- **Also:** (final review, for BENCH-T22_streaming_agent_ux) Kit: services/frontend/chat.py's agent branch shows a 'Stop' button beside st.status while a stream is open. Pressing it ends the script run, which closes the stream's response, and the route's disconnect handler stops the turn at its next model call with stopped_by 'cancelled'. Lesson (K6's 10.2 Level 1, after make stream): asks a question that needs two searches and presses Stop after the first step. Then reads the chat row: stopped_by cancelled, surface chat_stream, and the model calls and rupees spent so far. Then asks a follow-up on the same session, which answers because the thread is whole. This is captured once on a lane, or labelled unverified if Cloud Run does not pass the disconnect through. Same level, one definitions row: the stream's events (step, tool_call, tool_result, token, interrupt, done) beside AG-UI's standard event types (for example TOOL_CALL_START, TEXT_MESSAGE_CONTENT, RUN_FINISHED, checked against the AG-UI spec when the page is written) and 12.3's streamed A2A task updates. One sentence says ADK's run_live (Gemini Live's two-way audio) and A2UI's generated UI are not used, because the course's agents answer text in a Streamlit page. Proof: test_chat_stream.py's client-gone case stays green, and the lane capture shows stopped_by cancelled.
- **Closes:** GAP-G12, L10.2-W02, L10.2-S04, L10.3-W08, L10.4-W04, L10.4-W07, BENCH-T12_durable_execution, BENCH-T14_budgets_cost_latency, BENCH-T22_streaming_agent_ux

### K6. 10.2: see the graph and watch it run - draw_mermaid, stream modes, a UI level, a pinned venv, the brains' own test, the checkpoint history and a memory check that does not predict the model's words

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** lesson · **First phase:** P2 · **After:** K5, K8
- **Reuses:** Gaps plan G8 section 4.1's 10.2 scene ('Runs the new test', commands/tests/test_chat_brains.py), which never reached the page.
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.2**, Level 0, 'Step through the graph' panel: Steps a turn through the panel, whose node picture is now drawn at build time from the kit's LangGraphBrain via get_graph().draw_mermaid(). *The learner sees:* The kit's real graph. When the approval workstream's G2 lands, its approve node appears with no page edit.
  - **10.2**, New Level 1 'Watch the graph run' (deployed UI): After make stream, asks the LangGraph brain a gratuity question in the deployed UI, then asks the same with direct. *The learner sees:* The status box listing agent, then 'retrieve(query=..., doc_type=all) -> k passages in N s', then agent; then the answer streaming in, the citations and the caption. Direct shows a single step.
  - **10.2**, Level 2, step 3 (the graph in a venv): Builds the venv, now also pinned to langgraph==1.2.12 from services/chat/requirements.txt, and prints draw_mermaid() for the LangGraph brain and for the LangChain brain's create_agent graph. *The learner sees:* Both versions printed (checklist item 1), and two Mermaid texts to paste into any viewer. The first shows agent, tools and refuse with dotted conditional edges. The second shows model and tools, with no node for TurnLimitsMiddleware, because it only wraps the model call.
  - **10.2**, Level 3, step 4: Streams the plain turn with stream_mode=['updates', 'messages', 'custom'], then runs python -m unittest commands/tests/test_chat_brains.py -v in graph-venv with the chat pins (offline and free). *The learner sees:* What each mode carries: a node's writes, the model's tokens tagged with langgraph_node, and the tool layer's custom 'rag-api answered' event, which together are what the UI level consumes. Then the kit's own brain tests passing: per-turn keys, numbered citations, one error contract.
  - **10.2**, Level 3, step 5 (on your lane), checklist item 7: Checks the new session structurally with make thread SESSION=lesson102-new (K8's read-only reader), instead of predicting the model's words. *The learner sees:* 'The new session's thread holds one turn'. A real model that searches anyway, as SYSTEM tells it to, still passes.
  - **10.2**, Level 4, step 6 (what the checkpointer keeps): Calls get_state() and get_state_history() on a two-turn offline thread of the kit's LangGraph brain. Then removes one turn's question with RemoveMessage and calls _summary. *The learner sees:* Each checkpoint's step, source (input or loop), next node and message count. The build's 'two turns leave four messages and no system message' becomes the learner's own output. Then the ValueError the kit raises ('whatever trims a thread must keep its question'), the rule the context-window work (G7) must respect. A pointer leads to 11.2 for replay and fork.
- **Proof:** 10.2's build runs every new cell on the kit and asserts the Mermaid edges, the event order across the three modes, the history counts, the ValueError text and the test file's OK. check_lesson and audit_pages pass for 10.2. The UI level and the structural check are verified once on a lane after K5 and K8. The module_10 lesson_10_2 demos are regenerated and reviewed.
- **Risks:** 10.2 grows. It fits 90 minutes only if the presentation workstream collapses the shared setup (PRES-05). draw_mermaid's text varies across langgraph versions; it is pinned and asserted at build. The approval workstream's G2 rewrites 10.2's refuse scene in the same file, so sequence the PRs. The memory workstream's plan to fold 11.1 into 10.2's checkpointer step (PRES-07) edits step 6 too; one owner per step. One lesson per PR: K6 and K7 land as one 10.2 PR or as two in sequence.
- **Decision:** Whether to draw the Mermaid as the build's own inline SVG (recommended: no external script in the Wix page) or embed a Mermaid renderer.
- **Revised:** lesson_changes: Amend: the venv installs google-adk and runs with DOCUMIND_REQUIRE_LIBS=1, asserting no skips; 10.2's setup adds the connector, pg8000 and DSN access for make thread; step 5's output becomes a labelled lane sample with reworded prose; nodes laid out in build.py from get_graph() with edges asserted (no claim that G2's node appears without page edits), layout work counted. (after review: K6 issues and L10.2-W06/W11 notes.)
- **Also:** (final review, for BENCH-T21_testing_agents) 10.2 Level 3 step 4, after the learner's test_chat_brains.py run, adds a block() excerpt of deploy/.github/workflows/documind-dryrun.yml's step 'Chat brain regressions, in the chat image's pins (offline)'. That step runs the same command with DOCUMIND_REQUIRE_LIBS=1 on every push and pull request; the excerpt points back to 7.2, which introduced the workflow. One sentence states the kit's rule: scripted-model tests gate CI; live-model behaviour is reported as k of n (make agent-turn REPEAT, agent-eval --runs) and never gates. The build asserts the excerpt's command equals the cell's.
- **Closes:** GAP-G12, GAP-G13, PRES-02, PRES-10, L10.2-W02, L10.2-W06, L10.2-W08, L10.2-W09, L10.2-W11, L10.2-W13, L10.2-S04, L10.2-S05, BENCH-T21_testing_agents, BENCH-T22_streaming_agent_ux

### K7. 10.2 Advanced: custom state, reducers, fan-out and subgraphs on the kit's two graphs, with a Change-it that breaks and fixes a reducer

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** lesson · **First phase:** P2 · **After:** K6
- **Reuses:** Nothing in the gaps plan (G1-G9 schedule no LangGraph depth). The content is grounded in desk_graph.py:15-17's own reasoning and the Desk plan's next_part design.
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.2**, New Level 5 step 'State, reducers, fan-out and subgraphs', before 'Why the graph is built this way': Reads verbatim excerpts: DeskState (desk_graph.py:95, with messages under add_messages and plain overwrite channels); the module docstring's lines that parts never run as Command(goto=[a, b]) (desk_graph.py:15-17); next_part(); tools._number() with its _LEDGER lock; and desk_agent.py's checkpointer=False lines. *The learner sees:* The kit's real custom state, its one reducer, and two deliberate design choices: parts run in sequence, and the inner agent keeps nothing of its own.
  - **10.2**, Same step, cell A: Runs the kit's LangGraph brain offline with a scripted model that asks for two retrieves in one message (a handbook clause and a statute section), against a stub rag-api that takes 1 s per call. *The learner sees:* The turn takes about 1 s, not 2, because ToolNode ran both calls in pool threads. add_messages merges both results into messages, and the citations are numbered 1..k without a collision because _number() holds the lock.
  - **10.2**, Same step, a 'Change it' inside the level: Runs a cell that builds a three-node graph on the kit's DeskState and its _with_section() helper, with dispatch fanning out to both desks at once. Then edits the sections line of DeskState in the clone to Annotated[list, operator.add], re-runs the same cell, and restores the file with git checkout. *The learner sees:* First, LangGraph's InvalidUpdateError ('At key sections: Can receive only one value per step. Use an Annotated key to handle multiple values', confirmed on the scratch probe). Then the sections duplicated, because _with_section returns the whole list; a reducer's node must return only the delta. Then why the kit runs parts one after the other, under one Meter and the PART_MIN_S rule, and when fan-out is right: independent, idempotent branches merged by a reducer.
  - **10.2**, Same step, cell B: Adds the kit's LangGraphBrain graph as a node of a small parent graph that has a checkpointer, and prints draw_mermaid() with xray=1. *The learner sees:* A subgraph block in the Mermaid, and the inner graph's checkpoints held in the parent's thread under a namespace. The contrast is desk_agent's checkpointer=False: the Desk thread keeps only the section, never the agent's tool messages.
- **Proof:** 10.2's build runs cell A, cell B and both halves of the Change-it on the kit, applying the reducer to a scratch copy. It asserts about 1 s for two 1 s calls, citations 1..k, the InvalidUpdateError text, the duplicated list and the subgraph block. check_lesson and audit_pages pass for 10.2. These facts were confirmed on a scratch probe with langgraph 1.2.11 and are re-confirmed on 1.2.12 at build.
- **Risks:** A forward reference from 10.2 to the Desk (10.5, 10.6). Quoting desk_graph.py ties 10.2 to the Desk workstream's edits: check_lesson names 10.2 whenever DeskState moves. 10.2's length. The page must not read as 'never fan out'.
- **Decision:** Teach state, reducers and fan-out on the Desk graph in 10.2 (recommended: it is the kit's real custom state), or in 10.6 after the Desk plan's decision-18 split.
- **Revised:** lesson_changes: The reducer cell runs two turns on an InMemorySaver thread (or seeds a section first) and asserts the duplicated list; it teaches both effects (a node returns only its delta; a reset under an add reducer does nothing). (after review: K7 issue.)
- **Also:** (final review) Written as graph.get_graph(xray=1).draw_mermaid(): xray is get_graph's parameter (langgraph pregel/main.py:845), and the build asserts the subgraph block from that output. K7 also hosts B4's learner-written node as the go-deeper level.
- **Closes:** GAP-G13, L10.1-S03, L10.2-W01, L10.2-W02, L10.4-W07, BENCH-T14_budgets_cost_latency

### K8. Time travel on a real conversation: read a lane thread back at any step, then replay and fork it locally (make thread)

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **First phase:** P2
- **Reuses:** 11.2's own connector cells (demo_03 and demo_06: saver.list, the checkpoint SQL and saver.serde), and gaps plan section 5's add_messages id rule: a forked question gets a uuid4 id, as _turn() gives one. The replay and fork APIs were confirmed on a scratch probe (langgraph 1.2.11): a replay writes new checkpoints and becomes the thread's latest, and update_state on a past config forks.
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/commands/thread_read.py (new, read-only), with options --tenant, --user, --session, --at STEP and --export FILE. It connects as 11.2's cells do (Cloud SQL Python Connector and pg8000, with the DSN read from Secret Manager into memory). It lists a thread's checkpoints (step, source, next, ts, message count) and decodes the messages channel at a step from checkpoint_blobs (type, blob) with the pinned langgraph JsonPlusSerializer.loads_typed. --export writes those messages for a local replay.
  - deploy/mk/memory.mk (new; the gaps plan's G7 creates the same file, and whichever PR is first adds its mk/README.md row): make thread SESSION= [AT=] [TENANT=] [EMAIL=].
- **Lessons:**
  - **11.2**, Level 4, step 6, renamed 'Read it back at any step, then replay and fork it': Runs make thread SESSION=lesson102 AT=<the step before the tool call>. *The learner sees:* The thread's checkpoints and, at that step, the messages saved then: the question and the model's tool call, before the passages arrived. The page's claim 'a thread can be read back at any step' is now done on the learner's own lane thread.
  - **11.2**, Same step: Runs a local cell. It seeds an InMemorySaver thread with the exported messages (update_state) and builds the kit's LangGraphBrain with Gemini at location global, against the lane's rag-api as documind-ui-sa. It replays from that checkpoint with invoke(None, cfg), then forks with a different question (update_state on the past config, then invoke(None, fork)). *The learner sees:* A new answer to the same question, which is how you re-run yesterday's turn with today's prompt. A forked branch answering 'and for a contract employee?'. get_state_history listing both branches. And the lane thread's checkpoint count unchanged, because the lane was only read.
  - **11.2**, Level 5, 'Why storage is built this way': Reads the trade-offs. *The learner sees:* ShallowPostgresSaver keeps no history, so it ends time travel. A retention job (the retention workstream) must delete every branch. A replay costs real model calls.
- **Proof:** 11.2's build runs thread_read against its stand-in database, and runs the local replay and fork on the kit's brain with a scripted model, asserting both branches in get_state_history and the unchanged lane count. On a lane, make thread prints the learner's 10.2 thread, and the replay and fork run once with Gemini, captured as a labelled sample. check_lesson and audit_pages pass for 11.2. tools/kit_index.py is re-run.
- **Risks:** Decoding depends on langgraph-checkpoint-postgres 3.1.2's blob layout; it is pinned and asserted at build. The memory workstream may switch to ShallowPostgresSaver to bound storage (L11.2-W03), which would remove the history this step reads. Reading the DSN needs the access 11.2 already asks for. A replay costs a few model calls; the page states the cost from the rows and never invents it. 11.2 belongs to the memory workstream, so agree one owner per step.
- **Decision:** Keep full checkpoint history (time travel, debugging and audit, bounded by a retention job), or move to ShallowPostgresSaver (smaller tables, no time travel). Also: time travel as read-back plus local replay (recommended baseline), or add K9's on-lane fork.
- **Revised:** lesson_changes: The lane run is mandatory before merge (or thread_read is tested against a throwaway Postgres in CI); the page says 'replay a copy of the thread'; on-lane forking is left to K9. (after review: K8 issue.)
- **Closes:** GAP-G13, L10.2-W02, L10.2-W06, L10.2-W08, L11.2-S07, BENCH-T12_durable_execution

### K9. Optional: edit an earlier question on the lane - a fork route behind CHAT_TIME_TRAVEL and the UI's edit-and-resend

- **Effort:** L · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **First phase:** P6 · **After:** K8, K5, K4
- **Reuses:** Nothing in the gaps plan. The fork pattern was confirmed on the scratch probe, and the door coverage follows K5.
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/services/chat/agent.py: behind CHAT_TIME_TRAVEL (default off), two routes. GET /v1/chat/history?session_id= returns the caller's own thread only, because thread_config builds the thread id from the verified identity: each turn's checkpoint id, step, question and answer preview. POST /v1/chat/fork {session_id, checkpoint_id, question, brain} calls update_state on the past config with a new HumanMessage, then runs the brain on the fork under a fresh Meter, with heal first. langchain and langgraph only; ADK gives 501. The row literal names forked_from.
  - deploy/services/chat/desk.py: the fork route takes a question, so ChatDoor covers /v1/chat/fork too.
  - deploy/services/frontend/chat.py: an Edit button under each earlier question when the chat answers /v1/chat/history; the conversation redraws from the fork.
  - deploy/Makefile and deploy/mk/memory.mk: TIME_TRAVEL ?= off feeds CHAT_EXTRA_ENV; new target make time-travel.
  - deploy/commands/tests/test_chat_fork.py (new): another thread's checkpoint id gives 404; the fork becomes the thread's latest; a gate hit on /v1/chat/fork is answered by the door; a thread paused at an interrupt refuses a fork; with the switch off the route gives 404.
- **Lessons:**
  - **11.2**, Level 3, 'Configure': Runs make time-travel, then edits the first question of a three-turn conversation in the UI and calls GET /v1/chat/history. *The learner sees:* The answer redrawn from the fork, and the history listing both branches. 11.2's 'Configure' title gets something real to configure.
- **Proof:** test_chat_fork.py passes. On a lane, the fork REST cell shows the new branch as the thread's latest and the history lists both branches. check_lesson passes for 10.5 and 10.6 (desk.py) and 11.2.
- **Risks:** It adds a write route on production threads. Branches must be erased together (delete_thread removes every branch). The approval workstream's paused turns must refuse forks. Streamlit UX complexity. Each fork costs a turn.
- **Decision:** Build it, only if 11.2 should configure something real, or stop at K8 (recommended unless the memory workstream wants the route).
- **Closes:** (enables later tasks)

### K10. Budgets live: trip the rupee cap and the deadline on the lane, per-tenant limits, and the A2A peer's cap

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **First phase:** P2 · **After:** coordinate with the multi-agent workstream: PLAN-G1 needs G6-b first
- **Reuses:** Gaps plan G6 section 4.2: limits-drill.sh and the smoke's EXPECT_STOP; the G6-b rows for the peer (agent.py setdefault, /health, lesson-8.4.sh, smoke_agent); the G6-c 13.3 scene; decision 9's defaults. Departs from G6-c's placement: the live rupee drill moves into 10.3, which already runs limits-drill, because 13.3 never took it up (its parts have no turn_budget).
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/commands/limits-drill.sh: new STOP=turn_deadline beside model_calls and turn_budget. It sets CHAT_TURN_DEADLINE_S=6 with CHAT_MIN_MODEL_S 5, so the first model call and the search use up the time and the second call is refused. deploy/smoke/smoke_chat.py's DOCUMIND_EXPECT_STOP already compares stopped_by.
  - deploy/services/chat/agent.py: per-tenant limits from tenant_settings/{tenant}.chat_limits {max_model_calls, budget_inr, deadline_s}, read through the 60 s tenant_settings cache that desk.settings() keeps (or a shared helper beside it). They are capped at the service's values: a tenant can be stricter, never looser. The Meter is built with them, and the response's limits show the effective values. With no field, behaviour is unchanged.
  - deploy/commands/lane.py: limits --tenant prints the effective limits, and a new chat-limits subcommand writes or clears a tenant's chat_limits (lane.py's pinned subcommand set and commands/tests/test_lane.py updated). deploy/mk/agents.mk: make limits TENANT= and make chat-limits TENANT= MAX_CALLS= BUDGET_INR= [RESET=1].
  - deploy/services/agent/agent.py and deploy/commands/deploy-agent.sh: G6-b as designed. setdefault ADK_MAX_LLM_CALLS to 12, publish max_llm_calls on /health, and have the deploy line append |ADK_MAX_LLM_CALLS=12. deploy/smoke/smoke_agent.py requires 12.
  - deploy/commands/tests/test_chat_limits.py: the deadline drill's arithmetic; a stricter tenant override applies and a looser one is capped; the peer's /health names 12.
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.3**, Level 3, 'A turn that will not stop'; ships in K12's PR: Runs make limits-drill with STOP=turn_budget and STOP=turn_deadline, as well as model_calls. *The learner sees:* Each agent brain answering 200 with stopped_by turn_budget, then turn_deadline, and the restored smoke passing. Every limit is tripped on the lane, and the rupee cap is no longer deferred to 13.3.
  - **10.3**, Same step: Sets a stricter cap for acme with make chat-limits TENANT=acme MAX_CALLS=3, reads make limits TENANT=acme, asks one turn, then clears the cap with RESET=1. *The learner sees:* The effective limits (3 calls) in the tenant view and in the turn's limits. The box item 'the limits are the service's, not the tenant's' becomes a step.
  - **12.3**, Level 3, the peer and its permissions: Reads the peer's /health through make limits. *The learner sees:* max_llm_calls 12, and, offline, a peer task capped at 1 ending in state failed (G6-b's scene). 12.3's gaps box discloses the missing cap until this lands.
  - **13.3**, The month's breaker: Follows one paragraph from the month's breaker to the turn's clock. *The learner sees:* A pointer to 10.3's turn_budget drill beside the month's counter (G6-c). The loop's own spend stays outside the month unless K11 lands.
- **Proof:** On a lane, make limits-drill STOP=turn_deadline and STOP=turn_budget each print a PASS stopped line for every agent brain, then the restored smoke's pass lines. make limits shows the peer's 12. test_chat_limits.py and test_lane.py pass. check_lesson and audit_pages pass for 10.3, 12.3 and 13.3.
- **Risks:** The 6 s deadline depends on lane latency: any first call plus search taking more than 1 s stops the turn. Each drill caps the real documind-chat for a minute or two. Per-tenant limits add a cached Firestore read. The peer cap edits 12.3, the protocols workstream's lesson; 12.3's build asserts today that '"max_llm_calls"' is absent. 13.3 is outside Modules 10-12. Lands as one kit PR with the mechanical rebuilds, then 12.3's and 13.3's scene PRs; the 10.3 content ships in K12.
- **Decision:** Per-tenant limits now (recommended: small, and 10.3 lists the gap) or later. Whether 13.3 gets the full G6-c scene or a pointer (recommended: a pointer, since the drill lives in 10.3).
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: the rebuild note names 10.3 build.py:109 (not 12.3); per-tenant limits catch a failed Firestore read and fall back to service values (test); the step waits out the 60 s cache or prints its age; the turn_deadline drill presents both outcomes (both stopped_by turn_deadline). G6-b comes from J7. (after review: K10 issues.)
- **Revised:** effort revised to M after review (Scope.)
- **Also:** (final review, for BENCH-T14_budgets_cost_latency) Kit: services/chat/limits.py's Meter gains max_tokens, read from CHAT_TURN_MAX_TOKENS (default 0 = no token cap, so behaviour is unchanged) and from tenant_settings chat_limits.max_tokens (stricter only). allow_model_call stops the turn with stopped_by 'turn_tokens' once tokens_in + tokens_out has reached it, and STOPS gains 'turn_tokens'. commands/limits-drill.sh gains STOP=turn_tokens, setting the cap below one model call's tokens as make agent-turn printed them. test_chat_limits.py covers the stop, 0 meaning off and the per-tenant cap. Lesson (10.3, 'A turn that will not stop'): runs make limits-drill STOP=turn_tokens beside model_calls, turn_budget and turn_deadline, and sees each agent brain answer 200 with stopped_by turn_tokens. The same step ends with one table, 'every budget lever and where you pull it': calls, tokens, rupees and time (this step); cost per turn, tenant and brain (the chat row's cost_usd and rag_cost_usd, 10.4's four cost lines); model routing (10.6's gemini-3.1-flash-lite L1, with gemini-3.6-flash L2 only for unsure turns, its L2 share and router cost); caching (11.4's cached_tokens per turn, 10.4's answer-cache hits at Rs 0); parallel tool calls (10.1's offline overlap cell, 10.2's go-deeper level). Proof: on a lane, the drill prints a PASS stopped line with turn_tokens for every agent brain, then the restored smoke's pass lines.
- **Closes:** L10.3-W05, L10.3-S01, L10.3-S02, L10.3-S05, L10.3-S11, L12.3-W07, PLAN-G6, BENCH-T01_agent_loop_function_calling, BENCH-T14_budgets_cost_latency, BENCH-T28_versioning_release

### K11. Count the agent loop's own spend in tenant_daily, make usage and the month counter (gaps-plan decision 9's later phase)

- **Effort:** L · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **First phase:** P4
- **Reuses:** Gaps plan G6 decision 9 ('recording the loop's spend in the month counter and tenant_daily is a later phase') and G6 section 4.2's note that tenant_daily.sql:51 and usage_rows.py:31-32 exclude event chat.
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/terraform/sql/tenant_daily.sql: adds the chat rows' own cost_usd (event chat) per tenant per day as agent_cost_usd. It never adds rag_cost_usd, which rag-api's own rows already hold, as agent.py's row comment says.
  - deploy/evals/usage_rows.py: make usage prints the by-brain table with the chat rows' model calls and cost beside rag-api's; --selftest covers the double-count rule.
  - deploy/services/chat/agent.py: after logging the row, records the turn's own cost_usd into the month counter that rag-api's budget.record() keeps, through a shared helper, so the 80 percent breaker sees agent spend (subject to the decision below).
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.4**, Level 3, step 5 (four cost lines): Reads the four cost lines from make usage instead of the cell's hand-written join. *The learner sees:* rag-api's half and the agent's own half per brain, from one command.
  - **13.2**, The reconciled month: Reads tenant_daily. *The learner sees:* agent_cost_usd beside rag-api's cost, so the month includes the loop. The 'month's counter does not see the turn's own spend' items in 10.3 and 10.4 drop out when their builds' asserts flip.
- **Proof:** evals/usage_rows.py --selftest passes. On a lane, make usage HOURS=1 shows each agent brain's cost equal to the sum of its chat rows' cost_usd, and tenant_daily shows agent_cost_usd. check_lesson passes for every page that quotes tenant_daily.sql or usage_rows.py (5.2, 5.3, 5.4, 6.3, 9.3, 13.2, 13.3, 17.3).
- **Risks:** Wide rebuilds across Modules 5, 6, 9, 13 and 17. A breaker that counts agent spend changes 13.3's behaviour and could stop a tenant sooner. A slip that adds rag_cost_usd counts rag-api twice.
- **Decision:** Do it in this uplift (recommended only if the operations lessons are in scope) or keep decision 9's later phase. Whether the breaker counts agent spend, or only the reports do.
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: rebuild list built from check_lesson and INDEX (10.1 text change, 10.3 build.py:108, 16.2; 13.3 removed); the month-counter change sits behind a switch default off. Fallback dispositions if declined: L10.1-S09 and L10.4-S07 deferred with a 10.4 cell that joins chat rows and rag-api rows. (after review: K11 issues.)
- **Also:** (final review) Its reporting half (the chat rows' cost_usd beside rag-api's in make usage) is what decision 29's per-phase budget reads once it lands; until then the budget reads the chat rows with the logging query recorded in PR01.
- **Also:** (final review, for L10.4-S07) Replace the fallback with a taught gap that needs no kit change. If K11 is declined, 10.4 step 5 runs make usage HOURS=1 beside the cell's own join of chat and rag-api rows. Per brain, it prints the rupees make usage's by-brain table reports, the rupees the chat rows add (the loop's own model calls) and that share of the turn. The page says this share is what tenant_daily (13.2) and rag-api's month counter (budget.record, the 80 percent breaker) never see, and names K11 as the kit change that would count it. The build asserts the share is computed from the same rows.
- **Closes:** L10.1-S09, L10.4-S07, BENCH-T14_budgets_cost_latency

### K12. 10.3 re-cut: live recovery and faults on the lane, all three brains, the repeated steps compressed, a falsifiable proof, rehearsed once

- **Effort:** L · **Kind:** lesson · **First phase:** P2 · **After:** K1, K2, K3, K4, K10, K5

During the drill: a reply that search is unavailable, and no citations.
  - **10.3**, Level 0 panel 'Where a failure surfaces': Picks a failure in the panel. *The learner sees:* New rows built from the kit's own runs: a corrected argument, a dead search, a retried 503, an open breaker, a fallback to direct, a healed thread, and each stop including cancelled.
  - **10.3**, Steps 5 and 6 (the door, the filter): Reads one paragraph pointing to 8.2's refusal ladder, and meets the filter inside 'Recovery with a real model' (K1). *The learner sees:* No repeated 401/403 cell. The empty pool is now refused as an argument and corrected by the model, instead of a silent empty answer. The freed time goes to the recovery and fault levels.
  - **10.3**, Definitions, hero, chips, 'What changed on your lane', footer: Reads the updated words: self-correction, retry, backoff, circuit breaker, fallback, refusal kind, heal, cancelled. *The learner sees:* A lane box naming the revisions the limits drill and the fault drill left, and their rows.
  - **10.3**, course-manifest.json and the 10.3 rows of plan/course-plan-v5-story-2026-09-22.md and plan/course-roadmap-v5-2026-09-22.md: Reads the lesson's proof. *The learner sees:* The new proof, 'a refused argument corrected by the model on your lane; a forced fault answered 200 (search unavailable, or a labelled direct fallback)', in place of 'refusals non-empty; a timed-out tool reported'.

The module_10 lesson_10_3 demos are regenerated, reviewed in tools/workshop_demo_reviewed.json, and pass check_workshop_demos and run_tests. The manifest proof is updated along with its plan and roadmap rows.

Model words vary from run to run, so the checks are structural (tool_calls, refusal kinds, stopped_by, citations) and the words are labelled samples. The drills change the real documind-chat briefly. The local real-model cell now appears in 10.3, before 10.4 step 6, so 10.3's setup box must say so.
- **Decision:** Whether to drop step 5's door cell entirely (recommended; 8.2 owns the ladder) or keep one line of it. The wording of the new proof.

(after review: K12 issues.)

- **Closes:** GAP-G11, PRES-02, L10.3-W01, L10.3-W03, L10.3-W06, L10.3-W08, L10.3-S02, L10.3-S04, KIT-07, BENCH-T13_tool_failures_reliability

### K13. 11.3: a turn cut off mid-flight and a second turn at once - heal and 409 on the kit and on the lane

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** lesson · **First phase:** P3 · **After:** K4
- **Reuses:** The scratch probe's failure mode (a closed stream saving next ('tools',)) and G6's repair_thread.
- **Lessons:**
  - **11.3**, New Level 3 step 'A turn cut off mid-flight': Runs an offline cell. The kit's LangGraph brain writes to an InMemorySaver shared by two graph objects (a second process in miniature, as the scratch probe did). The stream is closed right after the agent asks for retrieve, and the second graph object asks again on the same thread. *The learner sees:* The saved thread with next ('tools',) and one open call. Without heal, the next model call would be sent an unanswered function call. With heal(), the open call is answered, the line 'healed thread: 1 open calls' is logged, and the next turn answers.
  - **11.3**, Same step, live: Sends two /v1/chat requests on one session at once, as two background curls as documind-ui-sa, using a question that searches. *The learner sees:* One 200 and one 409 'a turn is already running on this conversation', and one new turn in the thread.
  - **11.3**, Level 5, 'What the kit does not do yet': Reads the remaining gap. *The learner sees:* A paused approval surviving a redeploy, named as the approval workstream's (G2).
- **Proof:** 11.3's build runs the cut-off cell on the kit and asserts the open call before the heal and the whole thread after it. The concurrent cell is rehearsed once on a lane. check_lesson and audit_pages pass for 11.3.
- **Risks:** 11.3 belongs to the memory workstream. The 409 depends on timing on a lane: the first request must still be running, hence a question that searches.
- **Revised:** lesson_changes: The offline cut-off cell ages the checkpoint (or patches the threshold) and the page explains why; the live concurrency cell starts the second request after the first one's tool call is logged, using H6's lease. (after review: K13 issue.)
- **Also:** (final review, for BENCH-T12_durable_execution) The offline cut-off cell gains a resume branch before heal(): the second graph object calls invoke(None, cfg) on the cut-off thread (next ('tools',)). The pending retrieve runs and the turn completes from the checkpoint the first process saved, and the build asserts the completed answer and one tool result. The page states why /v1/chat heals instead: the caller's request is gone, so a resumed answer reaches no one and a side effect would repeat. It also adds a Level 5 table, 'Durable execution in this kit', with these rows: resume after a crash (this cell; K8's replay is the same call); resume after a redeploy (D6); at-least-once and idempotency (D3's rerun line and approval id, D2's claim, K2's never-retry-a-timeout rule); long-running (an approval waiting up to APPROVAL_TTL_H; an A2A task polled with GetTask in 12.3); event-triggered (an approver's decision resuming the turn; the Google Chat bridge's Pub/Sub-pushed /work worker with its lease and MAX_ATTEMPTS in 10.7's appendix); cancellation (K5's cancelled stop, D1's cancel).
- **Closes:** L11.3-W04, KIT-07, BENCH-T12_durable_execution

### K15. Workstream K's landing order, checks and lane rehearsal

- **Effort:** S · **Kind:** process · **First phase:** P0
- **Reuses:** Gaps plan section 3 (order of work, and the rules that hold across phases: row keys named in the literal, one owner per row key, one .mk file per module, kit comments that say 'workshop lesson N.M') and decision 16 (kit PRs carry their forced rebuilds; scene content lands one lesson per PR).
- **Proof:** Each kit PR carries the rebuilds check_lesson names (the gaps plan's section 3 rule) and passes: check_lesson and audit_pages for each rebuilt lesson (R39 is the known exception); tools/kit_index.py, then --check; tools/build_workshop_demos.py, then --check; tools/check_workshop_demos.py; and deploy/workshop_demos/tests/run_tests.py, with each changed digest reviewed in tools/workshop_demo_reviewed.json; then tools/publish_learner.py --check. The new tests join CI's chat-pins step (.github/workflows/checks.yml and deploy/.github/workflows/documind-dryrun.yml). Each lesson PR rehearses its live cells once on a lane. Python cells follow CLAUDE.md's heredoc form. The learner kit is published only after merge, each time with the author's explicit go-ahead.
- **Risks:** Add three collisions to the order table, each with an owner and a decision before the first kit PR: G1's CHAT_EXTRA_ENV assignment vs K2/K3/K5 appends; D's G2-b 10.3 scene vs K12; H's memory decisions vs K8's history read (ShallowPostgresSaver is deprecated in 3.1.2; keep full history).
- **Decision:** Landing order relative to the approval workstream's G2. Recommended: K1-K4 before G2, because G2's approve node then rebases on K4's heal and K3's repair signature; K5 after G2-a, so the stream relays interrupts from the start.
- **Revised:** risks revised after review (K15 issue.)
- **Closes:** L10.3-W06

## L. Page format, proofs and accuracy

**Goal.** Make every Act V page (10.1-10.6, 11.1-11.3, 12.1-12.3) show the agent acting on the learner's own lane, early and honestly. Expected outputs are captured once on a live lane instead of written by the author against stubs and scripted models. Proofs are earned by a real model or the lane, and they can fail. Setup is compact, titles match what is taught, each topic has one home, the deployed UI comes first, and pages carry no old-course numbering, no gates the learner cannot open and no accuracy slips. Then each lesson is rehearsed once on a lane so the roadmap can mark batch E Done. Wherever it can, the plan reuses the author's own mechanisms: pagebuild.data() captures (3.3, 7.2, 7.3), the workshop_demos evidence store, tools/leakscan.py, gaps-plan decisions 15 and 16 and Desk decision 18.

### L1. Capture live-lane outputs into the pages: the pipeline, piloted on 10.1

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **First phase:** P0 · **After:** A lane deployed from the current kit for the pilot, L8, if 10.1's deploy cell is renamed first; otherwise recapture 10.1 after L8
- **Reuses:** pagebuild.data() and the captured files of 3.3 (data/samples33.json), 7.2 (data/dryrun_runs.json, with its 'captured' date) and 7.3 (data/sdk_win_rates.txt); the workshop_demos evidence store and lesson_map.json windows; tools/leakscan.py; the after-every-pull-request checks in gaps plan section 3. The gaps plan has no item for captures, so this is new.
- **Kit:**
  - tools/lane_capture.py (new, authoring side, beside tools/leakscan.py). It reads the results/ tree a rehearsal leaves on the Linux workstation (results/module_NN/lesson_N_M/<run>/attempts/<attempt>/) and maps each saved log to its page window through deploy/workshop_demos/module_NN/lesson_N_M/lesson_map.json. It rewrites lane identifiers to the course placeholders: the project id to documind-ai-YOUR-ID, the project number to NUMBER, the operator's email ($ME) to you@example.com, and revision suffixes, execution ids and uuids to '...'. It refuses on any tools/leakscan.scan_text hit or PUBLISH_DENY string. It writes lessons/<NN-module>/<N.M-slug>/data/lane.json = {captured, kit_commit, region, model, windows}. --selftest runs on a fixture tree with planted identifiers.
  - deploy/workshop_demos/setup/workshop_helpers/steps.py and session.py: run_steps() tees each native Python step's stdout into the attempt folder, and each log is named after the page window it ran. Today only command() writes command_NN.log, so Python cells leave nothing on disk to capture. deploy/workshop_demos/tests/run_tests.py gains the case. Then regenerate with tools/build_workshop_demos.py and review the digests in tools/workshop_demo_reviewed.json.
  - pagekit/pagebuild.py gains three helpers. lane(lid) loads data/lane.json, the same pattern as data(), which 3.3, 7.2 and 7.3 already use for captured files. captured_window(label, text) labels a window 'expected (captured on a live lane on DATE, kit COMMIT; your ids, words, times and rupees differ)'. offline_window() labels a fault-injection run 'expected offline (a scripted model; no lane, no model)'.
  - pagekit/check_lesson.py, for a lesson that has data/lane.json: fail any expected window labelled stand-in, stub, fake or invented on a cell that reaches the lane, and fail a setup sentence that claims every output is the lane's while offline windows remain.
  - lessons/10-agents/10.1-agent-loop/build.py: the rag-api stub (class Api), the chat stub's PLAN dict and FAKE_GCLOUD give way to lane(). The build asserts only structure on the capture, never the model's words: the direct brain's tool_calls ['retrieve'], each langchain turn's n = 1..k, the chat row's model_calls, cost_usd and rag_cost_usd keys, and model gemini-3.6-flash. The 'What it costs' table reads the captured rows, priced at USD_INR 85.
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.1**, Steps 5 to 8 (the retrieve, direct, loop and rows cells) and the 'What it costs' table: Runs the same cells as today on their own lane. *The learner sees:* Windows labelled 'captured on a live lane' with a real Gemini answer, the tool_calls the model actually chose, real latencies, and the chat row's real model_calls, tokens and rupees. The cost answer no longer cites [1] for a page count the user typed; today that line is written by the author.
- **Proof:** python tools/lane_capture.py --selftest passes. python pagekit/build.py 10.1 builds from data/lane.json. python pagekit/check_lesson.py 10.1 and python pagekit/audit_pages.py 10.1 pass. The built 10.1 page has 0 lane-cell windows labelled stand-in, stub or fake; today 5 of its 9 expected windows are.
- **Risks:** Captures drift as the kit changes: any kit PR that changes a cell's output needs a recapture, and the label's kit COMMIT makes the drift visible. An identifier the patterns miss could leak, such as a Workspace email or a revision name; the tool replaces $ME and $PROJECT explicitly, and check_lesson already runs leakscan on every page, which is public on Wix. If a demo groups windows differently from the page, the log-to-window mapping breaks; the tool fails on any count mismatch rather than guessing.
- **Decision:** CLAUDE.md says lane-dependent values are placeholders. Approve reading that as identifiers only. Model words, token counts, milliseconds and rupees would appear as a dated capture from a real lane, labelled 'yours differ'. Project ids, project numbers, emails and revisions stay placeholders. Recommended: yes. Without it the pages keep invented values or 'Rs N.NNNN' placeholders, which is the PRES-02 problem.
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: the pilot capture adds the describe and /health cell (SVC, HEALTH) and the deploy 'shape' window; lane_capture refuses windows whose demo step carries an adaptation other than the pin unless reviewed; leakscan gains an 'eyJ...' JWT pattern and the operator's domain, planted in --selftest; the 'no longer cites [1]' promise dropped (structure asserted only). (after review: L1 issues.)
- **Revised:** depends_on: L3 (or a one-line 3.1 setup edit) lands with or before L1's check_lesson rule; lane deployed from the current kit; L8 first or recapture after. (after review: L1 issue: own proof fails without L3.)
- **Closes:** PRES-02, L10.1-W03, L10.1-W07, L10.3-W06, L10.4-W05, L11.1-W05

### L2. Rehearse batch E on a live lane, swap every stand-in for a capture, and define and reach Done

- **Effort:** L · **Kind:** process · **First phase:** P6 · **After:** L1, L3, L5, L6, L7 and L8: each lesson's page should be settled before its final capture, The workstream that owns each lesson's content lands its kit change before the final capture: agent capability (GAP-G01) for 10.1, HITL (GAP-G04) for 10.2, reliability and limits (GAP-G11) for 10.3, evaluation (GAP-G05) for 10.4, the Desk workstream for 10.5 and 10.6, memory (GAP-G08) for 11.x, protocols (GAP-G10) for 12.x
- **Reuses:** The 'On a lane' verification lists in plan/agents-mcp-gaps-plan-2026-09-29.md serve as the rehearsal script for 10.1-10.4 and 12.3: section 4.1 (G8) steps 1-5 and section 4.2 (G6) steps 1-6. Gaps plan decision 16: publish the learner kit at the end of each phase, each time with the author's go-ahead. The roadmap's own rule that a scene is done only when its proof has been rehearsed once on a live lane.
- **Kit:**
  - plan/course-roadmap-v5-2026-09-22.md (and its .html and .pdf): rewrite 'What done means' for one page per lesson. Done is the page plus its workshop demos; check_lesson, audit_pages and check_workshop_demos green; every proof rehearsed once on a live lane with its capture committed in data/lane.json; and a read by a second person. Remove the six files, 5 quizzes and 3 exercise cards that CLAUDE.md dropped. Rows 118-139 move from Drafted to In review to Done.
  - tools/build_workshop_demos.py and tools/group_workshop_demos.py: a demo whose windows appear in the lesson's data/lane.json gets live_verified true, with live_verification 'Rehearsed on a live lane on DATE (kit COMMIT)'. Regenerate the lesson_map.json files under deploy/workshop_demos/module_10, module_11 and module_12; all 96 Act V demo entries read live_verified false today.
  - In the remaining eleven Act V build.py files (lessons/10-agents/10.2-langgraph, 10.3-tool-failures, 10.4-adapters, 10.5-case-desk, 10.6-desk-router; lessons/11-memory/11.1-state-history, 11.2-durable-storage, 11.3-restart-isolation; lessons/12-protocols/12.1-mcp-tools, 12.2-mcp-deploy, 12.3-a2a-peer), replace the local stubs, PLAN dicts, fake gcloud, stand-in databases and 'Rs N.NNNN' placeholders with lane(). Scripted models stay only in offline fault-injection cells, labelled with offline_window(). Precomputed panels (12.1's 126 outcomes, 12.2's 150, 12.3's) are labelled as illustrations.
  - course-manifest.json: the status field of 10.1-12.3.
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.2, 10.3, 10.4**, Every live cell's expected window: 10.2 step 5's turns, 10.3 steps 4-6, 10.4 steps 4-6: Runs the cells as today. *The learner sees:* Real Gemini answers and the chat rows' real model_calls, tokens and rupees. 10.4's steps 5 and 6 now agree because both come from one capture; today the same loop shows Rs 0.5144 in one and Rs 0.2023 in the other. Each brain shows its real thinking tokens, where today every brain prints '(thinking 0)'.
  - **10.5, 10.6**, Every window labelled stand-in today: 10 on 10.5 and 23 on 10.6, including the scripted jn-03 agent turn: Runs the cells as today. *The learner sees:* The routing decisions, desk answers, route-eval figures and rupees that a real lane printed on the capture date, in place of 'Rs N.NNNN', 'N of 7' and 'NN.N%'.
  - **11.1, 11.2, 11.3, 12.1, 12.2, 12.3**, The live steps' expected windows, which today come from a fake gcloud, a stand-in database, a Cloud Run stand-in or a scripted peer: Runs the cells as today. *The learner sees:* Captured revision names (shortened to ...), real checkpoint rows, the real lines printed by make smoke-mcp and make smoke-agent, and the peer's real task history showing the tools the real model chose.
- **Proof:** For each of the 12 lessons: data/lane.json exists with a capture date and kit commit; check_lesson's L1 label rule passes; every manifest proof's checklist row points at a captured line that its build.py asserts; tools/check_workshop_demos.py passes with the rehearsed demos at live_verified true; the PR records a second reader; course-manifest.json reads Done.
- **Risks:** A second capture is likely, because other workstreams' kit changes alter outputs after the first; a recapture per lesson is one demo run plus tools/lane_capture.py. Cost: every chat turn bills Gemini and rag-api, and 10.6's dev eval alone sends 187 routing decisions and 173 desk answers. Read the spend with make usage after each lesson and print it in that page's cost table. A rehearsal on the author's lane mixes old labels (Desk decision 28), so run the final pass on a fresh throwaway project.
- **Decision:** Two passes or one. Two passes means a pilot now on 10.1 and 11.3, whose content is stable, then each lesson's final pass after its content workstream lands. One pass means waiting until the end. Recommended: two passes, with the final pass on a fresh project. The author should also approve an INR budget for the rehearsal.
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: spend priced from chat rows (cost_usd + rag_cost_usd) plus rag-api's rows (or usage_rows.py extended to documind-chat), with an upper-bound INR estimate from shared/prices.py in the budget request; L2 split into L2a (pilot, others depend on it) and L2b (final pass, depends on them); one sub-task and PR per lesson; definition of Done adds the chapter gate runs (make smoke-chat, restart check, smoke-mcp, smoke-agent) and rewrites roadmap line 4 and 'How to follow it'; 10.4 steps 5 and 6 described as two measured turns of the same question (structure asserted, not equality). (after review: L2 issues.)
- **Also:** (final review) Sized per lesson: about 1-2 author-days for each of the 16 Act V lessons (20-30 days in all, counted as 25 in the effort sums). The pilot (L2a: 10.1 and 11.3) lands in P0; each lesson's final capture lands in its module's pass (PR93-PR95).
- **Closes:** PRES-02, L10.1-W03, L10.2-W06, L10.3-W06, L10.4-W05, L10.4-S03, L10.5-W09, L10.6-W04, L11.1-W05, L11.2-W07, L11.3-W07, L12.1-W05, L12.2-W05, L12.3-W08, PLAN-RM-Done

Departs from the gaps plan's G8 scene, which kept 'the refuse node fires on a blocked tool': that holds only with a scripted model.
- **Kit:**
