  - **10.1**, Step 7 and checklist rows 5-7: Asks the langchain brain the figure question and reads its chat row. *The learner sees:* The proof becomes 'the model chose its tools': tool_calls the model emitted, [n] citations within this turn's 1..k, and model_calls on the chat row. It no longer rests on DirectBrain's literal ['retrieve'], which appears three times in brains.py. When the agent-capability workstream lands its calculator, the proof becomes 'retrieve, then the calculator, and check_args refuses an invented number'.
  - **10.2**, Step 4 and the checklist: Runs python -m unittest commands/tests/test_chat_brains.py in ~/graph-venv (the gaps-plan scene's 'runs the new test'), then the two-turn thread on the lane. *The learner sees:* The offline suite's pass lines as the deterministic half of the proof, and turn 2 answered from turn 1 on the live thread. After the HITL workstream lands, the proof becomes 'a withdrawal pauses for an approver and resumes' (gaps decision 15).
  - **10.3**, Step 4 and the checklist: Runs make limits-drill STOP=model_calls, which is live today. *The learner sees:* HTTP 200, stopped_by model_calls on the chat row, and the restored smoke passing, as the proof. A refused argument shows in refusals. After the reliability workstream lands, the proof adds a real model correcting its call after a refused argument.
  - **10.4**, Step 5 and the checklist: Reads one question's cost lines from the lane. *The learner sees:* The proof is four cost lines for one question, taken from the lane's chat rows and rag-api rows, not '/health lists four brains' (a constant). After the evaluation workstream lands, judged trajectories sit beside the cost lines.

  - **11.1, 11.3, 12.2, 12.3**, Checklist rows: Runs the proofs as captured. *The learner sees:* 11.1: 'a follow-up answered from its thread and not from a new session' (with L6), replacing the warning line 10.2 already showed. 11.3: make restart-check (L12). 12.2 and 12.3: the gates name smoke/smoke_mcp.py and smoke/smoke_agent.py and print their checks.

Without the repeated ladder, 12.2 is thin until the protocols workstream adds its MCP host and 401 door.

### L12. Prove Module 11 on the lane: a restart-check target, two real identities, and a live stale-knowledge follow-up

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **First phase:** P3 · **After:** L1, L6 (the realistic follow-up pair), L8 (make deploy-chat), 10.5's eval accounts on the lane (desk.tf, make desk-operators), The memory workstream (GAP-G08), if 11.1 is folded

Gaps decision 5's optional paused-approval-across-redeploy step stays with the HITL workstream.

  - deploy/mk/memory.mk (new; the gaps plan assigns this file to Module 11, and the first PR to create it adds its mk/README.md row) plus commands/restart-check.sh. The script sends one turn of a session as documind-ui-sa, rolls a new chat revision with no configuration change, checks that the new revision takes 100 percent of traffic, then sends the follow-up on the same session. The eval accounts exist only after 10.5's Terraform.

## M. The Desk, course structure and kit backlog

**Goal.** Turn the two Desk lessons into agent lessons and finish the Desk build plan. Re-cut 10.5 around where each guardrail sits in an agent turn, and show live the gap an outside agent leaves, masking, and the overdue alert firing. Split 10.6 into a router lesson and a new 10.7, where a desk runs as an agent whose calculator arguments are checked, with the Google Chat door as an optional appendix. Land the manifest and lesson-count changes for 10.7, 11.4, 12.4 and 12.5 (62 to 66 lessons, Core 49 to 53, 73.5 to 79.5 hours). Finish G10-c (the known gate misses, two-turn disclosures, a gate eval), G10-h (agent mode on a page and in the smoke) and G10-j (the lane probe). Meet the Desk's definition of done: no Desk rows left in UNOWNED.md, and the flag rule decided and tested. Teach the kit pieces no lesson shows yet: clause notes, two-part answers, /v1/desk/check, the route rate limit, shadow concurrency, the bridge internals and the Desk test suites. Add an agent inventory and governance record. Where this departs from the plans: 10.6 is split agent-first rather than with Desk decision 18's document-labelling lesson; the Google Chat door becomes an appendix rather than sitting inside 10.6's levels (Desk decision 34); embeddings are priced once their price is verified (decision 27; Desk decision 11); and desk_gate's 'rules' default stays as a written, tested exception to 'every flag defaults to off'.

### M1. Decide the Desk lessons' shape and land the manifest and lesson-count changes for 10.7, 11.4, 12.4 and 12.5

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** process · **First phase:** P0
- **Reuses:** Gaps plan section 3: a new lesson lands in one pull request with its manifest entry, roadmap and plan rows, page, demo map and every stated count. Gaps plan decision 1: the titles of 11.4, 12.4 and 12.5. Desk plan section 2.4: 66 lessons, Core 53, 79.5 hours with decision 1 and the split. Desk plan section 6.4: the count list, including REVIEW.md, the roadmap's batch table and the chapter 10 end state. Desk decision 18: the split. Departs from decision 18's shape (see the decision) because a lesson on labelling stored data adds a non-agent lesson to the module learners found unimpressive.
- **Kit:**
  - tools/check_counts.py (new, authoring repo, not the learner kit): reads course-manifest.json and fails when any stated count differs (total_lessons, the Core count and the Core hours) in CLAUDE.md:8, README.md:4 and :19, plan/README.md:5, tools/README.md:19, deploy/README.md:23, deploy/workshop_demos/README.md:3, deploy/workshop_demos/REVIEW.md:7, plan/course-plan-v5-story-2026-09-22.md:4 and :84, plan/course-roadmap-v5-2026-09-22.md:1, :201 (batch E's scene count, 12 to 16) and :207, and tools/build_workshop_demos.py:357 (lessons_expected). It runs in .github/workflows/checks.yml beside kit_index.py --check

  - plan/course-plan-v5-story-2026-09-22.md: rows 10.5 and 10.6 rewritten and a 10.7 row added; the chapter 10 end state (:54) gains 'work a figure out with an agent whose every argument is checked'. plan/course-roadmap-v5-2026-09-22.md: rows 122-123 and a 10.7 row
  - Kit comments renamed where a number moves or collides: the desk_agent.py and desk_calc.py docstrings go from 'workshop lesson 10.6' to '10.7' (in M3). Old-numbering comments that already say 11.4 or 12.4 (smoke/smoke_slm.py:3, mk/ingestion.mk:20, commands/lesson-12.4.sh) are reworded to name the service, before 11.4 and 12.4 land (gaps plan section 3 rule)
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.5**, hero, h1 and footer (the manifest title): opens 10.5 *The learner sees:* a title that says what the lesson delivers, recommended 'Put a rule gate in front of the agents and hand the law's cases to a person', instead of a hand-off promise an agent never makes
  - **10.6**, hero, In this lesson box, manifest proof and footer: reads the proof before starting *The learner sees:* 'Route each question to the desk that answers it', with a proof that passes on a lane today: code-decided turns at Rs 0 and lk-06 decided by the signals on /v1/route; a cited handbook answer and a statute answer with its in-force line; zeta's desk_shadow rows. The test-split gate is named as E's ship gate, not this lesson's proof. The footer names 10.7
  - **10.7 (new)**, manifest entry and the footer chain: follows 10.6's footer *The learner sees:* 10.7 'Run a desk as an agent with checked calculator arguments', between 10.6 and 11.1. As those pages land, 11.3's footer names 11.4, 12.3's names 12.4, 12.4's names 12.5 and 12.5's names 13.1
- **Proof:** python tools/check_counts.py passes in CI after each of the four page pull requests (62 to 66, in landing order); python pagekit/check_lesson.py 10.5 10.6 10.7 passes with the new titles; tools/check_workshop_demos.py finds a lesson map for every manifest lesson
- **Risks:** Four page pull requests from four workstreams move the same counts in an unknown order; the checker turns a missed file into a red CI run instead of silent drift. Retitling 10.5 and 10.6 changes check_lesson's title match and every 'Next:' footer that names them (10.4's, 10.5's). Choosing the wrong split means reworking a 2,439-line build.py twice.
- **Decision:** (1) The 10.6 split. Recommended: agent-first. 10.6 becomes 'Route each question to the desk that answers it' (router, Desk page, shadow, dev eval, with the relabel kept as a 'Before you start' step), and a new 10.7 'Run a desk as an agent with checked calculator arguments' covers agent mode, the calculators, clause notes and the one-agent arm. The alternative is Desk decision 18 as written: 10.6 'Label documents by class on stored data', then 10.7 for the router. (2) The three titles above. (3) The Google Chat door as an optional appendix at the end of 10.7 (recommended: keeps one page per lesson), or as a separate unnumbered page, which needs check_lesson and the demo map to accept a page with no manifest entry. (4) G7's place: a new 11.4 (gap decision 1, recommended), or 11.1 re-cut for context engineering as PRES-07 suggests (65 lessons). That content call is H's; M moves the counts either way.
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: check_counts.py matches counts by pattern, not line; adds the roadmap .html and .pdf (regenerated per page PR) and REVIEW.md's page count; leaves the course plan's :4 history alone (or appends a dated sentence); the old-numbering list is built with grep (12.5: commands/lesson-12.5.sh, mk/ingestion.mk:50, lesson-12.2.sh:23, :38, run_eval.py:27, :397, build_golden.py:293; 11.4/12.4: Makefile:93, :506, :648, :722, lesson-12.2.sh:32, gchat.sh:31, lesson-12.8.sh:19, :35, brains.py:14, sa.tf:106, :119) and reworded to name the service, keeping the Makefile at 885 lines; the 10.7 manifest proof is written: 'an invented cap is refused by check_args, then a Worked out in code line cites LV-07; make smoke-desk's agent checks pass'. (after review: M1 issues.)
- **Also:** (final review) Roadmap anchors: batch E's row is at line 203 and the count sentence ('Sixty-two scenes') at line 207; check_counts.py matches by pattern. M1's code (check_counts.py, its CI line, the reworded comments, then tools/kit_index.py) lands in PR01b, not in the decisions-only PR01.

Then runs offline test_desk.py's test_the_shadow_decides_beside_chat_and_writes_one_row, the busy and late skip tests, and test_the_route_rate_limit *The learner sees:* Why a shadow decision never delays a turn: busy and late skips, each with a desk_shadow_skipped line. And that ROUTE_RATE=120 holds per worker process: with gunicorn -w 2 (services/chat/Dockerfile:17) an account gets up to 240 calls a minute per instance. Desk rows carry index_version; test_desk.py and test_desk_operations.py pass with the new row field
- **Risks:** desk_router.py, desk.py and frontend/desk.py are quoted by 10.5 and 10.6, so check_lesson decides which pages rebuild; new code goes in new functions. The embedding price must be verified on the day. The rollback drill leaves acme off until the learner switches it back, which 10.7 needs. E's calibration cells and G's prompt excerpts also land on 10.6 and must fit the same page budget.
- **Decision:** Price the query embedding now (recommended), or keep Desk decision 11's line that reports calls only.
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: load_index point-reads a small version document each turn and reloads on change (one poll suffices), or route-index requires several consecutive matches spread over INDEX_TTL_S; WAIT=1 (not --wait); polls use a question that reaches the vote, cost stated. --sample scores only sampled rows, prints 'SAMPLE n of N: not a gate', exits 0 on gate misses; rupee share read from the run's cost line. frontend/desk.py _ask() keeps the caption fields (method, sections[].mode, tool_calls, refusals, model_calls, limits); refusals read from sections. Release patterns named correctly: shadow = dark launch; flag = kill switch; rollback paths (route-index from the previous commit's rows; redeploy the previous image); a prompt version added to desk_agent.system_prompt and agent rows if T28 stays claimed. A build-enforced budget (10,000 prose words) agreed with E and G up front; M4's level moves to 10.7. (after review: M2 issues.)
- **Also:** (final review) The settings cache constant is SETTINGS_TTL_S (services/chat/desk.py:138, 60 s); there is no SETTINGS_S.
- **Also:** (final review, for L10.6-W14) Take the first option only and drop 'or route-index requires several consecutive matches spread over INDEX_TTL_S'. route-index also merge-writes index_version into tenant_settings/{tenant}. _route_ctx passes the version from the settings doc it already holds (desk.settings(), 60 s cache) to load_index, which reloads a tenant's exemplars when it differs from the cached rows' index_version. Every process then picks up a new index within 60 s with no extra read. make route-index WAIT=1 returns once 60 s have passed since the write and a /v1/route decision names the new version. test_desk.py gains a case that a changed version reloads at once. The page states the measured wait.
- **Also:** (final review, for KIT-09) 10.6 Level 5 'Ship a router change': add one Rs 0 stdlib cell. Run it while acme's desk_route is off and again after it is back on, waiting 60 s after each make desk. The cell calls GET /v1/desk/check as evalacme and as evalgrc (etok). While off: evalacme returns 200 {email, tenant acme, via direct}, because eval accounts are served in any mode, and evalgrc returns 403 'the routed Desk is not switched on for your company'. While on: both return 200. The build asserts the 403 text against desk.py's literal. Remove 'the cells below do not call it' from the page.
- **Also:** (final review, for BENCH-T28_versioning_release) After the kill-switch flip, Level 5 'Ship a router change' adds a canary and a rollback: (1) Build a router prompt change with PROMPT_VERSION bumped (edit one PROMPT_EXEMPLARS line in the local copy; make build SERVICES=chat). Deploy it as a tagged revision with no traffic: gcloud run deploy documind-chat --image=<that image> --no-traffic --tag=canary (the tag pattern F10's attack-tags uses). (2) Gate it: run make route-eval SPLIT=dev SAMPLE=40 against the tag's URL and against the live URL, side by side. (3) Canary: gcloud run services update-traffic documind-chat --to-tags=canary=10. Send ten Desk questions, then read the desk rows grouped by resource.labels.revision_name with their prompt_version. (4) Roll back: update-traffic --to-revisions=<previous revision>=100, remove the tag and run git checkout. Then roll the exemplar index back with make route-index from the previous commit's rows (WAIT=1) and read index_version return to its old value on the next row. The page names where each versioned part is pinned or registered: image digests in Artifact Registry; PROMPT_VERSION, RULES_VERSION and index_version on rows; model ids in code; F9's tool pins; J6's card version. Proof adds: rows from two revisions with two prompt_version values during the split, and only the previous revision and index_version after the rollback.
- **Closes:** PRES-04, PRES-05, PRES-06, PRES-10, L10.5-S09, L10.6-W02, L10.6-W08, L10.6-W14, L10.6-S09, PLAN-CP10.6, KIT-09, BENCH-T28_versioning_release

- **Kit:**
  - smoke/smoke_desk.py: two agent-mode checks, each sent as documind-evalacme-sa with arm 'Astar' so agent mode always runs. Check 8: jn-03 returns a section in mode agent whose calculations include encashable_days with result 45, the balance found in 'your message' and the cap in 'LV-07 [n]'. Check 9: a gratuity question with a stated wage and length of service returns a gratuity_estimate calculation labelled an estimate and citing the Code on Social Security. The smoke's docstring check list grows to match (it has no agent-mode check today)
  - evals/routes.jsonl and evals/build_routes.py: one person-reviewed dev row for the gratuity question the smoke and the page use, agreed with E's G10-a2; test_desk_rules.py's scan keeps it at 0 gate fires
  - services/chat/desk_agent.py and shared/desk_calc.py docstrings: 'workshop lesson 10.6' becomes '10.7'. route_eval.py's --arm paragraph and mk/agents.mk's route-eval comment name 10.7 for the arms
  - commands/tests/test_desk.py: one test, quoted by the page, that the composed section names each argument's source in its 'Worked out in code' line for jn-03 on a scripted model
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.7**, Level 0, theory and an interactive: picks a calculator, a question and a handbook passage from the corpus in an 'Argument check' panel. The panel is built on desk_calc.CALCULATORS and check_args, as a JS port that node checks against the Python over a grid, as with 10.6's panel B *The learner sees:* Each argument accepted with its source (your message, LV-07 [1]), or refused with check_args' own sentence, for example 'cap = 60 is not written in LV-07 of a handbook passage this turn read'. The Desk drawn as a workflow that runs an agent for one step, only when a figure is asked. The analogy: a bank teller who may key in only the figures on the customer's slip or the bank's printed rate card
  - **10.7**, Level 1, the Desk page: runs make desk TENANT=acme NOTES=evals/desk/clause_notes.acme.json, then asks jn-03 and the gratuity question on the Desk page. Change it: edits the LV-07 note in a copy of the file, runs make desk NOTES= again, and asks jn-03 again *The learner sees:* 'Worked out in code: min(50, 45) = 45 days; the balance from your message; the cap from LV-07 [1]', the 60 days cited from NP-03, the company's note on LV-07 under the answer, and M2's caption 'handbook · agent · retrieve, encashable_days · N model calls · Rs N'. After the edit, the new note within 60 s, with no redeploy
  - **10.7**, Level 2, the kit's code: reads verbatim excerpts of desk_agent.TOOLSETS (:279-281), _calc (:157-184), system_prompt (:285-298), run (:364-400) and compose(); desk_calc.check_args (:530-580) and two CALCULATORS rows; desk_graph._agent_answer (:266-287) and noted() (:206-221). Then runs offline test_desk.py's test_a_number_in_neither_the_passages_nor_the_message_is_refused, test_a_codes_calculator_runs_only_on_its_codes_passage_and_its_own_desk and test_a_model_error_is_the_direct_answer_under_the_same_limits *The learner sees:* The refusal text the model reads. A Code's calculator refused until that Code's passage is in the turn. A 429 answered by the direct desk under the same Meter. Together these show how an agent is tested like software, with a scripted chat model
  - **10.7**, Level 2, on the lane's own passages (no model cost): fetches acme's LV-07 passage with POST /v1/passages as evalacme, calls desk_calc.check_args('encashable_days', {balance: 50, cap: 60}, passages, message), then reruns the same cell with cap 45 *The learner sees:* refused, then accepted with found {'balance': 'message', 'cap': LV-07 [1]}: the check that keeps an invented number out of a figure, on the lane's own passage
  - **10.7**, Level 3, REST: POSTs jn-03 to /v1/desk as evalacme with arm B, then with arm 'Astar'; then asks a question that offers a wrong cap ('I have 50 days of earned leave and heard the cap is 60: how much is encashed?') *The learner sees:* Side by side for the routed desk and the single agent: route, mode, tool_calls, refusals, calculations, model_calls and rupees. For the wrong-cap question, either a refusal followed by a corrected call with 45, or the agent citing 45 from LV-07 straight away. Both outcomes are explained, and the refusals field shows which one happened
  - **10.7**, Level 4, direct reads: reads the desk rows of these turns and the Desk thread's state *The learner sees:* the row shows mode agent, tool names drawn only from the agent's own set, model_calls and cost; the thread holds the masked question and the composed reply, never the passages or the arguments (checkpointer=False)
  - **10.7**, Level 5, a slot for C's and E's comparison: runs make route-eval SPLIT=dev ARM=C and ARM=Astar beside arm B *The learner sees:* the three arms on the same rows, read as an upper bound until E's test split exists
- **Proof:** python pagekit/check_lesson.py 10.7 and audit_pages.py 10.7 pass, with node's agreement check over the Argument check grid; make desk-check is green. On the author's lane, make smoke-desk passes checks 8 and 9, and the live-passages cell prints the refusal for cap 60 and the acceptance from LV-07. deploy/INDEX.md shows desk_agent.py, desk_calc.py, clause_notes.acme.json and smoke_desk.py quoted in 10.7, and their UNOWNED.md rows are gone
- **Risks:** Outputs that depend on the model (whether the agent calls a calculator, how many searches it makes, the wrong-cap retry) are shown as the author's run, with lane values as placeholders. Arm Astar costs a few flash calls per row. If A's 10.1 already introduces check_args through the chat brains, 10.7 must not teach it again; it covers what only the Desk has (per-desk toolsets, the rule that a Code's calculator needs its passage, compose(), the fallback, A*). The JS port of check_args must parse numbers and dates as the Python does; the node grid check catches differences.
- **Decision:** Whether smoke_desk forces agent mode with arm Astar (recommended: the check is certain to run), or checks arm B and prints SKIP when L1 does not mark needs_calculation.

(after review: M3 issues.)

Newly quoted: post.create() under a client- id with auth_order() (posting with no key); events.normalise() (both event shapes); replies (fixed replies equal to the Desk's denials); the /work worker (lease, MAX_ATTEMPTS=5, the fifth attempt's card); GET /v1/desk/check before a question is queued; and the documind-gchat-work topic stored in India. Only confirmed facts are stated, and the unsettled ones are listed once. Every learner sees the four refusals
- **Proof:** The probe's facts are in G10-j's pull request; make smoke-gchat passes; Desk plan section 4.11b's demo runs end to end once on the author's Workspace; make desk TENANT=acme DESK_GCHAT=off closes the door within 60 s; check_lesson 10.6 and 10.7 pass; the UNOWNED.md rows for services/gchat/*, terraform/gchat.tf, smoke_gchat.py and test_gchat.py are removed
- **Risks:** Without a Workspace account the probe cannot run, and the appendix keeps its list of unconfirmed facts. If Chat events carry no sender email, Desk decision 33's Directory lookup adds about two days. The smoke's refused delegations fire the alert by design. An appendix after 'What changed' is a new page shape that check_lesson must accept.
- **Decision:** Settle the Workspace question first; if no Business/Enterprise Workspace, mark L10.6-W09, L10.6-S11 and PLAN-G10-j partial and defer the probe and kit edits. The appendix shape goes through M1's decision (3) with check_lesson support; the appendix quotes a pin line from services/gchat Dockerfile and requirements.txt to clear its UNOWNED row.
- **Revised:** decision_needed revised after review (M5 issue.)
- **Also:** (final review, for L10.6-W09) Settle Decision 30 in PR01 instead of leaving it open: the author provisions a Business or Enterprise Workspace for the one probe session before PR88 (a time-limited trial is enough if Google offers one). Proof adds: every UNCONFIRMED marker in services/gchat/*.py and every 'from memory' field in terraform/gchat.tf is settled or deleted, and a grep finds none. The appendix shows the probe run as a labelled capture of the door accepting a call: the ack, the queued /work attempt and the posted card, with emails and ids as placeholders. Learners without a Workspace then see the door work, not only the four refusals.
- **Also:** (final review, for KIT-10) In 10.7's appendix, add to the 'Newly quoted' list, through block(): - post.publish() from services/gchat/post.py:135-152: Pub/Sub topics.publish over REST, the payload as one base64 message on documind-gchat-work, and a PostError that releases the claim; - the sync-only fallback in services/gchat/main.py: GCHAT_ASYNC (:96); queue_turn's 'if not GCHAT_ASYNC' branch (:328-338), which calls POST /v1/desk within SYNC_ONLY_BUDGET_S and answers in the HTTP response with no /v1/desk/check; and /health's async field (:639). One sentence says when the fallback is set: when auth_order() finds no credential that may post, as the G10-j probe records. After make deploy-gchat, the learner reads the bridge's /health and sees which mode their bridge runs. Proof adds: the build asserts both excerpts, and deploy/UNOWNED.md has no services/gchat rows.

### M6. Re-cut 10.5 around the agent: where each guardrail sits, what an outside agent already read, masking seen live, the overdue alert firing, a typed gate widget, and a lighter page

- **Effort:** L · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **First phase:** P5 · **After:** M1, M7, M9, F (desk_recall run once on the page, soft), D (the agent-initiated hand-off level, soft: its own content pull request), L (the shared setup collapsed)
- **Reuses:** Desk plan section 6.1's Level 0 promise ('The learner types a question and sees the class that fires'); section 4.11, lane proof step 5 (an SLA of 0 days fires the alert); 10.6's build pattern (a JS panel checked in node); gaps plan G5 (the outside agent's gap is the motive for 12.5).
- **Kit:**
  - services/chat/desk.py (ChatDoor) and services/rag-api/desk_door.py: one {'event': 'desk_mask', 'surface', 'tenant', 'kinds'} line when a door masks, with no text and no digits. It is not added to terraform/sink.tf's event list, so 13.2 and 16.2 are untouched
  - smoke/smoke_cases.py: a twelfth check. A question carrying a published Luhn-valid test card number (4111 1111 1111 1111) goes to /v1/query as ui-sa; the smoke then reads the latest desk_mask line with gcloud logging, as smoke_gchat.py reads its rows, and expects kinds ['card'] and no run of digits in the row. test_desk_rules.py's '0 masks on smoke questions' rule gets this one named exception, as it has for the POSH disclosure
  - commands/tests/test_cases.py: a test that with desk_gate off a scripted chat model behind the chat door receives the person's words, and with rules it is never called. This is the counterfactual the page runs offline
  - evals/desk/queues.lab.json (new, lab only): acme's queues with the grc SLA at 0 days, so make cases-overdue marks a fresh grievance breached and the case_overdue alert fires (Desk plan section 4.11, lane proof step 5)
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.5**, Level 0: types any sentence into 'Ask the gate'. The widget is a JS port of normalise() and gate(), exported from shared/desk_rules.py at build time; node checks it against the Python on every question test_desk_rules.py holds *The learner sees:* the class, or None, for their own words, with the checks ticked. Also a diagram of one agent turn built from the kit, naming each guard and what it can see: the chat door before any brain (desk.py), rag-api's door before retrieval (desk_door.py), the brains' guard before a tool (10.3's GuardMiddleware, 10.4's before_tool_callback), the model check behind the rules (desk_recall.py), and html.escape on the way out (frontend/chat.py:162-165)
  - **10.5**, step 3 and the page's weight: sets up the Desk on their lane *The learner sees:* A two-line check first: make desk, and make plan showing nothing to add on a lane built from this kit. The Terraform run and the three deploys apply only to a lane that predates the Desk. The statute and Local Committee detail sits in collapsible 'The law behind it' notes, and the desk_gate fail-safe is explained once, in Level 0
  - **10.5**, Level 1, the Chat page beside the Desk page: on the Chat page, with the langchain brain, asks lk-06 and then the disclosure *The learner sees:* the caption 'langchain · retrieve · N model calls · Rs N' for the question and 'desk_gate · model none · Rs 0' for the disclosure: the agent ran for one and never saw the other
  - **10.5**, Level 2, 'Two doors, and the agent you do not run': runs the offline counterfactual (with the door off, the scripted model's input holds the words; with rules, the model is never called). Then sends the disclosure with message/send to the A2A peer as documind-ui-sa, prefixed with the tenant, and reads rag-api's desk_gate rows for that minute *The learner sees:* the peer's own model read the words first; rag-api's door saw only the search words the peer wrote, and fired or not depending on them (both outcomes explained). This is the outside-agent gap the gaps box names, proved live for a rupee or two
  - **10.5**, Level 2, masking live: sends a question with a test card number to /v1/chat as evalacme, then reads the desk_mask line *The learner sees:* kinds ['card'], no digits, and the brain's answer to the masked question
  - **10.5**, Level 3: runs test_cases.py's audit lease tests, with a fake bucket that fails once *The learner sees:* the case.open event held in audit_pending, then flushed by the next call: the transactional outbox and its two-minute lease, failing and recovering
  - **10.5**, Level 4: loads queues.lab.json for acme, raises a grievance, runs make cases-overdue, then loads the real queues again *The learner sees:* a breached line with queue 'sensitive', and the case_overdue alert's incident in Cloud Monitoring, sent to the lane's own channel. A new 'separation of duties' checklist row sums up step 7's 403s: the committee cannot raise a case, and the raiser cannot move one
  - **10.5**, Level 5: reads the Advanced level *The learner sees:* M9's governance record; the gaps box keeps 'No delivery to the queue', with the Phase E reason
- **Proof:** check_lesson.py 10.5 and audit_pages.py 10.5 pass. The build's node check agrees with gate() on every question test_desk_rules.py holds, and the build counts at most 9,000 prose words. On the author's lane, make smoke-cases passes 12 checks; the peer cell's output and rag-api's desk_gate rows are captured once; the overdue alert fires once
- **Risks:** The peer counterfactual depends on the model, so both outcomes are written up. JS and Python regexes differ on \b and Unicode word boundaries (Devanagari); the node check over every test question catches that. Collapsible notes need the template's auto-height handshake to re-post the height on toggle (pagekit/template_tail.html). The alert emails the lane's channel. queues.lab.json must be undone before 10.6, which refuses incomplete POSH queues while routing is on.
- **Decision:** (1) The 10.5 title (see M1). (2) Collapsible notes in the page template, or plain note boxes. (3) Delivery of a case to the queue's own people: leave it to Phase E as Desk plan section 1.2 does (recommended), or build it now behind a default-off switch with an opaque queue label, so no log line ever names a POSH queue.
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: overdue drill uses grc sla_days 1 (a fresh grievance is due within 24 h, unacknowledged), run via gcloud run jobs execute documind-cases-overdue after make desk-job, then the case is closed and real queues reloaded; 'breached' would need a day. Declare the departure from Desk decision 12 (desk_mask not in sink.tf) or add the event and rebuild 13.2/16.2. The masking and known-miss cells import their strings from smoke_cases and a KNOWN_MISSES table, with test_desk_rules.py:482's exception widened exactly. Split into a kit PR (desk_mask, smoke check 12 and its exception, the counterfactual test, lab queues) and a page PR; M9's kit lands first and only its page level stays in 10.5 or moves; Level 1 quotes the real caption (chat.py:171-175). L10 folds into M6. (after review: M6 issues (verified cases.py:127-129, desk_alerts.tf:205-216).)
- **Also:** (final review) The overdue drill backdates a lab tenant's case (opened_at in the past) and runs the documind-cases-overdue job with gcloud run jobs execute, so the case_overdue line reaches Cloud Logging and the alert can fire. queues.lab.json keeps sla_days within 1-365 (shared/cases.py:127-129). This matches E5.
- **Closes:** PRES-05, PRES-06, L10.5-W01, L10.5-W06, L10.5-W08, L10.5-W11, L10.5-S02, L10.5-S04, L10.5-S05, L10.5-S06, L10.5-S07, KIT-05, BENCH-T16_guardrails_policy

### M7. Finish G10-c: fix the gate's known misses, keep a two-turn disclosure, and measure the gate's recall and precision

- **Effort:** L · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **First phase:** P5 · **After:** E (PLAN-G10-a2's people-written escalation and first-person rows: hard for the recall gate, not for the known-miss fixes), M8 (the flag rule)
- **Reuses:** Desk plan section 4.3, G10-c (0 fires on the 424 questions; every dev escalation row fires; RULES_VERSION on every row); risk 1 (two-turn disclosures); section 5.2 (at least a third of escalation rows in Hindi or Hinglish, by a fluent writer).
- **Kit:**
  - shared/desk_rules.py: patterns for the misses that reproduce offline on today's kit, where gate() returns None for 'He grabbed my hand in the lift yesterday.', 'A senior colleague keeps asking me out even after I refused.', 'Can I speak to someone in HR?' and desk_recall's own positive examples 'My team lead keeps messaging me about how I look...', 'Please delete all the personal data you hold about me' and 'Can I talk to someone from HR directly?'. Also a curated misspelling map in normalise() ('maneger', 'coments', SMS spellings), and RULES_VERSION bumped from 2026-10-01.4
  - commands/tests/test_desk_rules.py: a KNOWN_MISSES table in which each row must fire its class, each paired with a near-miss twin that must not fire; the rule of 0 fires on the 424 existing questions stays
  - evals/gate_eval.py (new, stdlib) and make gate-eval: runs gate() over routes.jsonl's escalation and first-person rows and reports recall and precision per class and language, with Wilson 95% intervals (route_eval.py's helper), at Rs 0. Tests in evals/tests/test_gate_eval.py
  - shared/desk_sticky.py (new): after a posh, grievance or privacy_request hit, the chat door and /v1/desk write a marker tenants/{t}/desk_sensitive/{sha256(user:session)} with expire_at and no text or class. The next first-person turn of that session with no hit gets the same fixed reply and case offer, model none. It sits behind tenant_settings desk_sticky_minutes, default 0 (off), set with make desk DESK_STICKY=30 (commands/desk_ops.py); a TTL field in terraform/desk.tf; tests in test_cases.py
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.5**, Level 2, 'Do it: the gate on your machine': runs the same cell over the known misses under the old and the new rules version. Change it: types a miss of their own into the widget, adds one pattern in a copy of desk_rules.py, bumps RULES_VERSION, and reruns the same cell and python -m unittest commands/tests/test_desk_rules.py *The learner sees:* None, then the class; the test suite still holds 0 fires on the course's 424 questions
  - **10.5**, Level 4, two turns: runs make desk TENANT=acme DESK_STICKY=30, sends the disclosure, then 'He did it again today. What should I do?' in the same session *The learner sees:* the second turn answered with the posh template, model none, Rs 0; with the switch at 0 it reaches a brain with no context, as today
  - **10.5**, Level 5 Advanced: runs make gate-eval *The learner sees:* recall and precision per class and language, with intervals; until G10-a2's people-written rows land, the escalation denominator is 0 and the page says so instead of printing a rate
- **Proof:** test_desk_rules.py passes with every known miss firing and 0 fires on the 424 questions. make gate-eval on G10-a2's rows shows every dev escalation row of each class firing (the Desk plan's G10-c test). On the author's lane the two-turn cell returns model none on turn 2. With desk_sticky_minutes at 0, the door's bytes are unchanged (M8's test)
- **Risks:** New patterns can fire on informational questions; the near-miss twins and the 424-question test guard precision. The misspelling map starts in English; Hindi and Hinglish variants need the fluent writer. The sticky marker ties a session to a sensitive hit for 30 minutes (hashed, no text), so it needs the author's privacy review.
- **Decision:** Build the sticky sensitive session, default off, or rely on the Desk plan's three paths for two-turn disclosures: the rules, the model check and the always-visible button.
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: M7 ships its own byte test for desk_sticky_minutes 0 (M8 consolidates later); learners edit deploy/shared/desk_rules.py in place and restore with git; desk_sticky spelled out: the door changes, a lane-wide OnTenants set, an expire_at comparison (TTL deletes late), session 'default' handling, the uncovered rag-api /v1/stream door, and a privacy review; gate_eval labelled a scaffold until G10-a2. (after review: M7 issues.)
- **Revised:** effort revised to L after review (Scope.)
- **Also:** (final review) The flag rule itself is decided in decision 9; M7 implements and tests it.
- **Also:** (final review, for L10.5-W05) Settle the decision now, overriding decision 27's 'a sticky session only if people's two-turn rows show misses': build shared/desk_sticky.py now (desk_sticky_minutes default 0; make desk DESK_STICKY=30). The miss already reproduces offline on today's kit: after a posh hit, gate('He did it again today. What should I do?') returns None. 10.5's Level 4 two-turn cell ships in PR71/PR77 without waiting for G10-a2's rows. Once those rows land, gate_recall (E5) reports the two-turn rows at desk_sticky_minutes 0 and at 30. Proof adds: on the author's lane, turn 2 returns the posh template with model none and Rs 0 at 30, and reaches a brain at 0. test_cases.py covers the marker's expiry and asserts that it stores no text or class.
- **Closes:** L10.5-W04, L10.5-W05, L10.5-W07, L10.5-S03, PLAN-G10-c

### M8. Meet the Desk's definition of done: UNOWNED.md cleared, the flag rule decided and tested, the Desk's test suites taught

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** process · **First phase:** P6 · **After:** M2, M3, M4, M5, M6, M7, M9, M10, E (route_eval, route_probe and route_threshold quoted), F (desk_recall quoted)
- **Reuses:** Desk plan section 1.3 (definition of done, items 1 and 5); section 4.0 (kit pull requests add UNOWNED.md rows, and the page pull requests that adopt the files remove them); section 1.1 (flags read through the 60-second cache, and a failed read counts as off).
- **Kit:**
  - deploy/UNOWNED.md: each of the 27 Desk rows is removed by the pull request that quotes its file. commands/desk_ops.py: M2 (the routing() print) and M7 (DESK_STICKY). shared/desk_recall.py: F. test_doc_types.py, a routes.jsonl row, test_route_eval.py's Wilson test and mk/agents.mk's route-eval target: M2. build_routes.py, route_probe.py and route_threshold.py: E. shared/identifiers.py, the queues.*.json files, smoke_cases.py and test_cases.py: M6. test_desk_rules.py: M7. clause_notes.acme.json, smoke_desk.py, desk_calc.py, desk_agent.py and test_desk.py: M3. desk_alerts.tf: M6 (case_overdue) and E (the router policies). desk_daily.sql and test_desk_operations.py: M10. services/gchat/*, gchat.tf, smoke_gchat.py and test_gchat.py: M5. The section heading is removed once the section is empty
  - tools/check_unowned.py (new, authoring repo, run in CI after kit_index.py --check): fails ('adopted: remove the row') when a file listed in UNOWNED.md is shown as quoted in deploy/INDEX.md. Also fails when a Desk file (services/chat/desk*.py, shared/desk*.py, services/gchat/*, evals/route*.py, smoke/smoke_desk.py, smoke_cases.py, smoke_gchat.py, terraform/desk*.tf, gchat.tf, terraform/sql/desk_daily.sql) is quoted nowhere and not listed
  - commands/tests/test_desk_flags.py (new; CI's chat-pins step and make desk-check): with desk_gate off, desk_route off, desk_gchat off, and neither desk_max_parts nor desk_sticky_minutes set, both doors replay a gate hit and an Aadhaar question byte for byte, POST /v1/desk is 403 for a person, the shadow decides nothing, and the bridge's /v1/desk/check refuses. With no desk_gate field, only gate hits and checked numbers differ
  - commands/desk-check.sh: test_desk_operations.py and test_desk_flags.py join the run, so make desk-check runs every Desk test
  - plan/documind-desk-build-plan-2026-09-30.md, section 1.1 and section 1.3 item 1: the desk_gate exception written down with its reason (a legal-safety gate is never off by accident), if the author keeps it
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.6**, Level 3, 'The Desk's offline check': runs make desk-check, which now names each suite *The learner sees:* the stdlib and chat-pins halves of test_desk.py, test_desk_operations.py, test_route_eval.py and test_desk_flags.py, with one test from each quoted where its behaviour is taught (10.5, 10.6, 10.7)
- **Proof:** tools/check_unowned.py passes with no 'DocuMind Desk' section left in UNOWNED.md; python tools/kit_index.py --check passes; test_desk_flags.py passes in CI's chat-pins step; items 1 and 5 of the Desk plan's definition of done hold
- **Risks:** Data and test files read awkwardly on a page; quoting one row or one test is enough to adopt each. Flipping desk_gate's default would change every tenant's behaviour and force rebuilds of 10.5 and 10.6. If F or E do not quote their files, M8 picks them up in the Advanced levels of 10.5 and 10.6.
- **Decision:** desk_gate's default. Keep 'rules' as a written, tested exception to 'every flag defaults to off' (recommended: the gate already ships that way, and a POSH disclosure must not reach a model because an operator forgot a flag). Or flip gate_state() to default to 'off' (shared/desk_rules.py:647-653) and have 10.5 write rules for acme, zeta and globex.
- **Revised:** kit_changes: Amend: desk-check.sh adds test_desk_rules.py and test_doc_types.py (with test_desk_operations.py and test_desk_flags.py), keeping the 'commands/tests/test_cases.py commands/tests/test_desk.py' substring test_desk.py asserts; check_unowned's Desk patterns widened to everything UNOWNED.md's Desk section lists (routes.jsonl, build_routes.py, evals/desk/*.json, desk_ops.py, commands/desk-check.sh, the Desk tests, test_desk_flags.py); desk-check.sh adopted or listed. (after review: M8 issue (verified desk-check.sh).)
- **Closes:** PLAN-D-unowned, PLAN-D-flags, KIT-11, BENCH-T21_testing_agents

### M9. An agent inventory and governance record: every agent on the lane with its identity, model and location, tools and side effects, caps, audit trail and residency

- **Effort:** M · **Kind:** kit+lesson · **First phase:** P5 · **After:** M6 (the page that shows it), J (the MCP audit bucket row and the 12.1 excerpts, soft), D (approval audit events, soft), H (retention and erasure, soft)
- **Reuses:** Nothing from the gaps plan: G1-G9 have no governance item. Desk plan section 1.1 (globex is a data_region 'in' tenant that makes no router model call), decision 29's --confirm-residency rule, and shared/audit_log.AUDIT_ACTIONS.
- **Kit:**
  - commands/agent_inventory.py (new, stdlib) reads the kit with ast: services/chat/brains.py (BRAINS), tools.py (TOOLS, BLOCKED), desk_agent.py (TOOLSETS), desk_router.py (the L1 and L2 models), limits.py (the per-turn defaults), services/mcp/server.py (the @mcp.tool names and _audit), services/agent/agent.py (the peer's toolset: no tool_filter, no cap) and services/gchat/. With PROJECT given it also reads the lane: each Cloud Run service's account and ingress (gcloud run services describe), and each tenant's desk_gate, desk_route, desk_gchat and data_region. It prints one row per agent surface, a side-effect class per tool (read, draft, write) and the audit destination (log line or audit bucket). A residency line flags any surface that calls Gemini on global for a tenant whose data_region is in; globex in single mode makes no router call
  - mk/agents.mk: make agent-inventory [PROJECT=] [TENANT=], added to .PHONY and mk/README.md
  - commands/tests/test_agent_inventory.py (stdlib, in CI): every tool name the kit declares appears with a side-effect class, so a new tool cannot ship unlisted; every surface has an identity and an audit entry
- **Lessons:**
  - **10.5**, Level 5, new 'The governance record': runs make agent-inventory offline, then make agent-inventory PROJECT=documind-ai-YOUR-ID TENANT=globex. Reads services/mcp/server.py's _audit (:121-133) quoted beside the Desk's case events *The learner sees:* The four brains, the router, the handbook, statute and A* desk agents, the MCP server, the A2A peer and the Google Chat bridge, each with its account, model@location, tools by side effect, caps (the peer has none) and audit trail. The audit trails: case.* and role.* events with case-reference actors; desk_gate rows with user null; MCP's mcp_call line, with no bucket row while AUDIT_BUCKET is unset on documind-mcp; nothing for the peer. globex's residency line names the surfaces that would send its words to global. Pointers lead to H's retention and erasure and to D's approval events
- **Proof:** On the author's lane, make agent-inventory lists every surface with an identity; test_agent_inventory.py passes, and fails on a scratch copy with an unlisted @tool; 10.5's Level 5 shows the output (M6); INDEX.md shows services/mcp/server.py's _audit quoted in 10.5
- **Risks:** The ast reader breaks when a file's shape changes; the test catches it in CI. Residency statements must match the kit: Gemini 3.x generation is served only from global (CLAUDE.md). gcloud describe needs viewer roles, which the operator already holds.
- **Decision:** Host the record in 10.5's Level 5 (recommended: beside the gate, the cases and the audit events), or hold it for 14.4's operational handover.
- **Revised:** lesson_changes: Host the full record at the end of Module 12 (12.3 or 12.5) or 14.4; 10.5 keeps only the Desk and chat surfaces. Report every location each surface calls (router embedding on us-central1, rag-api generation on global). 'stdlib' stated for offline only. List the existing Desk alerts (desk_delegation_refused, desk_check_tenants_unread, case_overdue) or drop the threat-alert claim. (after review: M9 issue.)
- **Closes:** KIT-12, BENCH-T30_governance_audit_compliance
